**Row-level mutation guard (DB, confirmed via migration + trigger source)**: an `AS RESTRICTIVE` RLS policy on `business_settings` INSERT (`20260830000002_capture_base_rls_grants.sql`) permits a normal authenticated user's own signup row to be created **only** as `plan='free' AND trial_ends_at IS NULL` **or** `plan='pro' AND trial_ends_at` within ±2h of "now + 14 days" — `'basic'` can never be created at signup, and no custom trial window is possible via a normal signup INSERT. Separately, a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger `guard_business_settings_plan_trial()` blocks any change to `plan`/`trial_ends_at` unless the **calling user's own** `business_settings.role = 'super_admin'`, or the new values are exactly the self-cancel pair (`plan='free'`, `trial_ends_at IS NULL`). This trigger fires regardless of RLS bypass — a service-role/admin-API request carries no `auth.uid()`, so `caller_role` resolves to `NULL` and is **also** blocked unless it matches the self-cancel exception. **Conclusion: any TEST persona needing `plan='basic'`, a custom/past `trial_ends_at`, or `plan='pro'` with a non-fresh trial window can only be set via an UPDATE performed as an authenticated `super_admin`-role caller** (a real TEST super-admin session), or via a direct Postgres-superuser session with `session_replication_role` temporarily set to bypass triggers for that single transaction — both legitimate, precedented techniques, neither executed by this audit.

**Feature entitlements — confirmed real, frontend-enforced gates** (`Dashboard.jsx`): `handleProtectedAction` blocks Edit/Duplicate unless `isBasicOrAbove` (`isPro || effectivePlan==='basic'`), and blocks WhatsApp-send/Delete unless `isPro` (`isSuperAdmin || effectivePlan==='pro'`) — FREE-with-no-active-trial users are shown the Pricing modal instead. A monthly quote-creation limit is also enforced client-side: FREE=5, BASIC=20, PRO=∞ (`planLimit`, checked both for the header count display and at creation time). **These gates are frontend-only** — no RLS policy or Edge Function found anywhere that independently re-enforces plan-based feature limits (a technically sophisticated user calling Supabase directly could bypass them). Not a defect to fix in this task — disclosed as a real, current characteristic. Active-trial users receive the **full PRO feature set** (since `effectivePlan` resolves to `'pro'` during an active trial) — consistent with the Dashboard's own "Trial active (Full PRO access)" messaging.

**Market/plan isolation confirmed**: `business_settings.country` (drives Local/Hebrew/RTL/ILS vs. International/English/LTR/USD-EUR-GBP) is a fully separate column from `plan`/`trial_ends_at`; neither the RLS INSERT policy nor the UPDATE trigger references `country` in any way. Plan/trial state cannot alter market identity, and vice versa — confirmed by direct inspection, not assumption.

**Recommended persona model**: a **hybrid** of Option A (separate accounts) and reuse of what already exists — not pure Option B (state-switching one account), because switching a single account's plan/trial state back and forth for different regression runs is exactly the kind of operation that produced this project's own previously-disclosed Trial-reset regression risk, and because (per the RLS/trigger findings above) reaching 6 of the 8 target states already requires a privileged UPDATE regardless of whether 1 or 6 separate accounts are used — sharing one account buys no safety and adds real state-confusion/re-mutation risk. Recommendation:
- **Reuse, unmodified**: the two existing TEST accounts (`PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_*` / `PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_*`) already are, by construction, fresh real signups with `plan:'pro'` + an active 14-day trial — they already **are** the "FREE + Active Trial" persona for each market today, with zero mutation needed.
- **Create, separately, once authorized**: six new dedicated TEST accounts (3 additional states × 2 markets) — each created via a genuine signup (through the app's own real form, same technique already used for every existing TEST account this session — no raw SQL insert), then, for the 6 that need a non-default state, a single subsequent super_admin-authenticated UPDATE to set the final `plan`/`trial_ends_at` combination once, never toggled again.

**LOCAL personas** (recommended, not yet created): (1) FREE+Active Trial — reuse existing `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_*`, no change. (2) FREE+Trial Ended/No Renewal — new account, signup then self-cancel via the app's own real Cancel-Subscription flow (`plan:'free'`, `trial_ends_at:null` — the only state reachable by a genuinely real, unprivileged user action, and the most honest representation available under the current architecture; see the Trial-Ended persona note below for the disclosed caveat). (3) BASIC — new account, signup then a super_admin-authenticated UPDATE to `plan:'basic'`. (4) PRO (paid, not trial) — new account, signup then a super_admin-authenticated UPDATE to `plan:'pro'`, `trial_ends_at:null` (so it is unambiguously "paid PRO," not "still on trial").

**INTERNATIONAL personas** (recommended, not yet created): the identical four states, mirrored — (5) reuse existing `PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_*`, (6)/(7)/(8) new accounts + the same mutation pattern as Local, with `country='International'` set the same way every other International TEST account already is (through the app's own region-selection UI at first login, the established, precedented technique from Item 25's own E2E unblock).

**Data mutation plan, per persona** (none executed — plan only): every persona needs a real Auth user (created via Admin API, existing established pattern) and lets the app's own signup flow create its `business_settings` row (never a raw SQL insert, consistent with every existing TEST account this session). Beyond that: FREE+Active Trial needs nothing further. FREE+Trial Ended needs the app's own real Cancel-Subscription action run once, logged in as that persona. BASIC and PRO need one super_admin-authenticated `UPDATE business_settings SET plan=..., trial_ends_at=...` performed once by/through a real TEST super-admin session (or the documented Postgres-trigger-bypass technique) — no billing/customer/invoice records exist to fabricate (none of that infrastructure exists yet, confirmed above), so none should be invented.

**Trial-Ended persona — the specific, important case**: the safest, most honest representation is `plan='free'`, `trial_ends_at=null`, produced by genuinely driving the app's own real Cancel-Subscription flow (not a fabricated plan value, not a frontend-only hide of the ticker) — this is the **only** state under the current live architecture that is both (a) reachable by an actual, unprivileged, real user action and (b) resolves via the real `effectivePlan` formula to `'free'` with no trial. **Important, disclosed caveat**: this does *not* reproduce the separate "trial simply lapsed, user never cancelled" scenario — per the Trial Reset Risk finding above, that scenario currently still resolves to `effectivePlan==='pro'` (full PRO access) under the live code, so it cannot honestly be represented as a "FREE" persona at all today; it is instead itself a disclosed product/architecture gap for the Owner's awareness, not a persona to fabricate around.

**BASIC/PRO personas — billing-state finding**: confirmed no paid/billing infrastructure exists (no Stripe connection, no invoice table, no customer-ID column) — `PricingModal.jsx`'s checkout button is a disclosed, deliberate stub. The only truthful TEST representation of "BASIC"/"PRO" today is the raw `business_settings.plan` value itself, set via the privileged UPDATE path above — nothing else to fabricate, and nothing fabricated (no fake invoice/payment history).

**Item 28 readiness**: the future Plan-Identity UI can safely consume `effectivePlan` (or its two inputs, `business_settings.plan` + `trial_ends_at`, computed the same way) to distinguish FREE+Trial-Active / FREE+Trial-Ended(self-cancelled) / BASIC / PRO **without inventing a new plan value** — this is fully consistent with the standing Plan/Trial-State-are-separate-concepts decision. Recommended TEST personas for Item 28 verification: the 8 personas above (2 markets × 4 states), once created. The Trial-Reset-Risk gap disclosed above is a pre-existing product question for the Owner, not something Item 28's UI work is expected to silently paper over or resolve on its own.

**ENV/credential strategy recommendation (names only, nothing added this task)**: extend the existing `PROFLOW_TEST_<MARKET>_<FIELD>` convention (already used for `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_EMAIL`/`PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_PASSWORD`/`PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_EMAIL`/`PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_PASSWORD`) with a persona-state suffix, e.g. `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_TRIALENDED_EMAIL`/`_PASSWORD`, `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_BASIC_EMAIL`/`_PASSWORD`, `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_PRO_EMAIL`/`_PASSWORD` (mirrored `_INTL_` for International) — self-describing purpose from the name alone, no secret values ever written into any of the six continuity docs, `.env`/`.env.localtest.local` only (already gitignored), never committed. A future Claude session can identify each persona's purpose purely from its variable name without needing any secret disclosed in documentation.

## §51. Trial Expiration → FREE — Root Cause, Fix, and Complete Entitlement Matrix (added 2026-08-30, Trial Expiration → FREE task — Owner product decision, implemented and TEST-verified)

**Owner's final product rule**: when a user's free Trial expires and the user has NOT upgraded to a valid paid BASIC/PRO subscription, the user must become **effectively FREE** and receive FREE plan limits/entitlements — not merely a visual badge change, the actual application behavior. Existing user data (quotes, clients, business settings, attachments, signed/finalized quotes) is never deleted or altered — expiration changes entitlements, not ownership/history.

### Root cause (re-confirmed fresh from live code, this task)

Signup (`Dashboard.jsx`) writes `plan:'pro'`, `trial_ends_at: now+14 days` — one write, at account creation. A repo-wide grep for every `plan:` write site in the codebase found exactly two: signup (→`'pro'`) and self-cancellation (`PricingModal.jsx`, →`'free'`+`trial_ends_at:null`, both fields together, always). **No automatic trial-to-free downgrade existed anywhere** — no cron, trigger, or Edge Function ever reverted `plan` when `trial_ends_at` passed. The pre-fix `effectivePlan` formula (`Dashboard.jsx`) resolved to `'pro'` unconditionally whenever raw `plan==='pro'`, regardless of trial expiry — so a real user who let a 14-day trial lapse without cancelling kept full PRO access indefinitely, contradicting the trial-expired banner's own text ("moved to the FREE tier") and the trial-reminder email's copy.

### Architectural safety — distinguishing Trial-PRO from genuine paid PRO/BASIC (per Owner's explicit "do not guess, STOP if ambiguous" instruction)

Proven safe, not assumed, from the exhaustive write-site/RLS/trigger audit (§50 above):
- `plan==='basic'` can **never** be produced by signup (the `AS RESTRICTIVE` RLS INSERT policy on `business_settings` only permits `plan='free'∧trial_ends_at IS NULL` or `plan='pro'∧trial_ends_at` within ±2h of "now+14 days" at INSERT time) — any `'basic'` row was necessarily set by a deliberate super_admin UPDATE. Unambiguous, unconditional PRO-tier-equivalent for entitlement purposes.
- `plan==='pro'` with `trial_ends_at===null` can **only** originate from a Lifetime-grant (`handleToggleLifetime`, Admin-only, already an established, pre-existing mechanic — confirmed it never touches `plan`, only toggles `trial_ends_at` between `null` and a fresh +14 days) or another deliberate super_admin UPDATE — never from signup or self-cancel (both always pair `trial_ends_at` with a non-null value in one direction or the other). Unambiguous PRO.
- `plan==='pro'` with a **non-null, real, past** `trial_ends_at` can **only** be a lapsed trial that was never reset — since `plan` is only ever set to `'pro'` at signup (always paired with a genuine ~14-day-forward date at that moment) and nothing else ever writes `'pro'`. This is the exact, unambiguous case the fix targets: **effectivePlan → 'free'.**
- **Known, narrow, pre-existing ambiguity, NOT touched or worsened by this fix, disclosed not fixed**: `plan==='free' ∧ trial_ends_at===null` could in theory arise from either genuine self-cancellation (intended: FREE) or a Lifetime-grant applied to an already-`'free'`-plan account without also restoring `plan` (intended: PRO) — the schema cannot distinguish these two from data alone. This combination is outside this task's scope (self-cancel already correctly resolved to `'free'` before and after this fix; the fix does not touch the `rawPlan==='free'` branch at all) and is recorded here for future awareness, not resolved.

### Implementation — one centralized source of truth

New pure utility `src/utils/planEntitlements.js`, `computeEffectivePlan({ plan, trialEndsAt, now })` — same "single source of truth" pattern already established by `quoteLock.js`'s `isQuoteImmutable`. Formula: `plan==='basic'` → `'basic'` unconditionally; `plan==='pro'` → `'pro'` if `trial_ends_at` is null (Lifetime/admin-granted) or still in the future, else `'free'` (the fix); otherwise (`plan==='free'`) → unchanged fallback (`'pro'` only if a genuine still-future `trial_ends_at` is present, else `'free'`).

**Every consumer found in the exhaustive audit was wired to this single function — three real, previously-independent, conflicting formulas were found and fixed, not just the one in `Dashboard.jsx`**:
1. `Dashboard.jsx` — replaced its own inline `effectivePlan`/`isTrialExpired`/`trialDaysLeft` calculation with `computeEffectivePlan(...)`. Everything already derived from `effectivePlan` (`isPro`, `isBasicOrAbove`, `planLimit`, the monthly-quota check, the WhatsApp/Delete/Edit/Duplicate gates in `handleProtectedAction`) now automatically inherits the fix with zero further changes.
2. `SettingsTab.jsx` — the Logo-upload gate (`disabled`, styling, the "(Requires Pro plan)" badge) previously checked the **raw** `bizPlan !== 'pro'` directly, independent of trial state — a real, separate bug from the same root cause, found this task. Now receives and uses the corrected `effectivePlan` via a new prop, passed from `Dashboard.jsx`. The "PLAN" name label was also changed from raw `bizPlan` to `effectivePlan`, so it never visibly claims "PRO PLAN" while entitlements are actually FREE (the Owner's "not merely a visual badge change" requirement, read in both directions — the fix must not leave a stale contradictory visual either).
3. `QuoteForm.jsx` — the Attachments feature ("Attachments (PRO only)", found this task, not previously documented in either prior audit) gated via **raw** `userPlan==='pro'` (`userPlan` was fed `bizPlan`, the raw value) — same class of bug, now fixed by feeding `effectivePlan` from `Dashboard.jsx` instead.
4. `QuoteForm.jsx`'s submit button additionally had its own **blanket** `disabled={isTrialExpired && !isSuperAdmin}` — this blocked **all** quote creation/editing entirely once the trial's calendar date passed, regardless of `effectivePlan`, which is stricter than FREE (FREE must still get 5 quotes/month) and directly contradicted the Owner's rule. Removed — the two already-correct, already-existing enforcement points (the monthly-quota check inside `handleSaveQuote`, and `handleProtectedAction`'s `isBasicOrAbove`/`isPro` gate before Edit/Duplicate/WhatsApp/Delete are ever reachable) are now the sole, correct, non-duplicated enforcement, exactly as the Owner's "avoid duplicated conflicting formulas, prefer one centralized source of truth" instruction required.

**Deliberately NOT touched, disclosed instead (standing "NO Admin work" boundary)**: `AdminUsersTab.jsx` (`planValue`/`isLifetime` calc) and `UserDetailsModal.jsx` (`displayPlan`/`isGrantedLifetimePro` calc) each have their own, independently-derived plan-display formulas — the same class of duplicated-formula issue, and `AdminUsersTab.jsx`'s in particular does not correctly demote a lapsed-but-non-null-past-trial `'pro'` row either (it only special-cases `trial_ends_at===null` as Lifetime, missing the past-non-null case this fix targets). Flagged for a future Admin-scoped task; not fixed here, per the explicit standing instruction not to touch Admin surfaces this session.

### Complete entitlement matrix, confirmed current (re-verified fresh from live code, this task)

| Gate | Requirement | Location | Formula before | Formula after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Monthly quote creation | FREE: 5/month, BASIC: 20/month, PRO: ∞ | `Dashboard.jsx` `handleSaveQuote` | `effectivePlan`-based (already correct once reachable) | unchanged, now reachable for expired-trial users |
| Edit / Duplicate | requires BASIC or above | `Dashboard.jsx` `handleProtectedAction` (`isBasicOrAbove`) | `effectivePlan`-based | fixed via root cause |
| WhatsApp send / Delete | requires PRO | `Dashboard.jsx` `handleProtectedAction` (`isPro`) | `effectivePlan`-based | fixed via root cause |
| Business Logo upload | requires PRO | `SettingsTab.jsx` | raw `bizPlan` (bug) | `effectivePlan` (fixed this task) |
| Quote Attachments | requires PRO | `QuoteForm.jsx` `handleAttachmentClick` | raw `userPlan`/`bizPlan` (bug, newly found) | `effectivePlan` (fixed this task) |
| New-quote/edit submission | blocked entirely once trial date passed | `QuoteForm.jsx` submit button | blanket `isTrialExpired` block (over-strict, newly found) | removed — quota/edit gates above are now the sole enforcement |
| Signatures, printing, Public Quote, PDF placeholder | not plan-gated | `PublicQuote.jsx`/`PublicQuoteEn.jsx` | confirmed no plan reference at all | unchanged |
| AI Chat | not plan-gated | `AIChatWidget.jsx` | confirmed no plan reference at all | unchanged |

**Backend enforcement**: re-confirmed frontend-only for every gate above — no RLS policy or Edge Function independently re-enforces any plan-based feature limit. This is a genuine, disclosed, **pre-existing** limitation, not introduced or expanded by this fix, and this task deliberately did not attempt a backend/RLS entitlement-security redesign (explicitly out of scope, would have been a major, separately-authorized undertaking). Recorded as a known gap for a future dedicated security/architecture task if the Owner prioritizes it — not a new TODO item, since it is a characteristic of the existing architecture being disclosed, not a new backlog request.

**`subscription_ends_at` finding, re-confirmed unchanged**: still does not exist on TEST (same `PGRST 42703` result as §50's original check) despite `send-subscription-expiration-email` referencing it. Not touched, not required for this fix — remains an independent, already-disclosed defect (§50).

### TEST verification (live, both markets, zero TEST database mutation)

A CDP network-response-interception technique was used: the browser's own `business_settings` REST response was rewritten in-flight (a past `trial_ends_at`, `plan` left as the real, live `'pro'` value) so the **real, live, rendered application** exercises the fix end-to-end — without ever writing to the real TEST database. This is safer and more reversible than mutating a shared TEST account (nothing to undo) and does not require creating any of the six new subscription personas (explicitly not authorized this task).

Confirmed, both HE (Local) and EN (International), identical results: **before** interception (real, unmutated active-trial data) — `"pro PLAN"` label, Logo upload enabled — confirming the active-trial regression check passes untouched. **After** interception (simulated expired trial) — `"free PLAN"` label, Logo upload disabled with the "(Requires Pro plan)" badge, the Attachments button correctly triggers the real upgrade-confirm modal, and — critically — the New-Quote submit button is **not** disabled (FREE users can still create quotes up to their limit). A second interception pass additionally fabricated 5 quotes dated in the current month (also network-injected only, zero DB mutation) and confirmed a 6th creation attempt is correctly blocked with the real "monthly quote limit reached" alert, proving the FREE 5-quote boundary. Zero horizontal overflow at 360/390/412px and desktop, both markets. The pure `computeEffectivePlan` function itself additionally has 14 dedicated automated unit tests (`src/utils/planEntitlements.test.js`) covering active trial, expired trial (the core fix), FREE-no-trial, BASIC, PRO (including the Lifetime/`trial_ends_at===null` case), the exact expiry boundary (both directions), and a malformed trial-date string (fails safe, does not crash).

## §52. Six TEST Subscription Personas — BLOCKED on Auth User Creation Mechanism (added 2026-08-30, Structured Quote Architecture Correction + Six TEST Personas + Landing Page Access Audit task)

**🟡 Part A of this task (Item 30/31 documentation correction) is fully recorded at `PROFLOW_TODO.md` item 30.C (new, corrected home for the Project→Section→Structured-Items hierarchy) and item 31 (corrected — the hierarchy removed, Additional Notes reconfirmed as genuinely supplemental free text). No implementation. See those items for full detail, not duplicated here — and see §95 below for this item's now-declared P1/Full-LIVE-gate priority and a substantive (not just pointer) summary of the architecture, added 2026-08-31 after this exact single-sentence pointer was found to be the reason a canonical-continuity search failed to reliably locate the design.**

**Part B — six new TEST subscription personas — BLOCKED on the account-creation step specifically, evidenced not assumed.** Owner authorized creating six new fictional TEST accounts (Local/International × Trial-Ended/Basic/Pro) to complement the two existing active-trial accounts, per §50's persona model. Before creating anything, this task re-confirmed Fresh Local State first, per instruction: the Trial Expiration → FREE fix (§51) is intact and uncommitted; `computeEffectivePlan` unchanged; the two existing active-trial TEST accounts still correctly resolve to PRO (live, unmutated, re-verified); 70/70 tests pass; lint/build clean.

**The blocker**: creating a genuinely new, usable TEST Auth user requires either (a) the Supabase Admin API (`auth.admin.createUser`, which can pre-confirm an email, skipping the confirmation-email step entirely) — this requires a service-role key, which is **not** stored anywhere in this repository's `.env`/`.env.localtest.local` (only a placeholder comment exists; the permanently-banned `supabase projects api-keys --reveal` command was correctly not attempted), or (b) the app's own real self-service signup form — but this was **live-verified, via the raw Supabase Auth REST API directly** (not the app's own generic, error-swallowing UI message), to require email confirmation before a new account can log in, and TEST's transactional-email sending is **currently rate-limited** (`HTTP 429`, `error_code: "over_email_send_rate_limit"`, confirmed via a real signup attempt against `quotecode-test`'s own Auth endpoint). Neither path is available within this task's own tool access — no TEST service-role key, no email inbox access to click a confirmation link.

**This is why the two existing TEST accounts work today but no new ones could be created this task**: they were evidently created through a mechanism this task's own tool access does not have (most plausibly, the Owner's own Supabase Dashboard access, or Admin-API access with a service-role key, given both accounts use real "+alias@gmail.com" addresses under an inbox the Owner controls — not something reproducible without either credential).

**Recommended resolution paths, for the Owner to choose from, not decided here**:
1. Supply the `quotecode-test` service-role key once (same handling discipline already used for the TEST anon key earlier this session — installed directly into a gitignored local file, never displayed/logged/printed) so a future task can use the Admin API's `email_confirm: true` option to create pre-confirmed accounts with zero email dependency.
2. The Owner performs the six signups personally (or authorizes a future task to drive them via the real form) using real "+alias@gmail.com"-style addresses under an inbox they control, then manually clicks each confirmation email themselves — mirroring exactly how the two existing accounts were evidently created.
3. Increase/reset TEST's transactional email rate limit or configure a custom SMTP provider for the TEST Auth project specifically (a Supabase project-settings change, not something this session's tooling can perform).

**Nothing was created, mutated, or fabricated to work around this** — no invented confirmation bypass, no guessed service-role key, no TEST database mutation. The six personas were not created; the two existing active-trial accounts and the underlying `computeEffectivePlan` architecture were re-verified instead (regression-only), which is the maximum safely achievable this task without the blocker's resolution.

## §53. Landing Page Access Audit for ChatGPT — Findings (added 2026-08-30, same task — READ-ONLY, no landing-page mutation)

**🟡 UPDATE (2026-08-30, Owner-Locked Regression Rule + UI QA + Vercel Domain Redirect Audit task) — CONFIRMED, not corrected, plus important missing context added**: a fresh, independent re-audit re-tested `quotecode.vercel.app` root/`/he`/`/en` explicitly WITHOUT auto-following redirects first, then with. Result: **still no redirect exists** — `HTTP 200` directly, no `Location` header, `num_redirects: 0`, final URL identical to the requested URL, for all three paths. The specific factual claims below (byte-identical HTML, matching video sizes, HTTP 200 direct-serve) are **re-confirmed accurate, not corrected**. **New context this task**: the canonical product intent is that `quotecode.vercel.app` should eventually **redirect** to `www.quotecodepro.com` (it is not meant to operate as an independent alternate domain long-term) — that redirect is simply **not yet configured** on Vercel today. This is a real, disclosed gap between intended architecture and current deployment, not an error in the original finding below. The recommendation to use `quotecode.vercel.app` as a ChatGPT access URL remains factually valid **today**, but should be understood as relying on a currently-unintended, not-guaranteed-to-persist absence of a redirect — not the permanent intended architecture. No Vercel configuration was changed by this audit (read-only, as authorized); implementing the intended redirect is a separate, future, explicitly-Production-routing-affecting decision, not undertaken here.

**Routing/build, confirmed from source**: `/he` and `/en` are two `react-router-dom` routes (`App.jsx`) within the **same single-page-app bundle** — `LandingLocal`/`LandingGlobal` respectively — never separate deployments or builds. `main` HEAD (`17ac4d3a...`) is confirmed unchanged and already established this session as what auto-deploys to Vercel Production; `git status` confirms zero local drift in `LandingLocal.jsx`/`LandingGlobal.jsx`/`App.jsx` specifically — so the live custom domain is provably running exactly this repository's current landing-page source.

**Alternate existing public URL found and verified equivalent, not merely assumed similar**: `https://quotecode.vercel.app` (Vercel's own default production-alias domain, already publicly documented in this project's own history as serving the same deployment, no redirect between the two domains). Live, read-only checks: `www.quotecodepro.com/he` and `quotecode.vercel.app/he` return **byte-for-byte identical HTML** (3615 bytes each, identical `diff`), same for `/en`; the referenced JS/CSS asset hashes are therefore necessarily identical too (same build). Both domains are HTTP 200-reachable.

**Demo video, confirmed from source and verified live**: served as a plain **public static file**, not an embed/signed URL — `LandingLocal.jsx` uses `/proflow-demo.mp4` (HE), `LandingGlobal.jsx` uses `/proflow-demoEN.mp4` (EN), both real files in `public/`. Live HEAD requests confirm both are directly, unauthenticated-ly fetchable on both domains with **identical `Content-Length`** (HE: 2,522,561 bytes; EN: 2,578,902 bytes) — no secrets, no tokens, no query strings, nothing private. Safe to share directly.

**SPA-rendering caveat, disclosed proactively**: the initial HTML response is a small Vite/React shell (script + link tags only) — the actual visible landing content renders client-side after JS execution. If ChatGPT's fetch tool does not execute JavaScript, reaching either URL (even if the network-level block that affects the custom domain doesn't apply to `quotecode.vercel.app`) may still only yield the bare shell, not the rendered page. This is why a fallback, non-network-dependent review package was also prepared this task (see below) rather than relying on URL access alone.

**Fallback review package — prepared, not merely recommended**: four full-page screenshots captured via isolated headless Chrome against the real, live, public production URLs (read-only, zero mutation, no login): `landing_HE_desktop_fullpage.png` (1425×3440), `landing_HE_mobile_fullpage.png` (390×5729, 390px viewport), `landing_EN_desktop_fullpage.png` (1425×3542), `landing_EN_mobile_fullpage.png` (390×5937, 390px viewport) — all saved to this session's local scratchpad, not committed or published anywhere; the Owner can hand these (and/or the direct video file URLs above) to ChatGPT through whatever channel they choose. Claude did not critique, redesign, or subjectively describe the landing content — captures only, per explicit instruction.

**Landing security, confirmed**: no tunnel, no localhost exposure, no TEST authentication/cookies/session tokens exposed, no ENV/API/service-role secrets referenced or exposed anywhere in this audit, no new public deployment created, no landing-page file touched. All URLs disclosed above were already public before this task and remain unchanged by it.

## §54. Owner-Approved = LOCKED / Regression-Protected — PERMANENT REQUIREMENT (added 2026-08-30, Owner-Locked Regression Rule task, Owner-established)

**Owner decision, standing rule, applies to ALL future ProFlow work — not scoped to any single item, does not expire, does not need to be re-requested.**

**The rule**: once a behavior, design, feature, or fix has (1) been implemented, (2) been TEST-verified, and (3) been reviewed/accepted by the Owner, it becomes **Owner-Approved / DONE / LOCKED / regression-protected**. From that point forward it must not change unless the Owner explicitly authorizes reopening or changing it. Covers, without exception: behavior, functionality, layout, visual styling, copy/text, responsive behavior, HE behavior, EN behavior, Local behavior, International behavior, calculations, business rules, permissions, workflows, and every previously-approved fix.

**No indirect changes.** A new task is never itself authorization to alter unrelated LOCKED behavior — including indirectly, through refactoring, shared CSS, responsive CSS, component cleanup, dependency changes, layout changes, design-system changes, code deduplication, performance optimization, translation work, HE/EN parity work, mobile fixes, desktop fixes, accessibility work, feature implementation elsewhere, or bug fixes elsewhere. "Improvement," "cleaner code," and "better design" are explicitly **not** authorization to touch LOCKED behavior.

**Regression protection is engineering's responsibility, not the Owner's.** The Owner must not have to repeatedly remember or manually re-verify whether something previously completed still works — that burden belongs to Claude Lead, Agent HE, Agent EN, and the test process. Once Owner-approved and LOCKED, the Owner is entitled to treat it as finished. If a later task causes a LOCKED item to change unexpectedly, that is a **regression**, to be caught and reported as such, not normalized away.

**Required stop condition**: if a genuinely new feature/fix requires changing an existing LOCKED behavior, **STOP before changing it** and report: what LOCKED behavior would need to change, why, alternatives considered, HE impact, EN impact, Local impact, International impact, and regression risk — then wait for explicit Owner authorization. Approval is never inferred from the new task's own existence or scope.

**Enforcement mechanism — uses existing structures, does not duplicate them with a new ledger.** Per explicit Owner instruction not to create unnecessary documentation duplication, this rule does **not** introduce a new tracking document. LOCKED state is the mechanisms already in continuous use across the six canonical files, now formally designated as this rule's enforcement surface:
- A `PROFLOW_TODO.md` item marked `🟢 COMPLETE / VERIFIED` (or `TEST VERIFIED`, `Owner visually accepted`, etc.) **is**, from that point, Owner-Approved/LOCKED under this rule — §47's existing "never delete a completed TODO item" discipline already protects its historical record; this rule adds that its *content/behavior* must also not silently drift.
- A `PROFLOW_HANDOFF.md` §18 step recording a verdict of `PASS` (Owner-reviewed or explicitly accepted) is a LOCKED checkpoint for whatever it describes.
- The existing **File-by-File HE/EN Change Ledger** (§37, cross-market tasks) is the concrete mechanism for identifying exactly which files/surfaces a given LOCKED item touches — a future task working near a LOCKED file should consult the ledger entry that last touched it before assuming a change is safe.
- Before any task changes a file/surface that a prior `PASS`/`COMPLETE`/`VERIFIED` entry already covers, that entry is the signal to check first — if the planned change would alter what was previously verified, this is the **stop condition** above, not a green light.

**Relationship to existing rules**: this rule does not replace §36 (Test-First/Owner-Gated Live Release), §37 (HE/EN Parity), or §38 (Effort-Level Declaration) — it adds a durable "once accepted, stays accepted" guarantee on top of them. §36 already requires Owner approval before LIVE; this rule extends that same approval's weight forward in time, protecting the approved result from later, unrelated erosion.

**Addendum (added 2026-08-31, Owner-established, carried forward explicitly from the Critical Signature Security Production Promotion task so it is not lost after that promotion)**: OWNER-APPROVED/LOCKED protects **observable behavior**, not merely a specific file or component. A change to a shared/parent/layout component, or a refactor, that never touches the LOCKED component's own file but changes what a user actually sees/experiences at that LOCKED surface is still a regression under this rule — "I didn't edit that file" is not a defense. Any task touching a shared/parent/layout surface must regression-test every LOCKED behavior that surface affects, even when the LOCKED component itself was not directly edited. One concrete, already-documented instance of this principle: §61's **"responsive transformations must preserve functional capability, not merely visible content"** rule (a Desktop capability — e.g., column sorting — silently absent after a Mobile responsive transformation is a regression, even though no single component was "deleted").

## §55. User UI QA Fixes — Row Amount Typography, EN Actions Clipping, Desktop Width, Trial Notice Position (added 2026-08-30, Owner-Locked Regression Rule + UI QA + Vercel Domain Redirect Audit task — implemented and TEST-verified, both markets)

**🟡 UPDATE (2026-08-30, Owner Visual QA Correction task) — CORRECTED, values below superseded, not the current state**: item 1 (row amount, `600`) and item 3 (desktop width, flat `1440px`) below were both revised again this same day after direct Owner visual review. Row amount is now `500` (not `600`); the desktop width token is now the viewport-relative `min(1320px, 85vw)` (not a flat `1440px`), after a live-verified regression (the flat value, and an initial narrower `72vw` candidate, both either left the geometry too wide or reintroduced the EN/HE Actions-clipping defect at 1280px). See §57 below for the full corrected record. **Also corrected**: this section's closing line originally said the four fixes "are now Owner-Approved/LOCKED per §54 once reviewed" — that was inaccurate even at the time; per §54 itself, nothing becomes LOCKED without actual Owner review/approval, and as of this update the width/typography values specifically remain **IMPLEMENTED / TEST-VERIFIED / AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL**, not LOCKED. Items 2 (EN Actions fix) and 4 (Trial notice position) below are unaffected by this correction and remain as originally recorded.

Four Owner-observed USER-interface issues, audited first, then fixed narrowly.

**1. Quote History row amount typography** (`src/components/QuotesTab.jsx`): the per-row `quote.total` amount (desktop table and mobile card) was `fontWeight: '800'`, changed to `'600'` (semi-bold) — confirmed via live `getComputedStyle` check, both markets. Nothing else touched — the summary/total cards live entirely in `Dashboard.jsx`, a separate file/location, completely unaffected; genuine quote totals elsewhere (`QuoteForm.jsx`, `PublicQuote.jsx`/`PublicQuoteEn.jsx`) were not touched at all.

**2. EN Quote History Actions column clipping** (`src/components/QuotesTab.jsx`): root cause — the Actions `<th>` had no explicit `width`/`minWidth` at all, unlike its sibling columns (Views: 60px, mail-icon: 50px), combined with the pre-fix 980px desktop content width leaving too little natural room for the table's last column. Fixed with an explicit `minWidth: '110px'` on the Actions header, matching the existing sibling-column pattern (not a broad CSS change) — combined with fix 3 below (more available width), the Actions button is now confirmed fully within the viewport in both markets: EN `{left:1318, right:1392}` vs a 1440px viewport (48px clearance); HE `{left:33, right:99}` (fully on-screen on the RTL side). No buttons hidden, no usability reduction, no horizontal page overflow introduced.

**3. Desktop — use available screen width** (`src/index.css`, `src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`): audited the existing shared `--pf-desktop-content-width` (980px) token first and found it is used by **both** the authenticated app shell (`Dashboard.jsx`, all tabs) **and** Public Quote (`PublicQuote.jsx`/`PublicQuoteEn.jsx`) — the latter's 980px value is itself an extensively-verified, Owner-locked value from an earlier dedicated Global Surface Audit (shell-padding/border `calc()` math specifically reverse-engineered to make the *visible content* exactly 980px). Public Quote was **not** named in this task's own regression-check list. Per §54 (no indirect changes to LOCKED behavior established in this very same task), widening the shared token would have silently also widened the separately-locked, unaudited-this-task Public Quote surface. **Decoupled instead**: a new, dedicated CSS variable `--pf-dashboard-desktop-content-width: 1440px` now governs only the authenticated app's content wrapper (`Dashboard.jsx` line ~2910); `--pf-desktop-content-width` remains exactly `980px`, untouched, still used only by Public Quote. Live-verified: Dashboard content wrapper now measures ~1405px (up from 980px) at a 1440px viewport, identical in both markets; Public Quote's own token confirmed still reading `980px` via a live `getComputedStyle` check on `document.documentElement` — genuinely unaffected. Regression-verified at Dashboard/New Quote/Quote History/Clients/Business Settings (all render inside the same single wrapper, so one check covers all) — zero horizontal overflow at any tested width, Client Type badge and other existing UI unaffected. Admin layout not touched (out of scope, untouched by construction — Admin does not use this wrapper).

**4. Trial notice vertical position** (`src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`): root cause — both notice variants (`dash-trial-slidebar` for expiring/expired, `dash-trial-ticker-lane` for a plain active trial) are `position: absolute; top: 100%` children of `.dash-header-bar`, occupying the gap between the purple header and the nav row below — but the header's own `marginBottom` (14px, unconditional) was shorter than either notice's actual footprint (~44-52px for the card variant, ~36px for the ticker), so the notice's own bottom edge extended into where the nav row begins, matching the Owner's observed collision. **Fixed by giving the notice a dedicated safe vertical lane, not by redesigning it**: a new derived `hasVisibleTrialNotice` boolean conditionally raises `.dash-header-bar`'s `marginBottom` from `14px` to `64px` **only while a notice is actually visible** — zero change to the header/nav gap the rest of the time (the default, most-common state), and zero change to either notice's own text, color, motion, timing, or "no card/background" design (both LOCKED, per the Owner's explicit instruction not to redesign them). Live-verified, both markets: notice bottom edge measured at 104px, nav row top edge at 132px — 28px of clear separation, `collision: false`, confirmed via direct bounding-box measurement, not just visual inspection; also confirmed visually via full-page screenshots.

**Verification summary**: all four fixes live-verified together, both markets (HE/Local via `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_*`, EN/International via `PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_*`), desktop (1440px) and mobile (360/390/412px) — zero horizontal overflow anywhere, zero regression in the Client Type badge (§26), the Trial Expiration → FREE fix (§51), or any other previously-approved UI. 70/70 automated tests pass (Trial Expiration tests preserved and re-confirmed green), lint clean, build succeeds.

## §56. ProFlow Global Surface Width Contract — PERMANENT REQUIREMENT (added 2026-08-30, Owner Visual QA Correction task, Owner-established)

**Owner decision, standing rule, applies to ALL future ProFlow work — not scoped to any single screen, does not expire.**

**The rule**: ProFlow must have ONE canonical responsive content-width strategy. The same canonical strategy must govern equivalent full-page product surfaces across the product — authenticated application (Dashboard, Quote History, New Quote, Clients, Business Settings, Catalog, Finances), HE, EN, Local, International, and Public Quote/displayed-quote presentation, unless the Owner explicitly approves a documented exception. At the same viewport width, HE and EN must have the same overall content geometry; Local and International must have the same overall content geometry.

**RTL/LTR is not a functional-reason exception.** Language is not a functional-reason exception. Market is not a functional-reason exception. Direction may change element order/alignment/mirroring — it must never change the canonical overall content width. A width difference is permitted only when there is a genuine functional reason **and** the Owner explicitly approves that specific, documented exception.

**One source of truth, not accumulated arbitrary widths.** The desired architecture is one canonical width contract/token that equivalent surfaces reference, rather than each screen independently choosing its own max-width, padding, or container strategy. Where consolidating an existing surface onto the canonical contract would modify Owner-LOCKED geometry (§54), the LOCKED rule's stop condition applies: audit and report the conflict, do not silently change it, wait for explicit Owner authorization for that specific surface.

**Relationship to §54**: this rule defines *what* the canonical contract should eventually cover; §54 governs *when* a specific numeric implementation of it becomes protected. A width value is never Owner-Approved/LOCKED merely because it satisfies this contract's architecture — it still requires the Owner's own explicit visual approval of that specific result (see §57).

## §57. Desktop Width Balanced Geometry (Corrected) + Mobile Quote History Width + Amount Hierarchy (Round 2) — IMPLEMENTED, AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL (added 2026-08-30, Owner Visual QA Correction task)

**Owner clarification acknowledged first**: an earlier report of an HE-vs-EN Desktop width discrepancy was the Owner opening the wrong EN URL during their own comparison — there is **no** code-level HE/EN width difference. This task's own fresh, controlled, same-condition measurement (fresh login per market, identical viewport, identical zoom, at 1280/1366/1440/1920px) independently confirms this: every measured value (wrapper left/right/width, nav, KPI grid, table wrapper) was **byte-identical** between HE and EN at all four widths, both before and after this task's changes. HE/EN parity was never broken and remains fully preserved.

**The real issue, per fresh Owner visual reference**: the shared Desktop width itself (already correctly identical in both markets) was simply too wide — a flat `1440px` cap (§55) provided almost no effective constraint below 1440px viewports (the only real gutter was `.dash-main-content`'s then-10px padding), producing a near-edge-to-edge appearance at the most common laptop resolutions. The Owner's new visual reference suggested content occupying roughly 70-75% of the viewport, explicitly described as an unconfirmed initial reference, not a hard-coded percentage.

**Iteration 1 (this task) — `min(1320px, 72vw)`**: chosen to hold a stable ~72% width-utilization ratio across the full 1280-1920 range (a flat pixel value cannot do this — its percentage of viewport necessarily drops as the viewport grows). Live-tested and **found to regress the earlier EN/HE Actions-clipping fix**: at 1280px, the narrowed wrapper (911px) left too little room for the Quote History table's columns, and the Actions column was visibly clipped/hidden at the viewport edge in HE (confirmed via full-page screenshot, not just measurement) — a real functional defect, not merely a tighter fit. Per this task's own explicit priority ordering ("EN Actions clipping correction... must NOT regress"), this candidate was rejected.

**Iteration 2 (final this task) — `min(1320px, 85vw)`**: re-tested at all four widths, both markets. Utilization: 1280px → 84%, 1366px → 84%, 1440px → 84%, 1920px → 68.75% (capped at 1320px). Live-confirmed **no clipping** at any tested width, both markets (`actionsFullyVisible: true`, and the table's own measured box now exactly matches its wrapper's box at every width — the earlier clipping run showed the table box overflowing past its wrapper, a reliable signal of the same defect). This value is meaningfully narrower than the prior flat `1440px` (which gave 91-94% utilization at common resolutions) while staying safely clear of the point where the table's columns stop fitting. It does not fully reach the Owner's loose 70-75% reference — that gap is disclosed as a real functional constraint, not hidden. `.dash-main-content`'s desktop padding was reduced back from `32px` to a modest `16px` "safety cushion," since the viewport-relative width token now does the primary gutter work (its own `margin: 0 auto` centering contributes far more effective gutter than the padding does at any tested width).

**Mobile Quote History width utilization — root cause and fix**: the "Recent Quotes History" panel wrapper (`QuotesTab.jsx`) used a single, uniform `padding: '14px'` on both desktop and mobile — combined with `.dash-main-content`'s mobile override (`6px`, pre-existing) and each mobile card's own internal padding (`8px 10px`, pre-existing, unchanged), this produced a genuinely compounded ~30px gutter on each side before any card content began — the exact anti-pattern the Owner described. Fixed narrowly: the panel's own `isMobileView` state (already used by this exact component to switch between the desktop table and mobile card list) now also drives its padding — `8px` on mobile, unchanged `14px` on desktop. Live-verified, both markets, 360/390/412px: total page gutter now `15px` each side (down from ~30px), card width `330px` at 360px viewport (91.7% utilization), zero horizontal overflow at any width, byte-identical between HE and EN at every measurement.

**Amount typography hierarchy (round 2)**: Rubik is self-hosted with explicit weight files for 400/500/600/700/800/900 (both Latin and Hebrew subsets, `src/fonts.css`) — `500` is a genuine, distinctly-rendering weight, not a synthetic fallback, confirmed before changing anything. Quote History row amount (`QuotesTab.jsx`, desktop table and mobile card): `600 → 500`. Dashboard Total Revenue/`סך הכנסות` (`Dashboard.jsx`, the `.dash-kpi-value.pf-money` element specifically — confirmed via direct DOM ancestor-chain inspection, not a generic class query, since more than one `.dash-kpi-value` element exists on the page and a naive selector can match the wrong one): `800 → 600`. Result: Total Revenue is now visibly, deliberately bolder than an ordinary row amount (`600` vs `500`), a clear, intentional financial hierarchy, confirmed via both computed-style inspection and visual screenshot review, both markets. No currency values, formatting, decimals, or calculations were touched.

They are **IMPLEMENTED / TEST-VERIFIED / AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL**. Only an explicit Owner approval moves them to LOCKED.

## Incident Record — Continuity Sync Execution Failure, Corrected 2026-08-30 (Continuity Sync Failure Audit + Recovery task — NOT a new permanent rule, disclosed per this project's standing incident-transparency practice)

**What happened**: across three consecutive tasks this session (bootstrap, TEST Acceptance Readiness Package 1, Client Type Badge), the six canonical files were correctly updated in the main working tree, and every task's Final Report accurately stated so — but the separate, already-established continuity-sync step (§17.J's "Claude local → isolated continuity worktree → secret/privacy scan → explicit staging → commit on `proflow-continuity` → push **only** `proflow-continuity`") was never executed. ChatGPT subsequently read `ref=proflow-continuity` on GitHub directly and found it stale (last commit `998f1bd`, predating this entire session) — `PROFLOW_CLAUDE_LATEST_REPORT.md` there did not contain the Client Type Badge report, and `PROFLOW_TODO.md` there lacked items 27/28.

**Root cause**: task-level instructions this session repeatedly stated "NO commit. NO push." — correctly scoped to **application code on `main`** (per every task's own surrounding context: paired with "NO Vercel deploy," "NO Production/LIVE action," etc.). §17.J already establishes that continuity-branch documentation sync is a **separate, standing, routinely-authorized layer** ("DOCUMENTATION SYNC AUTHORIZATION ≠ ... ≠ CONTINUITY PUSH AUTHORIZATION ≠ MAIN PUSH AUTHORIZATION"), verified safe (no Vercel deployment consequence) specifically so it would **not** need to wait for the same authorization as `main`. The execution failure was conflating the two: treating "no commit/push" as blanket guidance covering documentation too, when the existing architecture already treats them as entirely distinct.

**Correction applied same task**: the isolated continuity worktree (`quotecode-saas-continuity`, checked out on `proflow-continuity`) was updated with the current six files, diffed and secret/privacy-scanned (clean), staged explicitly by name (never `git add -A`), committed (`d4a6b03`), and pushed to `proflow-continuity` only. **Read back directly from GitHub** (via the GitHub REST Contents API against `ref=proflow-continuity`, not local git cache) to confirm all six files are present and current, `PROFLOW_CLAUDE_LATEST_REPORT.md` contains the Client Type Badge report, and `PROFLOW_TODO.md` contains items 27/28 — all confirmed. `main`'s HEAD (local and remote) was unchanged throughout (`17ac4d3a...`); no application file was staged, committed, or touched.

**No new permanent rule was added.** §17.J already fully covers this; the fix is executional (actually perform the existing step at the end of each task), not architectural. Future sessions: perform the §17.J continuity-branch sync at the end of every meaningful task, independent of whatever application-code commit/push authorization state is in force for that same task — the two are unrelated gates.

## §58. Customer-Signature Authorization — Forensic Findings + Permanent Customer-Only-Actions Rule (added 2026-08-30, Critical Signature Forensic Audit task)

**Permanent intended product rule (documented separately from the fresh technical findings below, per this task's own explicit instruction not to conflate the two)**: CUSTOMER-ONLY ACTIONS MUST REMAIN CUSTOMER-ONLY. A business-side user must never gain customer-side approval/signature authority as a side effect of refactoring, shared components, route reuse, auth changes, HE/EN work, responsive work, UI changes, or feature development. Changing this boundary requires explicit Owner authorization. UI hiding alone is never sufficient to satisfy this rule — the authoritative enforcement must exist at the mutation/persistence boundary.

**Fresh forensic findings (this task, evidence-based, not guessed)**:

- **Signing path traced end to end**: `PublicQuote.jsx`/`PublicQuoteEn.jsx` (`handleApprove`) -> `useSignaturePad.js` (canvas capture, shared hook, identical both markets) -> `supabase.rpc('public_approve_quote', { p_quote_id, p_signature_data_url })` -> Postgres function `public.public_approve_quote(uuid,text)` (`SECURITY DEFINER`, owner `postgres`, `search_path` pinned, `EXECUTE` granted to `anon`+`authenticated`, `PUBLIC` revoked -- captured faithfully from live Production in `supabase/migrations/20260830000001_capture_base_functions_triggers.sql`).
- **The RPC's actual body validates only**: quote id present, signature is a non-empty, size-capped, regex-valid `data:image/png;base64,...` string, and the target row's current `status` is `draft`/`sent` with an empty `signature` (one atomic conditional `UPDATE`). **It contains no reference to `auth.uid()` anywhere, no ownership check, no anonymous-only requirement, no customer-specific token.** `guard_quote_immutability()` (the other quotes-table guard trigger) only fires once a quote is ALREADY approved/paid/signed -- it provides zero protection against the FIRST transition into that state, contrary to what the original Aug 25 remediation's own documentation implied ("existing quote immutability trigger remains an independent lower-layer guard").
- **UI-layer protection that DOES exist and DOES work correctly**: `get-public-quote` (Edge Function, service_role) computes `is_owner_viewing: Boolean(callerUserId && callerUserId === quote.user_id)` from the caller's own Authorization header (present automatically via `supabase.functions.invoke` whenever the browser tab holds an authenticated session) -- when true, `PublicQuote.jsx`/`PublicQuoteEn.jsx` hide the signing canvas/button entirely and show an "admin view, signature area is customer-only" notice instead. This correctly protects the specific case of a quote's own creator opening their own link in the same authenticated browser tab. **It provides no protection whatsoever against (a) a direct call to the RPC bypassing the UI, by anyone -- the quote's own owner, a different authenticated business account, or an anonymous script with the quote id -- or (b) any authenticated business user (not just the quote's own owner) opening a DIFFERENT business's quote link, since for them `is_owner_viewing` is false and they see the normal, fully-functional customer signing UI, identical to a genuine customer.**
- **Live TEST reproduction (Owner-authorized, TEST/local mutation only, no Production interaction)**: signed in as `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_EMAIL` via ordinary `signInWithPassword` (the established, precedented credential-use pattern, §22), selected one of that account's own `draft` quotes with no existing signature, and called `public_approve_quote` directly via the TEST project's REST RPC endpoint using that session's own access token -- bypassing `PublicQuote.jsx` entirely. Result: **HTTP 204**. Re-queried the same quote immediately after: `status` = `approved`, `signature` = a 114-character valid PNG data URL, both persisted. This is a direct, reproduced demonstration of a business user persisting customer-role approval/signature state on their own quote, exactly the Owner-suspected scenario.
- **Historical integrity -- what can and cannot be proven**: the `quotes` table has no column recording who or what performed an approval (no `signed_by`, no approver role, no IP/session marker) -- only `status` and a raw `signature` base64 blob. **What CAN be proven**: the mutation path has no server-side identity check today, and is TEST-confirmed exploitable by an authenticated business session. **What CANNOT be proven**: whether any specific existing/historical signature on any real quote (TEST or Production) was actually created this way rather than by a genuine customer -- there is no data trail to distinguish the two. No historical signature has been or should be treated as invalid based on this finding alone; none were altered.
- **Previous protection -- proven vs. unknown**: the original "Public Quote Security Remediation" commit (`1caaff6`, 2026-08-25) that created `public_approve_quote` documented, for the sibling `public_increment_quote_view` RPC, "live tests passed for anon, owner, different authenticated TEST user" -- but recorded **no equivalent owner/different-user test for `public_approve_quote` itself**, only functional payload-validation tests. There is no evidence in git history that the RPC's identity check was ever present and later removed. **Conclusion: UNKNOWN whether this is a true regression of something that once worked; the more evidence-supported characterization is an unclosed gap present since the RPC's original creation, not a subsequently-removed protection.** The `is_owner_viewing` UI guard itself has not regressed and is unchanged/working.
- **HE impact**: AFFECTED -- `PublicQuote.jsx` calls the identical RPC with identical parameters. **EN impact**: AFFECTED -- `PublicQuoteEn.jsx` calls the identical RPC with identical parameters. Both markets share one signing path; there is no market-specific protection to compare.
- **Production exposure -- read-only evidence only, no live Production query or mutation performed**: `supabase/migrations/20260830000001_capture_base_functions_triggers.sql`'s own header states every function in that file, `public_approve_quote` included, was "captured EXACTLY as it is defined live on Production today" (captured 2026-08-30, i.e., very recently). That captured body is the same zero-identity-check definition found and TEST-reproduced above. **PRODUCTION POTENTIALLY AFFECTED: YES**, on this documentary evidence -- no Production quote was signed/approved/mutated/queried to reach this conclusion.
- **Recommended minimal safe fix (proposal only -- NOT implemented this task)**: add an authorization check inside `public_approve_quote` that rejects the call whenever the caller is identifiable as ANY ProFlow business account (`auth.uid() IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM business_settings WHERE user_id = auth.uid())`) -- not merely "caller equals this quote's own owner," since that narrower check would still leave the cross-account gap open. Anonymous/genuine-customer approval (the intended, legitimate path) is unaffected by this check.
- **Required regression tests for any future fix (proposal only)**: (1) anonymous/customer approval still succeeds; (2) the quote's own authenticated business owner calling the RPC directly is rejected; (3) a different authenticated business account calling the RPC on someone else's quote is rejected; (4) `is_owner_viewing` UI hide still functions unchanged; (5) `guard_quote_immutability` still blocks post-approval edits; (6) both HE and EN re-verified, since they share the RPC; (7) the frontend's existing generic error-toast handling still degrades gracefully for a legitimately-rejected direct-API call.

**No remediation was implemented this task -- audit only, per explicit instruction.** No Production quote was signed, approved, mutated, or used for testing; no existing signature was modified; all reproduction used TEST-only data. The mutated TEST quote's approved/signed state was NOT reverted, since the immutability trigger blocks reverting an approved/signed quote by design -- this is disclosed, not hidden.

## §59. Final Canonical Desktop Width Alignment -- IMPLEMENTED, AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL (added 2026-08-30, Critical Signature Forensic Audit + Final Canonical Width Alignment task; supersedes §57's width experimentation)

**Owner-final decision**: all prior Desktop width experimentation (flat `1440px`, flat `1320px`, `min(1320px,72vw)`, `min(1320px,85vw)` -- §55/§57) is superseded. There is no new width to design, estimate, or approximate. **The current Owner-approved Public Quote Desktop width contract (`--pf-desktop-content-width`, 980px, `src/index.css`, LOCKED, unchanged) IS the canonical ProFlow Desktop width contract.** `--pf-dashboard-desktop-content-width` now reads `var(--pf-desktop-content-width)` directly -- a genuine single source of truth rather than two independently-set values that happen to be equal. The dedicated token itself was kept (not fully inlined away) so a future Dashboard-only change still cannot indirectly touch Public Quote's own CSS, preserving the §54 risk-isolation intent from the task that first split them.

**Tables adapt to the product width; the product width does not expand to fit a table.** The prior task's own reasoning ("72vw clips Actions, therefore ProFlow needs 85vw") is explicitly rejected as a pattern. `QuotesTab.jsx`'s Quote History table was narrowed to genuinely fit inside 980px (952px of real available width once the panel's own padding is subtracted) instead: compact metadata columns (Client Type, Views, email indicator, Actions) reduced to near their real minimum content width; Client Name/Description (the flexible, descriptive columns) still receive the larger remaining share. Actions column kept an explicit `minWidth` (92px, down from 110px) rather than removed entirely, to avoid reintroducing the earlier EN Actions-clipping regression via a different route. Live-measured, both markets, all four required breakpoints (1280/1366/1440/1920): wrapper width exactly 980px everywhere (byte-identical HE/EN), table width and its wrapper's width match exactly (no overflow) at every width, Actions column fully visible in both RTL (HE, left edge) and LTR (EN, right edge) -- confirmed both numerically and via screenshot.

**Quote History Desktop density** (Owner-observed excess vertical whitespace, same task): root cause was simply generous cell padding (`6px` vertical per `<td>`/`<th>`, comparable padding on the status badge and Actions button) rather than any structural bug. Reduced uniformly: cell padding `6px 8px -> 4px 6px`, status badge padding `3px 8px -> 2px 7px`, Actions button padding `4px 10px -> 3px 9px`. No information was removed -- the before-VAT secondary line, all badges, and all columns remain exactly as before, just less padding around them. Live-measured (HE, representative row with the before-VAT subline): row height `51px -> 47px`; (EN, no subline): `38px -> 34px`; Actions button height `25px -> 23px`; status badge height `23px -> 21px` (measured directly with a corrected selector -- an initial generic rounded-pill query matched the unrelated fixed-size Client Type icon badge first, a measurement-script bug, not a code defect, resolved by matching on the actual status label text instead).

**Incidental discovery and fix**: the prior task's `min(1320px, 85vw)` token, because it applied unconditionally to the Dashboard content wrapper with no explicit pixel floor, was ALSO constraining Mobile card width whenever `85vw` fell below the actual mobile viewport width (it always does, at every phone width) -- producing a 27-31px page gutter at 360/390/412px instead of the previously-reported 15px, confirmed via fresh, precisely-selector-corrected live measurement (before-state re-test, this task). This was not caught by the prior task's own mobile verification. The new fixed `980px` value removes this entirely by construction -- `980px` always exceeds any mobile viewport, so the wrapper's `maxWidth` never engages there, exactly matching the original design comment's own stated assumption. Confirmed live: mobile gutter is now a clean `6px` at all three required widths, both markets, matching `.dash-main-content`'s dedicated mobile padding override; card width `348px`/`378px`/`400px` at `360`/`390`/`412px` (96.7-97.1% utilization); zero horizontal overflow anywhere.

**Public Quote itself: measured, compared, NOT modified.** Its own file/CSS were not touched this task. Its width (980px content / 1062px shell) is now, by construction, identical to the authenticated app's canonical width -- this closes the "bring Public Quote onto the same contract" item from §57's Global Width Surface Matrix as a natural consequence of aligning the Dashboard TO Public Quote, rather than requiring a separate change to Public Quote.

**Status, UPDATED 2026-08-31 (Signature Fix + Mobile Cleanup + Email Audit + Lock Final Width task) — OWNER-APPROVED / LOCKED.** The Owner completed a visual QA pass of this section's `980px`-aligned width, narrowed table columns, and reduced row padding, and explicitly approved them: "THE CURRENT DESKTOP WIDTH IS APPROVED. DO NOT CHANGE IT." Per §54, this moves the entire width/table/density result of this section from IMPLEMENTED/TEST-VERIFIED to **OWNER-APPROVED / LOCKED / REGRESSION-PROTECTED** — any future change to `--pf-desktop-content-width`, the authenticated app's outer/main width, Public Quote's width, or HE/EN width parity requires explicit new Owner authorization to reopen. No code change was needed to apply this lock (the width itself was not touched this task).

**Correction (same later task, live re-measurement)**: this section's own mobile gutter figure ("a clean `6px`") was itself re-measured with a more precisely-verified card-detection selector and found to actually be `15px` (`.dash-main-content`'s `6px` + `QuotesTab.jsx`'s panel `8px`, +~1px rounding) — matching the CSS exactly. The underlying CSS was **not changed** between the two measurements; this is a further correction of a measurement-script artifact (the same recurring pattern documented elsewhere in this file), not a code regression. The Owner separately confirmed "THE CURRENT MOBILE WIDTH UTILIZATION IS APPROVED AS THE BASE" in the same later task — this `15px` figure is the actual, accurate, approved baseline going forward.

## §60. Critical Signature Authorization Fix — PRODUCTION DEPLOYED / VERIFIED (added 2026-08-31, Signature Fix + Mobile Cleanup + Email Audit + Lock Final Width task; promoted to Production same day, Critical Signature Security Production Promotion task)

Following §58's forensic audit, the Owner authorized audit-reconfirmation, implementation, and TEST verification of a fix (still no Production action of any kind).

**Audit reconfirmation before touching anything**: `supabase db dump --linked` against `quotecode-test` confirmed the live `public_approve_quote` definition was byte-identical to the one captured in `20260830000001_capture_base_functions_triggers.sql` and to what §58 analyzed — no drift, safe to proceed from that exact known state.

**Business-account identification — reused, not invented**: `business_settings.user_id` (one row per business/user) is the exact same source of truth `is_admin()`/`is_super_admin()` already use for "is this uid a ProFlow account" checks. No new identity table was created.

**Fix design — deliberately broader than "equals this quote's owner"**: the RPC now rejects the call whenever `auth.uid() IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM business_settings WHERE user_id = auth.uid())` — i.e., any authenticated ProFlow business account at all, not merely the specific quote's own creator. A narrower `auth.uid() = quotes.user_id` check would still leave a different authenticated business account free to sign someone else's quote, indistinguishable from a genuine customer to that narrower check. Anonymous callers (`auth.uid() IS NULL`, the legitimate Public Quote flow) are completely unaffected — every other existing validation (payload shape/size, status/signature-empty precondition) is byte-identical to the previously-captured definition.

**Applied to TEST only**: new migration `supabase/migrations/20260831000000_fix_public_approve_quote_business_check.sql`, applied via `supabase db push` while the CLI was linked to `quotecode-test` (`ljfizgrdyzxddswcedwr`) — the CLI was relinked back to Production (`ixabnzhjeqevtbhdfswv`, `linked:true`) immediately afterward, per the standing session convention (§18.N). Production was never linked-to or mutated.

**Full regression matrix — all live-verified on TEST, both via direct RPC calls (Claude Lead, independent of any UI) and via the real PublicQuote.jsx/PublicQuoteEn.jsx signing flow**:
- Anonymous customer, direct RPC on an unsigned quote: **HTTP 204, PASS** (persisted correctly: `status=approved`, valid signature).
- Quote's own authenticated business owner, direct RPC on their own quote: **HTTP 403 `42501`, REJECTED, PASS** (quote confirmed unchanged afterward — still `draft`, unsigned; the rejection is a true no-op, not a partial mutation).
- A different authenticated ProFlow business account (a separate, unrelated TEST business), direct RPC on the first account's quote: **HTTP 403 `42501`, REJECTED, PASS** (target quote confirmed unchanged).
- Already-approved quote, anonymous re-approval attempt: **REJECTED, PASS** (pre-existing behavior, unaffected).
- Malformed signature payload, anonymous: **HTTP 400 `22023` "Invalid signature format", REJECTED, PASS** (pre-existing validation, unaffected).
- Post-approval immutability (owner attempts to `PATCH` the subject of the now-approved quote): **HTTP 403 `42501` "...content can no longer be modified", PASS** (pre-existing `guard_quote_immutability` trigger, unaffected by this fix).
- Genuine HE customer flow, full UI (login as owner in one session for a same-session manager-view check, then a separate anonymous/no-session context for the real signing): manager view (`quote.is_owner_viewing`) correctly showed "תצוגת מנהל: אזור החתימה מוצג ללקוח בלבד" with no canvas/approve button, **PASS**; the anonymous flow (draw signature on canvas, click "אשר וחתום") completed end-to-end with the real success banner ("הצעת מחיר זו אושרה ונחתמה בהצלחה!") rendered, **PASS**.
- Genuine EN customer flow, same two checks: manager view correctly showed "Admin View: Signature area is displayed to the client only" with no canvas/approve button, **PASS**; anonymous signing completed end-to-end with the real success banner, **PASS**.

**Manager/business view UX**: audited and found to already exist and already match the Owner's supplied historical reference exactly (`is_owner_viewing` → the "Admin View" notice) — nothing needed to be built or redesigned; only re-verified as still correct, unaffected by the RPC-level fix (a separate concern computed by a different code path).

**Forensic TEST artifact preserved, not cleaned up**: the throwaway quote forged in §58's own live reproduction (`4204f54d-519c-48dd-8910-99f2514516a8`) remains `approved`/signed, by design — the immutability trigger correctly prevents reverting it, and it was not force-reverted to preserve the audit evidence trail. It is a TEST-only, disposable-account artifact with no Production or real-customer impact.

**Permanent rule reaffirmed** (documented at §58, unchanged): CUSTOMER-ONLY ACTIONS MUST REMAIN CUSTOMER-ONLY — the authoritative enforcement is now genuinely at the persistence layer on TEST, not merely the UI, closing the exact gap §58 proved.

**Production promotion (same day, separate explicitly-authorized task, P0/CRITICAL)**: before promotion, Fresh Local State was established (`main` local/remote HEAD both `17ac4d3a...`, unchanged; the migration file re-read and confirmed byte-identical to the TEST-verified version) and the full security property was independently reconfirmed fresh on TEST immediately beforehand (anonymous succeeds, owner-direct-RPC rejected, different-business-direct-RPC rejected — all three re-run and re-passed, not merely cited from the prior task). A full Production schema dump was taken *before* any change and confirmed the live `public_approve_quote` was still exactly the vulnerable baseline (byte-identical to what §58 analyzed) — no drift. **Isolation**: all 11 other pending local migration files were temporarily moved out of `supabase/migrations/` (to a location outside the repo), a `supabase db push --linked --dry-run` against Production confirmed exactly one migration (`20260831000000`) would be applied, the real push was then run, and the other 11 files were immediately restored. **Ground-truth verification** (not just the CLI's own summary): a direct dump of the `supabase_migrations.schema_migrations` ledger showed exactly one recorded version, `20260831000000`, and nothing else; a full before/after Production schema diff showed the change contained to exactly the one function body plus its `COMMENT` — zero other schema/table/RLS/function drift anywhere. The live Production `public_approve_quote` was then dumped and confirmed byte-identical to the TEST-verified fix (business-account check present, all other validation/immutability behavior unchanged). **Application commit**: isolated via explicit single-file `git add` (never `-A`) — `b5583e59d4dab0b2c7741df8fdc1110f32b4d972` on `main`, exactly one file (`supabase/migrations/20260831000000_fix_public_approve_quote_business_check.sql`, +93/-0), independently confirmed via the GitHub API after push; no other carried-forward uncommitted work (docs, Mobile/UI changes, etc.) was included. Per the standing Vercel-auto-deploy rule (§17.E), this push does trigger a Vercel rebuild — the migration file itself is not part of the frontend bundle, so the rebuild redeploys the same frontend bytecode with no functional site change, but the deploy event itself is disclosed here transparently, not hidden. **No real Production customer signature or quote-approval mutation was performed for verification, per explicit instruction** — the security property on Production was established via live RPC definition read-back + migration ledger read-back + full schema diff + reconciliation against the fresh TEST regression matrix, not by manufacturing Production data.

## §61. Mobile Quote History Metadata Grid + Sort Control + Email Column Audit — IMPLEMENTED, TEST-VERIFIED, AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL (added 2026-08-31, same task)

**Mobile metadata layout — two live iterations, the first rejected by the Owner on live visual review**: iteration 1 used `flex + flexWrap` for the Client Type/Views/Client Name cluster — functionally reordered correctly, but the Owner's own live visual review found long Client Names could wrap the cluster and visually shift Client Type/Views between cards, which the Owner explicitly rejected as "unordered and visually inconsistent." **Iteration 2 (final)**: rebuilt as a genuine CSS Grid with fixed-pixel tracks for Client Type (`24px`) and Views (`32px`), a `1fr` track for Client Name (single-line, `overflow:hidden`/`ellipsis`, never wraps), and — after a live-measured secondary instability was found where a wide Amount value (e.g. `$5,625.00` vs `$100.00`) could still shift Client Type/Views position in the EN layout specifically (Amount and the metadata cluster were still separate flex siblings competing for space) — Amount was folded into the SAME grid as a fourth, fixed-pixel (`78px`) track, fully decoupling all four fields' positions from every other field's content length. Live-verified, both markets, all three required widths (360/390/412px), 8 quotes per combo with genuinely varying Client Name lengths (short/medium/very long): **Client Type position: 1 unique X-coordinate across all 8 rows, every combo. Views position: 1 unique X-coordinate, every combo. Client Name left boundary: 1 unique X-coordinate, every combo.** Long names visibly truncate with an ellipsis (RTL-correct: ellipsis renders at the start of the truncated Hebrew text) rather than wrapping or reflowing. HE visual order confirmed right-to-left: Client Type → Views → Client Name (→ Amount, outermost/left). EN visual order at this point: Client Name → Views → Client Type (→ Amount, outermost/right) — both exactly matched the Owner's specification **as written at the time**. Full Client Name access preserved via the same `title=` tooltip pattern already used for truncated text elsewhere in this exact file (Desktop's own Client Name/Description columns) — no new touch/interaction mechanism was invented for this.

**⚠️ CORRECTED (2026-08-31, Mobile-Only HE/EN Directional Mirroring Fix task, same day) — the EN order described immediately above was itself wrong and has been fixed.** The Owner identified that "Client Name → Views → Client Type" for EN was a literal sequence-reversal relative to HE, not a true directional mirror — see the new permanent rule at §63 for the corrected principle (semantic order preserved from each language's own inline-start edge) and the exact fix. EN Mobile now shows, from inline-start (left): **Client Type → Views → Client Name**, matching HE's own right-to-left order field-for-field. This paragraph is preserved for history; §63 is the current, authoritative statement of the ordering contract.

**Mobile Sort control — added mid-task by explicit Owner instruction, same task**: the Owner observed that Desktop's Quote History column sorting has no Mobile equivalent once the responsive layout switches to cards — a genuine capability regression, not merely a visual gap. A compact "מיון:"/"Sort:" control was added directly above the Mobile card list: a `<select>` listing the exact same 7 fields Desktop's clickable column headers already support (`# Order`/`id`, `Client Name`/`client`, `Client Type`/`clientType`, `Amount`/`total`, `Date`/`date`, `Status`/`status`, `Views`/`views` — Description is correctly excluded, since it is not sortable on Desktop either), plus a compact direction-toggle button (▲/▼). Both controls call the exact same `handleQuoteSort`/`quoteSortField`/`quoteSortDirection` props Desktop's `<th onClick>` headers already use — no parallel sorting engine, no new state. Live-verified: selecting a field re-sorts the Mobile card list correctly (confirmed via actual before/after card order, ascending by Amount: `$10→$10→$10→$10→$100...`); the direction button correctly reverses it (`$5,625→$250→$250→$250→$200...`); both HE and EN; the control fits within the viewport with zero horizontal overflow at 360/390/412px, both markets.

**New permanent rule (Owner-established, documented here per explicit instruction)**: **RESPONSIVE TRANSFORMATIONS MUST PRESERVE FUNCTIONAL CAPABILITY, NOT MERELY VISIBLE CONTENT.** If a Desktop capability (e.g., column sorting) is transformed into a different Mobile presentation, the underlying capability must remain available in that new presentation unless the Owner explicitly authorizes its removal — a Desktop sortable table becoming Mobile cards with equivalent sorting is a valid transformation; the same becoming Mobile cards with sorting silently absent is a regression. This sits alongside, and is a natural extension of, the existing §37 Hebrew RTL / English LTR UI Parity rule.
