**Email column audit — CONFIRMED REAL FUNCTION, PRESERVED, not removed**: traced `renderEmailDot(quote, emailStatus)` (shared by both Desktop and Mobile) to two real, distinct, non-dead data sources — `quote.email_bounced` (a genuine, persisted database column, cleared/set by the email-send flow and the `resend-email-webhook`, per the codebase's own commit history: "Detect bounced/invalid recipient emails and turn the quote email indicator red") renders a **red** dot that survives page reloads; `emailStatuses[quote.id]` (`Dashboard.jsx`'s own `useState({})`, set to `'success'`/`'failed'` immediately after a "Send Email" action) renders a **green**/red dot but is **ephemeral, in-memory, session-only state that resets on every page reload**. This fully explains the Owner's original observation ("rows appear to contain no useful value") — on a fresh page load, only genuinely-bounced quotes show anything under this column; a quote that was successfully emailed earlier in a *previous* session shows nothing, by design, since success isn't persisted. Since the column has a real, confirmed function (not dead/unused UI), it was **not removed**, per the task's own explicit "if it has a real function, do not remove it" instruction. It was already narrowed to a `36px` icon-only column in the prior task (§59) and needed no further width reduction — no conflict with the width/mobile goals requiring a STOP.

**Status**: per §54, the Mobile metadata grid and the Mobile Sort control are new code changes this task and are **IMPLEMENTED / TEST-VERIFIED / AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL** — not Owner-LOCKED. The Email column decision (preserved, unchanged) requires no approval gate since nothing was changed. §60's signature fix is a security correction, not a visual result, and is TEST-verified via the regression matrix above; it awaits the same Owner + ChatGPT review as the rest of this task before any Production consideration.

**Correction (same later task, Owner Visual QA)**: the Views track's original implementation conditionally hid its content entirely when `view_count` was `0` (`{quote.view_count > 0 && (...)}`) — the Owner correctly identified this as a violation of the fixed-column requirement itself ("a dedicated fixed Views column means the value must be represented on EVERY quote card, including zero. Zero is a valid value, not an absent value"). Fixed narrowly: the eye icon + count now always render (`{quote.view_count || 0}`), with no other geometry/position change. Live-verified, both markets, 360/390/412px, cards spanning `0/1/2/6/8` views: the Views track's X-position is identical across every row regardless of its displayed value — confirmed via direct text-content + bounding-box inspection, not just visual inspection. Still IMPLEMENTED/TEST-VERIFIED/AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL, per §54 — this correction does not itself grant LOCKED status.

## §62. P1 Full Reconciliation + Session Timeout Forensic Audit — AUDIT ONLY, NO IMPLEMENTATION (added 2026-08-31, P1 Full Reconciliation task)

**Scope note**: no document in this project's canonical continuity set uses a literal "P1" label on any backlog item. This audit treats "P1" as the open/audit-required items in the Master Product TODO (`PROFLOW_TODO.md` items 1-11, 15, 19-21, 22-25) that are not already fully COMPLETE/VERIFIED and are not among this task's own explicitly excluded categories (Admin, Item 28/30/31, Landing Page, Vercel/domain, new-feature work). The full item-by-item ledger is in this task's chat report (not duplicated here in full) — this section records only what is new/verified this task.

### Session Timeout — Forensic Findings

**No ProFlow application-level session-timeout mechanism exists anywhere in this codebase.** This is a strong, evidence-based negative finding, not an assumption:

- `src/shared/supabase.js` (the sole Supabase client instantiation) passes zero custom `auth` options — no `autoRefreshToken`, `persistSession`, `detectSessionInUrl`, or session-lifetime override of any kind. It uses supabase-js v2's pure platform defaults (`autoRefreshToken: true`, `persistSession: true`) — normal JWT background refresh, not a ProFlow-authored timeout.
- A repo-wide search for `lastActivity`, `idleTimer`, `inactivity`, `session-timeout`/`sessionTimeout` found matches only inside the six continuity documents (all references to the still-open TODO item 8 itself) — zero matches in `src/`.
- `package.json` has no idle/inactivity-detection dependency of any kind.
- `supabase.auth.signOut()` is called from exactly one place in the entire codebase: `Dashboard.jsx`'s `handleSignOut`, itself only reachable via the explicit user-clicked Sign Out button → `SignOutModal` confirm callback. No timer, no automatic path, no other caller exists anywhere.
- Every `onAuthStateChange` listener in the app (`App.jsx`, `AppLocal.jsx`, `AppGlobal.jsx`, `Dashboard.jsx`) reacts normally to `SIGNED_IN`/`SIGNED_OUT`/`TOKEN_REFRESHED`/`PASSWORD_RECOVERY` events by updating local React state — none contain custom timeout/expiry logic, none force a logout independent of an actual Supabase-emitted `SIGNED_OUT` event.
- Every `setTimeout`/`setInterval` found in `src/` was individually traced and is unrelated to auth: a hot-quote-carousel rotation (4s), an exchange-rate refetch (10min, two public calculator widgets), a generic 10-minute UI-refresh tick (`setLiveTick`, drives "recently active" client-list indicators and time-relative displays — confirmed it never touches `session` state or calls `signOut`), and date-math for the 14-day trial period (unrelated to session lifetime).

**Root cause of the Owner-observed timeout behavior: UNKNOWN, but NOT a ProFlow application timeout (ruled out with high confidence).** Two remaining, unruled-out candidates, both outside this repository's own code and therefore not confirmable or deniable from a source-code audit alone:
1. **Normal Supabase refresh-token lifecycle** (a genuine "Type B" per this task's own taxonomy) — if a background token refresh ever fails (network blip, a long-suspended/backgrounded browser tab throttling the scheduled refresh timer, a corrupted/cleared `localStorage` entry), supabase-js emits a real `SIGNED_OUT` event and the user is shown the login screen — indistinguishable, from the Owner's perspective, from "the app logged me out."
2. **A Supabase-project-level Auth session policy** (a genuine "Type D") — Supabase's Auth product supports project-level session settings (e.g., an absolute session lifetime / inactivity timeout) configurable only via the Supabase Dashboard's Auth settings, entirely outside this Git repository. This cannot be confirmed or denied by auditing application source code. **Not checked this task** — the Supabase CLI has no read command for this setting, and attempting a raw Management API call (which was not clearly established as safe/precedented in this project's history, and could return other sensitive Auth-provider configuration alongside it) was deliberately not attempted without further explicit authorization. **Recommended next step**: the Owner directly inspects Supabase Dashboard → Authentication → Sessions/Settings for both the Production and `quotecode-test` projects, or explicitly authorizes a separate, scoped, read-only Management API task to do so.

**LOCAL/TEST/PRODUCTION/HE/EN**: no application-controlled timeout exists in the source code that ships to any of these environments — the code is identical everywhere. Whether the unconfirmed platform-level setting (candidate 2 above) differs between the Production and TEST Supabase projects is itself unknown and was not checked.

**Multi-device impact**: not applicable today — there is no ProFlow-authored session policy of any kind to conflict with simultaneous multi-device logins. Supabase's default `persistSession`+`autoRefreshToken` behavior (unmodified) inherently supports independent simultaneous sessions per device, each with its own local refresh cycle.

**No code was modified to investigate this. No session was deliberately destroyed on TEST or Production to test this.**

### Behavioral Lock Rules — Verified Present and Coherent

Confirmed via direct read of canonical continuity (not merely trusting the latest Claude report): §54's addendum ("OWNER-APPROVED/LOCKED protects observable behavior, not merely a specific file or component... shared/parent/layout/refactor changes must regression-test affected LOCKED behavior even when the LOCKED component itself was not directly edited") and §61's responsive-capability rule ("responsive transformations must preserve functional capability, not merely visible content") are both present, intact, and internally consistent — the former is the general principle, the latter its first concrete documented instance.

### Uncommitted Application-File Inventory

Summarized here; full per-file classification is in this task's chat report. `main` currently carries only the isolated P0 signature-security commit (`b5583e5`) beyond the prior documentation baseline (`17ac4d3a`). Every other application file modified/added across this entire multi-task session remains uncommitted, exactly as before — this task did not commit, stage, or alter any of them. Nothing in this inventory is newly implemented by this task; it is a read-only snapshot of pre-existing state.

## §63. PERMANENT RULE — HE/EN Directional Symmetry (Inline-Start Contract) (added 2026-08-31, Mobile-Only HE/EN Directional Mirroring Fix task, Owner-established)

**The rule**: whenever a ProFlow UI structure has a defined semantic order in Hebrew/RTL, the corresponding English/LTR structure must preserve the **same semantic order**, measured from each language's own **inline-start edge** (right in RTL, left in LTR). RTL/LTR changes physical direction; it must never change semantic priority/order.

```
HE / RTL:  RIGHT (inline-start) → A → B → C
EN / LTR:  LEFT  (inline-start) → A → B → C
```

**NOT** a reversed sequence between languages (`HE: A→B→C` vs `EN: C→B→A`) — that is a **violation** of this rule, not a valid mirror, unless the Owner explicitly approves a different semantic order for a specific surface.

**The contract is measured as "1st/2nd/3rd from inline-start,"** never as hard-coded "left"/"right" — this is what prevents the exact misinterpretation this task corrected. For the Mobile Quote History card specifically, the permanent contract is:

- **Position 1 from inline-start**: Client Type
- **Position 2 from inline-start**: Views
- **Position 3 from inline-start**: Client Name
- (Amount remains at the opposite/outer edge, per the existing separately-approved layout — unaffected by this rule)

HE inline-start = right. EN inline-start = left. **Desktop Quote History is explicitly NOT part of this specific Mobile ordering contract** and remains separately LOCKED/unmodified by this rule.

**Implementation guidance**: the correct, robust way to satisfy this contract is a single, language-**non-conditional** DOM/element order, combined with the existing `dir="rtl"`/`dir="ltr"` attribute already present on the containing element — the browser's own RTL/LTR layout engine (flex or grid) then places the first element at each language's inline-start automatically. A language-**conditional** DOM order (`isHebrew ? [A,B,C] : [C,B,A]`) is very likely a symptom of this exact violation, not a correct mirror — it produces a literal sequence-reversal instead of true directional mirroring. This matches the pattern already used successfully elsewhere in `QuotesTab.jsx`'s own mobile card (the order#/date/status row) and is now also how the Client Type/Views/Client Name/Amount row is implemented.

**Relationship to existing rules**: this is a concrete, permanent instance of §54's "OWNER-APPROVED/LOCKED protects observable behavior" principle — the semantic order itself, not just its current implementation, is now the protected behavior. It is a sibling rule to §61's "responsive transformations must preserve functional capability" — both protect what a user actually experiences at a LOCKED surface, one across viewport-size transformations, this one across language/direction transformations.

**Root cause of why this was not caught earlier (Owner's explicit question)**: this was **not** a case of independent HE-agent-PASS / EN-agent-PASS masking a real defect through insufficient cross-checking, though that risk is real and addressed below. The direct root cause is that an **earlier explicit Owner instruction, in a prior task this session, literally specified the reversed-sequence version** ("HE: Client Type → Views → Client Name" / "EN, mirror this logically: Client Name → Views → Client Type") — that instruction was followed exactly, implemented, and TEST-verified as specified. Both HE and EN individually matched their own specification perfectly; the specification itself embedded the now-corrected misconception (treating "mirror" as "reverse the sequence" rather than "preserve the sequence from each side's own start"). No agent or verification step failed to catch a deviation from spec — the spec itself needed the Owner's own correction, which this task now implements. This is disclosed plainly, not attributed to a process failure that didn't occur.

**Permanent process strengthening (the risk that IS real and now addressed)**: even when a specification is correct, independent HE-PASS + EN-PASS verification is **not sufficient** to catch a directional-symmetry defect, because each side can correctly match its own half of an incorrect (or ambiguous) spec while the pair fails true mirroring. **New standing verification requirement**: for any shared HE/EN UI contract with a defined element order, verification must report each element's **position counted from inline-start** (not raw pixel coordinates, and not "PASS/FAIL" against each language's own spec in isolation) — HE's "Client Type = position 1 from inline-start" must be directly compared against EN's "Client Type = position ? from inline-start"; any mismatch is a FAIL regardless of whether each side individually renders correctly relative to its own written instructions. This task's own verification script implements exactly this: it reads each element's physical `left` coordinate, sorts by `direction`-aware inline-start ordering (RTL: highest-`left`-first; LTR: lowest-`left`-first), and compares the resulting semantic sequence between HE and EN directly — not visual inspection alone.

**Status**: the corrected Mobile ordering is **IMPLEMENTED / TEST-VERIFIED / AWAITING OWNER VISUAL APPROVAL** — not automatically Owner-LOCKED by virtue of being implemented and tested. The permanent **rule** documented in this section (§63 itself) is Owner-established and takes effect immediately as a standing constraint on all future work, independent of whether this specific Mobile Quote History correction has yet received final Owner visual approval.

## §64. Canonical Domain Consolidation + ChatGPT Landing-Page Access — AUDIT ONLY, NO PRODUCTION MUTATION (added 2026-08-31, Canonical Domain Consolidation task)

**1. Canonical Production domain**: `https://www.quotecodepro.com` — confirmed already the sole intended canonical domain throughout the entire codebase (canonical `<link>` tag, hreflang alternates, Open Graph/Twitter meta tags, `sitemap.xml` (36 references), `robots.txt`, every server-side email-generating Edge Function (`send-quote-email`, `send-trial-expiration-email`, `send-subscription-expiration-email`)) — zero code-level reference to `quotecode.vercel.app` anywhere except one explanatory comment.

**2. Current exact role of `quotecode.vercel.app`**: live-verified (fresh HTTP, no redirect-follow) to return a direct **200 OK** at `/`, `/he`, `/en` — **no redirect exists today**, it is a fully independent, content-serving second public origin, exactly matching the Owner's concern. **Proven to be serving the byte-identical current Production build** (not merely "looks the same"): response body length identical (3615 bytes), and — the strongest evidence — the content-hashed asset filenames (Vite content hashes) are **identical** between the two domains. Both already carry the canonical `<link>` tag pointing to `www.quotecodepro.com` even when served from the `.vercel.app` origin — the app's own canonical tag is already correct regardless of which domain serves it.

**3. Whether project-level removal is supported/safe**: **UNKNOWN with a disclosed limitation** — this session's Vercel CLI is not authenticated (`vercel whoami` returns "Logged out"), so the actual Vercel project Domains configuration could not be read directly; no Vercel API/Dashboard evidence was available. Based on general Vercel platform architecture (not verified this session, explicitly flagged as such): every Vercel project is automatically provisioned a project-name-based `.vercel.app` domain that mirrors Production, and this default alias is not typically a per-project toggle that can be fully de-provisioned while the project remains actively deployed — but this was **not confirmed** and should not be treated as verified fact. **Recommended next step for this specific question**: Owner directly inspects Vercel Dashboard Project Settings Domains section, or grants this session authenticated Vercel access for a separately-scoped follow-up read.

**4. Whether legacy-link compatibility favors redirect first**: **YES, a real (not hypothetical) reason was found.** Two genuine application-code dependencies on the *current browsing origin* were confirmed via direct source read: (a) password reset (Supabase's password-recovery call, four call sites across `App.jsx`, `AppLocal.jsx`, `AppGlobal.jsx`, `Dashboard.jsx`) all use the browser's own current origin dynamically, not a hardcoded canonical value; (b) business-user-generated Public Quote "Copy Link"/WhatsApp-share links (`Dashboard.jsx`, `QuotesTab.jsx`) also use the browser's own current origin dynamically. A user who requests a password reset, or a business owner who shares a quote link, while browsing via `quotecode.vercel.app` gets a link embedding that exact origin. Immediately de-provisioning the domain would break any such link already in flight or already shared with a real customer; a proper HTTP redirect (path+query preserved, and browsers natively preserve URL fragments across a redirect when the target does not specify its own) would instead gracefully forward these to canonical. **This is the real reason favoring redirect-first, per the task's own explicit instruction not to choose removal merely for aesthetic cleanliness if existing links would break.** By contrast, signup confirmation and every transactional email link (quote delivery, trial/subscription expiration) are already hardcoded to canonical and carry zero risk either way — confirmed via direct source read, including an existing code comment already documenting exactly this rationale for the signup case.

**5. Recommended final domain architecture**: **C — STAGED REDIRECT, THEN LATER REMOVAL** (not immediate removal, not permanent redirect-only). See this task's Claude report for the full decision matrix and rationale. **Not implemented — recommendation only.**

**6. Auth/session impact of redirecting (not removing) `quotecode.vercel.app`**: a same-project redirect at the HTTP/edge layer (before the app or Supabase Auth ever sees the request) has no session-storage or cross-origin implication — Supabase sessions are stored in the browser's own storage, scoped per-origin; a user who has an existing session under one origin would need to sign in again under the other after being redirected (an origin change always requires this, by browser design — not new risk introduced by consolidation). The **multi-device requirement is fully preserved either way**: nothing about this consolidation touches Supabase's refresh-token/session model, which already treats every browser/device as an independent session regardless of which domain is used to reach the app.

**7. ChatGPT landing-page access method (recommended, not yet granted)**: use the **canonical Production URLs directly** — the Hebrew and English Landing Pages at the canonical domain itself. Given item 2's proof that the canonical domain and `quotecode.vercel.app` currently serve the byte-identical build, the canonical URL is not merely "as good as" a `.vercel.app` deployment URL — it is definitionally the live Production experience, requires zero new infrastructure, zero TEST exposure, and does not depend on `quotecode.vercel.app` remaining available at all. **Caveat, disclosed plainly**: this reflects whatever is live at the moment it is visited — if a new deploy happens between this audit and ChatGPT's visit, the content could differ; no Vercel API access was available this session to obtain a permanently-pinned historical-deployment URL as an alternative.

**8. Relationship between the ChatGPT access URL and the Production deployment**: **SAME EXACT PRODUCTION DEPLOYMENT** — this is the canonical domain itself, not an alias or a copy; classification is definitional, not inferred from appearance.

**9. Demo-video access**: both HE and EN demo videos (referenced by `LandingLocal.jsx`/`LandingGlobal.jsx` as root-relative static assets) were live-verified this task via a real HTTP range request: `206 Partial Content`, `Content-Type: video/mp4`, `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, no authentication challenge, no expiring token — genuinely public static assets. Not modified.

**10. No secrets required**: confirmed — every URL recommended for ChatGPT access (both Landing pages, both videos) is a plain, unauthenticated, public HTTPS URL. Nothing in this audit required or exposed any secret value.

**11. Approved-status color**: remains deferred in `PROFLOW_TODO.md`, untouched, not discussed further this task, per explicit instruction.

**12. No Production domain mutation performed**: confirmed — this entire task was read-only HTTP verification, source-code search, and documentation. No Vercel configuration, no DNS, no Supabase Auth Site URL/redirect allow-list, and no `vercel.json` were changed. Awaiting explicit Owner + ChatGPT decision before any option above is implemented.

**Repo-wide domain-reference classification (Part E)** — every functional occurrence found this task:
- Signup-confirmation redirect (`Dashboard.jsx`) — **AUTH**, already hardcoded-safe to canonical.
- Trial/subscription-expiration email CTAs (both expiration Edge Functions) — **AUTH/EMAIL CTA**, already hardcoded-safe.
- Public Quote link built server-side in `send-quote-email` — **PUBLIC QUOTE**, already hardcoded-safe, with its own explicit anti-phishing rationale comment.
- Password-reset `redirectTo` (four call sites) — **AUTH**, origin-dependent — the real removal risk identified this task.
- Public Quote "Copy Link"/WhatsApp share-link builders (three call sites) — **PUBLIC QUOTE**, origin-dependent — the real removal risk identified this task.
- Canonical/hreflang/OG/Twitter tags (`index.html`) — **SEO**, already hardcoded-safe.
- `sitemap.xml`/`robots.txt` — **SEO**, already hardcoded-safe, zero vercel.app exposure.
- Support/info email addresses (Contact page, AI Chat, email functions) — **OTHER** (email addresses, not URLs, unaffected by hosting/domain consolidation).
- One explanatory code comment naming `quotecode.vercel.app` — **DOCUMENTATION**, not a functional reference.
- Demo-video relative paths — **ASSET**, resolve correctly under either domain today.

**STALE/LEGACY findings**: none found — every reference above is either already correctly hardcoded to canonical or a deliberate, currently-functional dynamic-origin use.

## §65. ChatGPT Landing-Page Access Resolution — AUDIT ONLY, NO PRODUCTION MUTATION (added 2026-08-31, ChatGPT Landing-Page Access Resolution task)

**Trigger**: ChatGPT directly attempted `www.quotecodepro.com` (root, `/he`, `/en`) from its own browsing environment and could not access it — this session's §64 recommendation to use the canonical domain directly is therefore superseded for the specific purpose of ChatGPT access (the canonical domain remains the correct Production domain in every other respect, unchanged).

**Exact current Production deployment identified — via GitHub, not Vercel credentials**: this session's Vercel CLI remains unauthenticated. Instead, GitHub's own Deployments API (queried against the `main` branch's current HEAD commit, `b5583e59d4dab0b2c7741df8fdc1110f32b4d972` — the same commit as the P0 signature-security fix) returned Vercel's own posted deployment record: environment `Production`, status `success`, with an `environment_url` of `https://quotecode-adp6tay5v-quote-code.vercel.app`. This is genuine Vercel-sourced evidence (Vercel's GitHub integration posts this itself at deploy time), obtained via a read-only GitHub API call — no Vercel token, login, or secret was used or exposed.

**That exact deployment-specific URL was tested live and found to be blocked by Vercel Deployment Protection (SSO)**: every path returned `302 Found` to `https://vercel.com/sso-api?...`, requiring Vercel account authentication to pass through. **Not a viable ChatGPT-access candidate** — reported as such rather than described as a success. No Deployment Protection setting was touched (per explicit instruction not to disable protection globally).

**Fallback candidate — `quotecode.vercel.app` itself (the project's existing public alias)**: re-confirmed live this task — still 200 OK, no redirect, no auth wall, still serving the byte-identical build as canonical (matching Vite content-hash asset filenames, re-verified fresh). This is the only currently-existing, unprotected, zero-config-change public URL available for a one-time ChatGPT inspection. Recommending its use for this narrow purpose does not itself decide or advance the separate, still-pending §64 domain-consolidation question (redirect/removal remains unimplemented) — but it does mean that question's timing should account for this use: redirecting or removing `quotecode.vercel.app` before ChatGPT's review is complete would remove the very access path being offered here.

**Fallback review package prepared** (regardless of whether the URL above works in ChatGPT's environment, since the true cause of ChatGPT's original access failure is unconfirmed and could recur): full-page HE/EN Desktop and Mobile screenshots plus the two genuine current demo-video files, all captured/downloaded from the confirmed-current-Production build this task. Exact file paths and sizes are recorded in this task's Claude report (not duplicated here, since scratchpad paths are session-local and not meaningful continuity content). Both video files verified as genuine valid MP4 (`ftyp isom` container, matching Production's exact byte sizes for `/proflow-demo.mp4` and `/proflow-demoEN.mp4`).

**No Production routing mutation performed.** `quotecode.vercel.app` and `www.quotecodepro.com` both remain exactly as they were before this task. The §64 recommendation (staged redirect, then possible later removal) is unchanged and still pending explicit Owner + ChatGPT authorization before any implementation.

## §66. Live Interactive ChatGPT Review Access — Architecture/Options Audit + Key Root-Cause Finding — NO IMPLEMENTATION (added 2026-08-31, Live Interactive ChatGPT Review Access task)

**Owner requirement**: ChatGPT must be able to review the Landing Pages live and interactively (scroll, follow navigation, inspect CTAs, replay for future changes) — a static screenshot/video package is fallback only, not the target end-state.

**KEY FINDING — likely true root cause of ChatGPT's fetch failure, directly reproduced, not merely theorized**: ProFlow's Landing Page is a 100% client-side-rendered React SPA with zero server-side rendering or prerendering anywhere in the stack — confirmed by reading the raw, unrendered HTML served by both `www.quotecodepro.com` and `quotecode.vercel.app`: the `<body>` contains only `<div id="root"></div>` and a minimal 2-3-line `<noscript>` fallback; every visible element of the actual Landing Page (hero, pricing, FAQ, features) is injected by `index-*.js` after it loads and executes in a real browser. The repository's only Edge Middleware (`middleware.ts`) does nothing but set a geolocation hint cookie on `/` — there is no prerendering-for-bots mechanism, no SSR, no SSG anywhere in this codebase. **This was independently reproduced this task** using this session's own AI-driven web-fetch tool (a different tool than ChatGPT's, but the same general category — content-retrieval-and-read, not full JS execution) against `www.quotecodepro.com/en`: it reported back "the page appears to be a loading shell or minimal content state," seeing only the page title, none of the actual marketing content — the same class of symptom the Owner described for ChatGPT. Separately, robots.txt (`Allow: /` for all user-agents, all paths), response headers, and User-Agent-varied `curl` requests were all checked and found completely clean — no WAF/bot-challenge, no UA-based blocking, no crawler restriction of any kind. This rules out deliberate blocking as the cause and strongly points to the missing-SSR/SSG architecture instead.

**Critical implication for every option below**: this root cause is **orthogonal to which Vercel host/domain/protection-setting serves the page** — `www.quotecodepro.com`, `quotecode.vercel.app`, a new preview deployment, and a hypothetical `review.quotecodepro.com` would all serve the **identical client-rendered bundle** and would therefore likely all fail the same way for a non-JS-executing fetch client, regardless of domain or authentication configuration. Solving the domain/protection dimension (what this task and the prior one were scoped to) does not by itself solve the rendering dimension. This is disclosed as the single most important finding of this task, going beyond the literal scope of the requested option matrix, because every option's real-world viability depends on it.

**Vercel-native mechanisms researched (current documentation, not assumed from memory)**: **Shareable Links** (a secure per-deployment link letting external viewers bypass Vercel Authentication without their own Vercel account — but the exact interaction pattern, e.g., a client-side redirect/cookie exchange, was not verified to be compatible with a non-browser fetch client). **Protection Bypass for Automation** (a secret bypass token passed as a header or `?x-vercel-protection-bypass=` query param — functional, but the token is itself a credential; pasting a URL containing it into a ChatGPT conversation would expose that credential outside ProFlow's control, in tension with "no secret required"). **Deployment Protection Exceptions** (marks a specific preview domain fully public, bypassing protection entirely for that domain — confirmed to require an Enterprise plan or the Advanced Deployment Protection add-on on Pro; **this project's actual plan tier was not verified this session**, since it requires Vercel Dashboard access this session does not have). **Preview Deployments are public by default** unless Deployment Protection is explicitly extended to the Preview environment — since the one deployment-specific URL tested (`quotecode-adp6tay5v-quote-code.vercel.app`, a Production-environment generated URL) was found SSO-protected, but the **current protection scope for the Preview environment specifically was not independently confirmed** (also requires Dashboard access).

**No implementation performed.** Full option matrix, recommended architecture, and staged (non-executed) implementation plan are recorded in this task's Claude report, not duplicated here in full — see there for the complete comparison.

**Recommendation summary**: (1) immediate, working today, zero further investigation needed — use the static review package already prepared in the prior task (screenshots + genuine demo-video files), since it fully sidesteps the rendering-dependency problem; (2) if genuine live interactivity is still required as a permanent capability, the real prerequisite is a **separate, dedicated future audit** of server-side-rendering/prerendering options for the Landing Page specifically (e.g., a build-time static-HTML snapshot, or bot-aware prerendering middleware) — not a Vercel domain/protection change — since no domain-only option is expected to solve the actual problem found here. This was not implemented and is recorded as a new candidate future item, not authorized work.

**Domain consolidation status unchanged**: `quotecode.vercel.app` remains exactly as it was; the staged-redirect recommendation (§64) remains pending and, per explicit instruction, sequenced to occur only after ChatGPT access is independently confirmed working — this task's own finding (that the rendering problem, not the domain, is likely the real blocker) is additional information for that sequencing decision, not a reason to change it.

## §67. Production Vercel Alias Redirect — IMPLEMENTED, LIVE, MOSTLY VERIFIED (one disclosed cache-propagation nuance) (added 2026-08-31, Production Vercel Alias Redirect task)

**`www.quotecodepro.com` is now, functionally as well as by intent, the sole ProFlow application origin. `quotecode.vercel.app` is a legacy-compatibility redirect only, not an independent second origin.**

**Implementation**: since this session has no authenticated Vercel Dashboard/API access (confirmed again fresh this task — `vercel whoami` → "Logged out"), the true Dashboard-configured domain-level redirect could not be used. Current Vercel documentation (looked up live this task, not assumed from memory) confirmed `vercel.json`'s `redirects` array supports a `has: [{type: "host", value: "..."}]` condition — a genuine Vercel edge-routing mechanism, evaluated before any application code runs, functionally equivalent to a Dashboard-configured domain redirect and explicitly distinct from an application-level React redirect. Added one rule: requests whose `Host` header is `quotecode.vercel.app` are redirected (`permanent: true` → HTTP 308) to the identical path and query on `https://www.quotecodepro.com`; requests to the canonical domain never match this rule (different host), so no redirect loop is possible by construction.

**Isolated commit**: `e03001745859ae6b81f162a4af5bdca3c95cac5a` on `main`, exactly one file (`vercel.json`, +10/-0), staged explicitly (never `-A`), pushed to `origin/main`. This does trigger a Vercel Production deploy (per the standing §17.E rule, disclosed) — confirmed completed via GitHub's own commit-status polling ("Deployment has completed") before any verification was attempted.

**Live verification — PASS for every tested path except the bare root**: `/he`, `/en`, `/public-quote/<id>`, `/en/public-quote/<id>?lang=en`, and `/dashboard` all correctly return `308` with the exact expected `Location`, and following the redirect lands on the correct final canonical URL with path **and** query string fully preserved (confirmed via `curl -L` showing `num_redirects:1` and the exact expected `url_effective`). The canonical domain (`www.quotecodepro.com` root/`/he`/`/en`) remains completely healthy and unaffected throughout.

**Disclosed anomaly, not hidden**: the bare root path (`https://quotecode.vercel.app/`) continued returning a stale, pre-redirect cached `200 OK` for several minutes after deployment completed (`X-Vercel-Cache: HIT`, `Age` growing monotonically past 270 seconds across repeated checks) — a CDN cache-propagation delay specific to that one already-cached URL, not a configuration defect. The redirect *rule* itself is proven correct and active by every other tested path (including the structurally-identical `/dashboard`), so this is assessed as a cache-serving nuance expected to self-resolve, not a failure of the implementation. No Vercel cache purge or additional deploy was attempted this task (no authenticated access to do so safely, and not clearly within this task's explicit authorization) — **the Owner should re-check the bare root path after some additional time, or trigger a cache purge/redeploy via the Vercel Dashboard if immediate resolution is desired.**

**No Supabase change was required or made.** Session/auth behavior is unaffected by this change beyond the expected, pre-existing origin-change requirement (a session tied to one origin doesn't transfer to another — inherent browser behavior, not new risk; see §64's prior analysis, unchanged). **No application code (`.jsx`/`.js`) was touched.** **No unrelated Vercel setting was changed** — the diff is exactly the one redirect rule, nothing else in `vercel.json` was modified.

**`quotecode.vercel.app` itself was NOT removed or de-provisioned** — per explicit instruction, this task implements the redirect only; removal remains optional and deferred, to be considered later only after legacy-link dependence has faded.

**Follow-up read-only re-check (same day, Vercel Root Redirect Final Read-Only Verification task)**: the bare root path was re-tested, fresh, with zero configuration/cache/deploy action taken (explicitly read-only). Still stale: identical `Etag` to the original pre-redirect response, `Age` at 653 seconds and climbing, `X-Vercel-Cache: HIT`, no `Location` header, `0` redirects when followed. `/he` and `/en` continue to redirect correctly; canonical root remains healthy. Per explicit instruction, no cache purge/redeploy/config change was attempted. **VERCEL DOMAIN CONSOLIDATION STATUS: PARTIALLY VERIFIED, NOT YET CLOSED** — every path redirects correctly except the bare root, which requires either further natural cache expiry or an Owner-initiated Vercel Dashboard cache purge/redeploy (outside this session's available access) to resolve.

## §68. Landing-Page Prerender/SSR Feasibility Audit — AUDIT ONLY, NO IMPLEMENTATION (added 2026-08-31, Landing-Page Prerender/SSR Feasibility Audit task)

**Scope**: exactly `/`, `/he`, `/en` (Landing Pages, assets, videos, public marketing SEO surface) — Dashboard, Public Quote, Admin, signature flow, and every other authenticated/dynamic surface explicitly out of scope and untouched.

**Confirmed current state**: `main.jsx` mounts via React 18's `createRoot(...).render(...)` — a pure client-render call, not `hydrateRoot`. This means the app does not attempt hydration at all today; it fully replaces whatever is in `#root` on every load. This is favorable for the recommended option below: adding prerendered static markup inside `#root` carries **no React hydration-mismatch risk**, since the client never tries to reconcile against it — it simply re-renders over it exactly as it already does today for real browsers (a real browser sees, at most, an imperceptible instant swap from prerendered markup to freshly client-rendered markup — same visual result either way).

**Confirmed the Landing components are already SSR-safe as written, with zero code changes required for this property specifically**: every `window.`/`document.`/`navigator.` reference in `LandingLocal.jsx`/`LandingGlobal.jsx` is inside a `useEffect` (confirmed via direct source read) — React never runs effects during server-side rendering, so `react-dom/server`'s `renderToString` can already render these components today without throwing. The Landing Pages have **zero live/dynamic data dependency** (no Supabase calls, no `fetch`, only local UI-interaction state for the billing-cycle toggle/FAQ-accordion/accessibility-panel) — all marketing copy, pricing, and FAQ text is embedded directly in the component source, meaning a build-time static snapshot can never silently go stale relative to a rebuild.

**React Router v7's own native "Pre-Rendering" feature was investigated and ruled out for this codebase specifically** (not generically) — confirmed via current official documentation: it is explicitly "Framework Mode only," unavailable in "Declarative Mode" (`<BrowserRouter>`/`<Routes>`/`<Route>`). This project's actual routing (confirmed via direct source read of `AppGlobal.jsx`/`AppLocal.jsx`/`App.jsx`) uses exactly Declarative Mode. Adopting React Router's native prerendering would require migrating the entire app's routing architecture to Framework Mode — a full-app change, not a Landing-only one, and explicitly out of this task's scope ("do NOT propose or begin a full ProFlow SSR migration").

**Third-party SPA-prerendering plugins were checked and found unsuitable**: `prerender-spa-plugin` (the historically common choice) is archived/unmaintained since 2023. `vite-plugin-prerender` has unconfirmed current maintenance. `vite-react-ssg` is actively maintained but its own documentation recommends React Router v7 projects use React Router's own native capability instead (which is itself disqualified above) — introducing it would add a maintained-but-narrower-fit dependency for no real benefit over the option below.

**Recommended architecture — a small, self-contained, official-pattern build step, not a third-party plugin**: Vite's own official SSR guide documents exactly this pattern as a first-class, framework-endorsed technique — a minimal Node "entry-server" script that imports only `LandingLocal`/`LandingGlobal` (not the full app/router), wraps each in `StaticRouter` (react-router-dom's own purpose-built component for exactly this: server-rendering at a fixed URL with no real browser navigation), calls `react-dom/server`'s `renderToString`, and writes the result into the existing `dist/index.html`/per-path HTML output for `/`, `/he`, `/en` only. Built via Vite's own `vite build --ssr <entry>` mode, run as an additional build step alongside (not replacing) the existing `vite build`. No new runtime dependency, no change to the Dashboard/Public-Quote/Admin routes, no change to `main.jsx`'s existing client-render behavior for real browsers.

**Full option matrix, priority-ranked recommendation, and staged (unexecuted) implementation plan are recorded in this task's Claude report — not duplicated here in full.**

**Status**: audit only. No code was written or changed. No dependency was installed. No build was run. No deploy occurred. Owner + ChatGPT authorization required before any implementation phase begins.

## §69. Landing-Page Prerender Local PoC — Phases 1-3, LOCAL/UNCOMMITTED ONLY (added 2026-08-31, Landing Page Prerender Local PoC task)

**Authorization**: Owner + ChatGPT explicitly authorized exactly Phase 1 (Local PoC), Phase 2 (Raw HTML verification), Phase 3 (Local visual regression) of the plan recommended in §68. Preview/TEST/Production deployment, Vercel/DNS/Supabase changes, and any application commit/push were explicitly NOT authorized and were not performed.

**Fresh Local State (confirmed before and after)**: `main` HEAD `e03001745859ae6b81f162a4af5bdca3c95cac5a`, identical locally and on `origin/main` before and after this task. Working tree unchanged except one new untracked file (`src/entry-server.jsx`) — every other untracked/modified file already existed before this task began (carried-forward prior work, untouched). Zero commits, zero pushes to any branch other than the standard §17.J continuity sync to `proflow-continuity`.

**Phase 1 — Local PoC, PASS**: created exactly one new file, `src/entry-server.jsx` — a small SSR entry that imports only `LandingLocal.jsx`/`LandingGlobal.jsx` (existing components, zero modifications), wraps them in react-router-dom's `StaticRouter` (confirmed exported from the main `react-router-dom` v7.18.2 package already in `package.json` — no new dependency needed), and calls `react-dom/server`'s `renderToString` (ships with the already-installed `react-dom`). Built via `npx vite build --ssr src/entry-server.jsx --outDir dist-ssr` (an additive Vite CLI invocation; `dist-ssr` is already listed in `.gitignore`). Ran successfully in Node for all three routes (`/`, `/he`, `/en`) with zero runtime errors — including confirming `AIChatWidget.jsx`'s module-scope `supabase.js` import (transitively pulled in by `LandingLocal`/`LandingGlobal`) does not throw when constructed in a plain Node/SSR context. No new third-party dependency was installed. No Landing component was modified.

**Phase 2 — Raw HTML verification, PASS**: assembled full static HTML files (one new scratchpad-only orchestration script, `assemble.mjs`, entirely outside the repo) by injecting each route's `renderToString` output into a copy of the repo's own real `dist/index.html` (produced by an ordinary `npm run build`), then read the three resulting files back with plain Node `fs.readFileSync` — a non-JS-executing read, matching exactly the ChatGPT/crawler access pattern this whole effort targets. Results: root(`/`, rendered as HE per an explicit PoC decision — see note below) 51,513 bytes total / 47,823 bytes of real prerendered markup inside `#root`; `/he` 51,515 bytes / 47,823 bytes; `/en` 49,624 bytes / 45,932 bytes. All three contain real, verified marketing copy — confirmed present: EN hero headline, "Plans &amp; Pricing" heading, "Frequently Asked Questions" heading, correct EN demo-video source (`proflow-demoEN.mp4`); HE hero headline, "מסלולים ומחירים" heading, "שאלות נפוצות" heading, correct HE demo-video source (`proflow-demo.mp4`). **JS EXECUTION REQUIRED FOR MARKETING TEXT: NO** — the stated success condition was met for all three routes.

**PoC decision requiring Owner attention before any real implementation**: which language the bare root path (`/`) prerenders as was not specified by the task; this PoC rendered it as HE. This is a disclosed, reversible PoC-only choice, not a proposal — a real implementation should make this an explicit, deliberate product decision rather than inheriting the PoC's default.

**Disclosed gap for a future real implementation (not a PoC failure)**: `<title>`/`<meta name="description">` remain the generic English default across all three generated files, because `setSeoMeta()` (which sets these) only runs in a client-side `useEffect` and is never invoked during `renderToString`. This PoC's assembly script manually corrected only `<html lang dir>` and `<link rel="canonical">` per route (confirmed correct per route), not `<title>`/description/hreflang. A real implementation should bake locale-correct `<title>`/`<meta description>` statically per route too, not only body content.

**Phase 3 — Local visual regression, PARTIAL (disclosed tooling limitation, not a code defect)**: this session's browser-automation tool (`browser-harness`) requires a pre-provisioned named daemon in this sandboxed environment (`BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON=1`, fail-closed) and none was available — `browser-harness --doctor` confirmed Chrome running but no active daemon/connection, and a direct command failed with "required daemon 'default' is not running." **No screenshots were taken and none are claimed.** As the deepest available substitute, ran the actual real built client bundle (the same `/assets/index-*.js` served in the real production build) against each prerendered file using `jsdom` (already an existing repo devDependency, zero new dependency) with `runScripts: 'dangerously'`, served from a local static file server (`serve.mjs`, scratchpad-only, not part of the repo). Findings, all via real code execution, not simulation: **for both `/he` and `/en`, the `#root` element's HTML was byte-for-byte identical before and after the real client (`createRoot`) took over** (45,842 bytes HE, 45,899 bytes EN, unchanged) — the strongest possible evidence that no visible flicker, layout jump, duplicate content, or blank interval can occur for these specific deterministic (no-data-fetching) components, since the client's own fresh render produces the exact same markup already visible. Zero JS console/runtime errors during real client boot. Exactly one `#root` element present (no duplication). `document.documentElement`'s `dir` attribute and the components' own top-level wrapper `dir` attribute both correctly read `rtl` for HE and `ltr` for EN, both before and after client boot. Correct per-locale video `<source>` present in final DOM. Pricing and FAQ sections present in final DOM for both locales. All three generated files reference the identical real built CSS/JS asset files as the actual production build (no styling was changed by this PoC). **Not verified this task** (requires live interactive browser automation, unavailable): pixel-level visual screenshots, actual video playback, mobile-viewport rendering, and interactive click-testing of the FAQ accordion/billing toggle/accessibility panel/CTA buttons/locale-switch links.

**SPA isolation confirmed**: Dashboard, Public Quote, Admin, and Auth routes are unaffected — `src/entry-server.jsx` is a new, standalone file not imported by `main.jsx`, any router file, or any other existing route; `git status` after this task shows zero existing files modified, exactly one new untracked file added.

**Quality gates**: `npm run test` — 70/70 tests passed. `npm run lint` — 0 errors relevant to source (the SSR build artifact `dist-ssr/entry-server.js`, a generated/gitignored bundle, was linted incidentally, flagged 2 pre-existing-pattern `no-empty` findings inside third-party-bundled code, and was deleted after use since it is fully regenerable from `src/entry-server.jsx`; excluding that generated artifact, lint reports 0 errors and only 6 pre-existing warnings in files this task never touched). `npm run build` (normal production build) — passed cleanly, unaffected by the new SSR entry file. The additive `vite build --ssr` invocation itself passed cleanly.

**Recommendation**: the Landing-only prerender architecture recommended in §68 remains recommended — this PoC found no blocker. Phase 4 (preview/TEST-safe deployment verification) was explicitly NOT authorized this task and was not attempted; the audit finds no technical blocker to proceeding to it once Owner + ChatGPT separately authorize that phase.

**Status**: local PoC only. `src/entry-server.jsx` remains present in the working tree, uncommitted, per explicit instruction ("Keep the PoC local/uncommitted"). No commit, no push, no deploy, no Preview/TEST/Production mutation, no Vercel/DNS/Supabase change occurred.

## §71. Browser Harness Restoration — Diagnosis + Verified Owner Setup Procedure (added 2026-08-31, Restore Safe Browser-Harness Environment task)

**Purpose**: diagnose why real-browser QA is blocked (§70) and produce an exact, source-verified (not guessed) Owner setup procedure to restore it — without starting, stopping, or configuring anything this task.

**Method**: read the installed `browser-harness` v0.1.10 Python package source directly (`daemon.py`, `admin.py`, `run.py` under `AppData/Roaming/uv/tools/browser-harness/Lib/site-packages/browser_harness/`) and the installed Claude skill (`~/.claude/skills/browser-harness/SKILL.md`), plus read-only inspection of `~/.config/browser-harness/runtime/` (pid/port files) and its log — no command that starts, stops, or reconfigures any process was run.

**Confirmed from source (not inferred)**:
- When `BU_CDP_URL` is set (it is, `http://127.0.0.1:9222`, confirmed set only in this session's own process environment — `[Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable(...,'User')`/`'Machine'` both return empty, `'Process'` returns `1` for `BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON` — meaning **a separately-opened PowerShell/CMD window the Owner runs themselves does NOT inherit `BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON=1`** and runs browser-harness in its normal, self-healing mode), `daemon.py`'s `get_ws_url()` **only polls that URL for 30 seconds and never launches Chrome itself** — confirmed by reading the function body directly; its own timeout error message (`daemon.py` line 251) is: *"is the dedicated automation Chrome running? Launch it with `--remote-debugging-port=<port> --user-data-dir=<dedicated dir>`"* — this is the tool's own authoritative, built-in instruction, not a guess.
- `admin.py`'s `ensure_daemon()` (used automatically whenever `BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON` is not `"1"`) is the actual daemon-bootstrap mechanism: it spawns `python -m browser_harness.daemon` as a background subprocess (confirmed via its literal `subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-m", "browser_harness.daemon"], ...)` call) — that spawned process then itself calls the same `get_ws_url()` above to attach to the already-running dedicated Chrome. **Chrome-launching behavior inside `ensure_daemon()` (`_launch_browser()`, the `chrome://inspect` auto-open, the macOS Allow-popup handling) is all explicitly gated behind `if local and ...`, where `local = _is_local_chrome_mode(env)` — none of it applies when `BU_CDP_URL` is set**, confirming Chrome is never auto-launched in this environment's configuration, only ever connected to.
- `admin.py`'s `require_existing_daemon()` (used automatically whenever `BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON=1`, i.e. always in this Claude Code session) does exactly two things by its own docstring and body: checks the existing daemon is alive, and does one CDP health-check call. Its own docstring states plainly: *"Require a healthy existing daemon without spawning or reconnecting. Trusted orchestrators use this after they provision a scoped CDP transport. Failing closed prevents a later CLI call from silently discovering a different local Chrome when that orchestrator-owned daemon dies."* It never spawns Chrome or the daemon subprocess under any condition.
- `~/.config/browser-harness/runtime/bu-default.pid` (`5244`) was confirmed stale — `Get-Process -Id 5244` returned nothing, i.e. that process no longer exists — matching `doctor --json`'s `"daemon": {"alive": false}`.
- `~/.config/browser-harness/tmp/bu-default.log`'s earliest surviving lines show a **previously-working** session: `connecting to ws://127.0.0.1:9222` then `listening on 127.0.0.1:49881 (name=default, remote=local)`, with later entries showing tabs attached both to `http://127.0.0.1:9222/json/version` and to a local ProFlow dev server (`http://localhost:5184/dashboard`) — confirming this exact `127.0.0.1:9222` dedicated-Chrome architecture was genuinely functional before, exactly as this task's own premise stated. The log contains **zero** evidence of the daemon ever spawning a `chrome.exe` process itself — consistent with the source-code finding above that Chrome must always be started externally.
- The installed Chrome executable was confirmed present at `C:\Program Files (x86)\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe` (`Test-Path` → `True`). The proposed dedicated profile directory `C:\Users\sales\ProFlow-BrowserHarness-Profile` was confirmed **not** to already exist (`Test-Path` → `False`) — free to use, no collision with any existing directory.
- `browser-harness --help`'s documented CLI surface contains no explicit "daemon start" subcommand (bootstrapping happens implicitly via any normal script invocation, as described above) and no explicit "stop" subcommand beyond `browser-harness --reload`, documented as *"stop the daemon so next call picks up code changes"* — confirmed by its own code comment in `admin.py`'s `stop_remote_daemon()`: *"`restart_daemon` is misnamed — it only stops the daemon... a follow-up `browser-harness` call would auto-spawn a fresh one via `ensure_daemon()`."*

**Recommended dedicated-Chrome launch command (for Owner review, not executed)**:
```
& "C:\Program Files (x86)\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe" --remote-debugging-port=9222 --remote-debugging-address=127.0.0.1 --user-data-dir="C:\Users\sales\ProFlow-BrowserHarness-Profile" --no-first-run --no-default-browser-check
```
`--remote-debugging-address=127.0.0.1` makes the loopback-only binding explicit (Chrome's own default when the flag is omitted, included here for defense-in-depth clarity, not because omitting it would expose it externally). A distinct `--user-data-dir` is a fully independent Chrome instance by Chrome's own long-standing, well-documented design — separate cookies/history/extensions/process tree — and does not read, lock, or interfere with the Owner's normal `Default`/`Profile 1` directories in any way; multiple concurrently-running Chrome instances under different `--user-data-dir` values is a standard, Chrome-supported configuration, not a workaround.

**Responsibility classification** (diagnosis only — nothing executed):
- **Launching the dedicated Chrome** (Step 1 below): recommended as **OWNER REQUIRED** for this first restoration (so the Owner can see and trust the isolated window appear); technically within Claude's tool capability to execute via Bash once explicitly authorized for routine future restarts, but not attempted this task per explicit instruction.
- **Verifying `http://127.0.0.1:9222/json/version`** (Step 2 below): **CLAUDE CAN EXECUTE** — a read-only GET request with zero side effects, of the same kind already used throughout this session's diagnostics.
- **Bootstrapping the daemon itself** (Step 3 below, a normal browser-harness invocation from a shell that does not carry `BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON=1`): classified as **OWNER REQUIRED, not Claude-executable even with future authorization** — doing this from within this session's own Bash tool would require stripping or overriding an environment flag that the orchestrator deliberately set on this session's process tree specifically to prevent it from self-provisioning a daemon; that is a bypass of the fail-closed design regardless of phrasing, and is treated as permanently out of bounds for this session, not merely deferred.
- **Re-running `browser-harness --doctor`** (Step 4 below): **CLAUDE CAN EXECUTE** — the same read-only diagnostic used throughout this and the prior task.

**Persistence recommendation**: worthwhile. A single Owner-facing `.bat`/`.ps1` launcher (double-click, e.g. "Start ProFlow Review Browser") that (a) launches the dedicated Chrome with the exact flags above if not already running, (b) polls `127.0.0.1:9222/json/version` until reachable, (c) pipes one bootstrap script into `browser-harness` from that same unrestricted shell, (d) prints the final `--doctor` status, would eliminate copy-paste risk and make this fully repeatable across sessions **without weakening fail-closed behavior in any way** — `BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON=1` remains scoped only to Claude Code's own session process tree regardless of whether such a launcher exists, since it is a process-local (not User/Machine-persisted) environment variable. **Not created this task, per explicit instruction** — evaluation only.

## §76. Permanent Rule — Browser Harness Tab Hygiene (added 2026-08-31, Desktop HE/EN Mirroring task, Owner mid-task instruction)

**The rule, effective immediately and permanently**: during Browser Harness QA, open only the minimum number of tabs/pages required for the current verification; reuse an existing QA tab/page for locale, viewport, and route changes whenever practical rather than opening a new tab per case; close temporary tabs once a test case is complete; and — critically — **perform mandatory browser cleanup at the end of every task**: close every tab/page Claude/Browser Harness opened for that task (and any clearly-stale automation tabs left by prior runs), never touching the Owner's own personal browser tabs or unrelated sessions. A task that finishes its QA but leaves unnecessary Claude-created tabs open has **not** completed browser cleanup — cleanup is part of task completion, not optional follow-up.

**Why**: the Owner's machine was under heavy load (fan running hard) from RAM consumption by dozens of accumulated Claude-created QA tabs across this session's prior tasks — confirmed directly this task: a `list_tabs()` check mid-task found **34 open tabs**, nearly all stale `localhost:4173`/`localhost:5186`/temporary-Vercel-preview tabs left over from prior tasks' Landing-prerender and Dashboard QA. 32 were closed immediately at the Owner's urgent request, consolidating to exactly the 1-2 tabs genuinely needed for the remainder of this task's work.

**Important secondary finding from this same cleanup**: reusing an old tab across a locale switch surfaced a real testing-methodology risk worth recording — `localhost:5186`'s two dashboard tabs share the same browser origin, so logging into a different TEST account on one tab silently changes the Supabase session for **every** tab on that origin (no per-tab isolation). Reusing a stale tab after switching accounts on another tab produces mixed/stale state (e.g., one tab briefly showed the INTL account's email with HE UI text and cached LOCAL-account row data simultaneously). The safe pattern going forward: when a task's QA requires switching between different TEST accounts, always do a full `localStorage.clear()` + fresh navigation + fresh login before measuring on ANY tab, never assume a previously-authenticated tab's session is still valid for its original account after any other same-origin tab has since logged in as someone else.

**Status**: permanent operational rule, in effect for all future Browser Harness sessions on this project.

## §77. Quote History Desktop HE/EN Client Type + Views Mirroring — IMPLEMENTED, TEST-VERIFIED, LOCAL/UNCOMMITTED (added 2026-08-31, Quote History Desktop Mirroring + Currency Invariant Documentation task)

**Owner-approved product contract**: on Desktop, `Client Type` must be the outermost/first metadata item from each locale's own inline-start edge (right in HE/RTL, left in EN/LTR), with `Views` immediately following it — before `#Order`, `Client Name`, and every other column. "This is intentional," per the Owner's own explicit framing — not a redesign of the rest of the table.

**Root cause, confirmed by source read before any code change**: the Desktop table (`QuotesTab.jsx`, `dir={tableDir}` on the `<table>` itself, `tableDir = isHebrew ? 'rtl' : 'ltr'`) already relies on native HTML table RTL/LTR column mirroring of a single, non-language-conditional DOM order (`<th>`/`<td>` children in source order) — the same established pattern already used for Mobile (§63). Before this fix, the DOM order was `[#Order, ClientName, ClientType, Description, Amount, Date, Status, Email, Actions]` — `ClientType` was the 3rd column from the inline-start edge (after `#Order` and `ClientName`), and `Views` was the 8th (far away, near `Actions`), not adjacent to `ClientType` at all. This did not satisfy the Owner's contract, which requires `ClientType` and `Views` to be the first two columns, contiguous, at the outermost inline-start position.

**Files changed**: `src/components/QuotesTab.jsx` (Desktop `<thead>` row and `<tbody>` row blocks only — the `<th>`/`<td>` for `ClientType` and `Views` were moved to the front of each row's DOM order; every other column's own styling/behavior/relative order among themselves is unchanged). New/updated: `src/components/QuotesTab.test.jsx` (4 new tests, see below).

**Implementation**: literally reordered the DOM — `ClientType` `<th>`/`<td>` now first, `Views` `<th>`/`<td>` now second, followed by `#Order`, `ClientName`, `Description`, `Amount`, `Date`, `Status`, the email-status icon column, and `Actions` in their prior relative order. No new conditional logic was added — the existing single, non-language-conditional DOM order plus the existing `dir={tableDir}` continues to handle RTL/LTR mirroring automatically, exactly matching the Mobile pattern's own established methodology. No typography/color/status-badge/business-logic/filtering/sorting/data-loading change.

**Desktop QA evidence — real DOM `getBoundingClientRect()` measurement on real TEST data, not inferred from screenshots alone**: logged into `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_*` (HE) and `PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_*` (EN) — same established credential pair, read from the gitignored `.env`, never printed. **HE Desktop**: `ClientType` `right` edge = 1427.5 (the outermost/maximum value among all header cells — confirmed rightmost), `Views` `right` edge = 1393.3125, which is exactly `ClientType`'s own `left` edge — i.e., the two columns are pixel-contiguous, `Views` immediately adjacent toward the left, exactly as specified. **EN Desktop**: `ClientType` `left` edge = 477.5 (the outermost/minimum value — confirmed leftmost, matching the exact same numeric distance from its container edge as HE's rightmost value, true directional mirror), `Views` `left` edge = 529.3125, exactly `ClientType`'s own `right` edge — contiguous, immediately adjacent toward the right. Both results were independently re-measured twice this task (once during initial QA, once during a full re-verification pass after a session cross-contamination was discovered and corrected — see §76) and were byte-identical both times.

**HE ORDER / EN ORDER**: both PASS, per the DOM evidence above.

**VIEWS ZERO**: PASS — `quote.view_count || 0` (unchanged, pre-existing code, not modified) correctly renders the literal digit `0` rather than hiding it; confirmed visually on both locales' real TEST data (multiple real rows with `0` views displayed, not blank) and via a new regression test asserting the same.

**LONG CLIENT NAME**: PASS — `Client Name`'s existing `maxWidth: 150px` + `overflow: hidden` + `textOverflow: ellipsis` + `title` (full-text-on-hover) truncation behavior is pre-existing and was not touched; since it occupies a fixed-width column independent of `ClientType`/`Views`' new position, a long name cannot displace or resize the `ClientType`/`Views` columns.

**Responsive regression — all 8 combinations PASS, all independently re-verified after correcting the tab cross-contamination described in §76**: HE/EN × Desktop (1920×1080), Mobile (390×844 @3x), Tablet Portrait (768×1024 @2x), Tablet Landscape (1024×768 @2x). Confirmed via the existing `isMobileView` breakpoint (`window.matchMedia('(max-width: 768px)')`, defined in `QuotesTab.jsx`, unmodified) that both Mobile and Tablet Portrait (exactly at the 768px inclusive boundary) correctly render the pre-existing, untouched Mobile card layout (§63's `{clientTypeEl}{viewsEl}{clientNameEl}{amountEl}` order, unaffected by this task); Tablet Landscape (1024px, above the breakpoint) correctly renders the Desktop table with the new mirrored order. **Testing-methodology note**: confirmed empirically that `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` does not always trigger the `resize`/`matchMedia`-`change` listeners that `isMobileView` depends on to recompute after the component has already mounted at a different viewport — this exact CDP-testing quirk is already documented in the codebase's own comments (added during an earlier session's Mobile QA). Worked around, as the codebase's own comment prescribes, by explicitly dispatching `window.dispatchEvent(new Event('resize'))` after every `setDeviceMetricsOverride` call; the very first (initial-mount) viewport of a freshly-loaded page does not require this, since `isMobileView`'s `useState` initializer reads `matchMedia(...).matches` synchronously at mount. Zero horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth <= clientWidth`) confirmed on all 8 combinations.

**Regression protection added**: `src/components/QuotesTab.test.jsx` — 4 new tests (`describe.each` over HE/EN), asserting (a) the first two `<thead>` header cells are `Client Type` then `Views` in that DOM order, and (b) the first two `<tbody>` first-row cells' rendered content is consistent with `ClientType` first / `Views` second, including the literal `0` value. These test DOM **source order** (which `jsdom` can assert reliably) rather than visual pixel position (which `jsdom` does not compute) — the real-browser `getBoundingClientRect()` evidence above is what proves the actual visual mirroring; the test protects the underlying DOM-order invariant that mirroring depends on, matching the same "narrow, non-brittle, source/structure-level guard, not a full visual re-proof" philosophy already established for the Hot Quote regression test (§74).

**Quality**: 76/76 total tests pass (72 prior + 4 new). Lint: 0 errors. Normal build: PASS.

**International Quote Currency Immutability — documented as a new permanent product invariant in `PROFLOW_TODO.md` item 33, NOT implemented, NOT audited/verified, no data mutated this task**, per explicit instruction: a quote's currency is fixed at creation time from the Business Profile's then-current currency and never changes retroactively when the Business Profile's currency later changes. Full worked example, applicable surfaces, and a future (not-yet-authorized) end-to-end TEST audit item are recorded there.

A read-only process inspection (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process`) confirmed **no `chrome.exe` process anywhere carries a `--remote-debugging-port` flag** — only the Owner's own personal Chrome and its helper processes are running. **Root cause**: the prior task's end-of-task tab cleanup (per the new §76 tab-hygiene rule) closed the *last* remaining tab in the dedicated automation Chrome; with zero windows/tabs left, that Chrome instance appears to have exited entirely. This session cannot restart it (`BH_REQUIRE_EXISTING_DAEMON=1` still applies, per §71's own responsibility classification — bootstrapping a fresh daemon/Chrome is Owner-required, not self-service). **Refinement to the §76 tab-hygiene rule, recorded here**: end-of-task cleanup should close every *extra* Claude-created tab but should stop short of closing the dedicated Chrome's *very last* tab/window, since a fully tab-less dedicated instance can self-terminate — leave one blank/idle tab open as a keep-alive, or close it only via a method confirmed not to kill the process.

**Given this, live visual/pixel QA for this task could not be performed.** Everything not dependent on the browser was completed: source-level audit, implementation, and non-browser quality gates. This is disclosed honestly below rather than fabricated.

**Audit of current state (source-level, since live UI was unavailable)**: re-read `QuotesTab.jsx` fresh at task start (not relying on the prior task's report). Confirmed the §77 Desktop mirroring fix (Client Type first, Views second, `dir={tableDir}`-based mirroring) was intact and uncommitted, exactly as the prior task left it. Confirmed the exact starting point for this task's own changes: no header content was centered (`textAlign` was edge-aligned per locale, or unset, on most columns), every sortable text column carried a decorative `lucide-react` icon (`Hash`/`Building2`/`AlignLeft`/`Banknote`/`Calendar`/`CircleDot`) whose only role was visual (the `onClick` sort handler is bound to the entire `<th>`, not the icon), the `Views` header showed both the eye icon and the word "צפיות"/"Views", the email-status header was already icon-only (`Mail`, 36px), and `#Order` had no explicit width (content/text-driven).

**Header centering — IMPLEMENTED**: every textual Desktop header (`#Order`, `Client Name`, `Description`, `Amount`, `Date`, `Status`, `Actions`) now uses `textAlign: 'center'` (previously edge-aligned per locale, or in `Actions`' case, opposite-edge-aligned). `Client Type`, `Views`, and the email-status header were already centered and remain so. Body/row data alignment was deliberately **not** changed — the Owner's instruction addressed header content specifically; centering only the headers while leaving row data at its existing edge alignment is a standard, intentional table convention, not an inconsistency.

**Decorative header icons removed**: `Hash` (#Order), `Building2` (Client Name header only — the `Building2` import itself is preserved because it is also used by `ClientTypeBadge`'s own icon logic, confirmed via `grep` before removal), `AlignLeft` (Description), `Banknote` (Amount), `Calendar` (Date), `CircleDot` (Status). All five were confirmed via `grep` to have no other usage in the file before their imports were removed entirely (not just their header usage). None of these participated in sorting, click behavior, tooltips, or accessibility — the sortable `<th>`'s own `onClick` covers the full cell regardless of icon presence, confirmed by reading the handler wiring before removing anything.

**Functional icons preserved**: `Eye` (Views — now the column's only visible content, per the Owner's explicit "icon-only" requirement below), `Mail` (email-status — already icon-only, unchanged in kind, only resized), `Building2`/`User` (inside `ClientTypeBadge`, the actual per-row client-type indicator, functionally load-bearing, untouched).

**Views column — compacted to icon-only**: header text ("צפיות"/"Views") removed; `Eye` icon retained as the column's sole visible header content, alongside the existing sort-direction arrow (▲/▼, shown only when actively sorted by views). The existing `title` tooltip (`'מיון לפי צפיות'`/`'Sort by views'`) is preserved unchanged; a new `aria-label` (`'צפיות'`/`'Views'`) was added since none existed before, per the Owner's explicit "preserve localized aria-label/title" instruction — there was no aria-label to preserve, so one was added to meet the underlying accessibility intent. Header width reduced from `46px` to `28px`; the row/body cell (which shows the actual `quote.view_count || 0` value plus its own separate `Eye` icon — unchanged, still always renders including the literal `0`) had its horizontal padding tightened to match (`6px`→`2px`) without touching its content or logic.

**Email delivery status — audited, semantics documented, logic untouched**: read `renderEmailDot` (existing, unmodified function) directly. Exact semantics:
- **RED**: two distinct underlying causes render identically — (a) `quote.email_bounced` is true (the recipient address doesn't exist / the message bounced, detected asynchronously by a `resend-email-webhook`, not by the immediate send result — stays red until a successful resend, per the function's own comment), or (b) `emailStatus` is truthy but not `'success'` (the immediate send attempt itself failed). Tooltip differs per cause ("כתובת המייל ששלחת אינה קיימת"/"The email address you sent does not exist" for bounced; "שליחת האימייל נכשלה"/"Email failed" for a failed send).
- **GREEN**: `emailStatus === 'success'` — the email was sent successfully.
- **BLANK** (no dot rendered at all): both `quote.email_bounced` is falsy and `emailStatus` is null/undefined — no email has been sent for this quote yet (no entry exists in the `emailStatuses` map for it).
No email-sending logic, backend, data field, or status-semantic was touched — only the header's own width (`36px`→`28px`) and padding were adjusted; `renderEmailDot` itself is byte-identical to before this task.

**#Order — compacted**: `Hash` icon removed, header centered, explicit `width: '72px'` added (previously unconstrained/content-driven). The column's real body values (`formatQuoteFallback(quote)`, e.g. `A100732`) are short (~7 characters); `whiteSpace: 'nowrap'` was added to the body `<td>` as a safety net so the value itself can never wrap, while the *header label* ("מספר הזמנה"/"# Order") may wrap to two lines if needed at this width — the Owner's own instruction distinguishes these ("without undesirable wrapping" was stated about the order-number *values*, not the header text). Since the table uses the browser default `table-layout: auto` (not `fixed`), `width` is a sizing hint the browser will still expand past if content genuinely demands it — this is a safety property, not a defect: no data can ever be clipped or hidden by this width value, worst case is a less-than-ideal column width on an unusually long value, which does not occur with this data's actual format.

**Description — the primary beneficiary of recovered width, per the Owner's explicit §9 instruction**: header `minWidth` increased `130px` → `190px`; the row/body cell's `maxWidth` (governing the existing, unmodified ellipsis-truncation + full-text-on-hover `title` behavior) increased `190px` → `260px`. Both remain **bounded**, not unlimited, exactly as instructed ("Description remains bounded/stable — not unlimited").

**Source-declared width changes (NOT live-measured — see blocker above)**:
| Column | Before (source) | After (source) |
|---|---|---|
| Views (header) | `46px` | `28px` (-18px) |
| Email (header) | `36px` | `28px` (-8px) |
| #Order (header) | unconstrained (content-driven, ~icon+text) | `72px` (explicit) |
| Description (header `minWidth`) | `130px` | `190px` (+60px) |
| Description (body `maxWidth`) | `190px` | `260px` (+70px) |

These are the exact source-code width *values/hints* changed, honestly labeled as such — they are **not** a substitute for the Owner's own requested live `getBoundingClientRect()` before/after measurement, which requires the dedicated Chrome to be restarted first. The task's own required "TOTAL WIDTH RECOVERED" / "DESCRIPTION WIDTH GAIN (px + %)" figures depend on live rendering (real font metrics, real icon/badge dimensions, `table-layout: auto`'s actual column distribution) and are **not reported as verified** — reporting a computed guess as if it were a real measurement would violate the task's own explicit "Do not report merely 'looks better'" standard in spirit, so this task reports the source-level intent honestly instead of fabricating a pixel figure.
