This section is kept for the historical record of the mid-course correction, not as the current state of the code.

## §89. Trial Bar Owner-Reference Correction — Exact Visual Match (added 2026-08-31, same day as §88, supersedes it)

**What changed the requirement**: while §88's control-row-merge implementation was being documented, the Owner sent an explicit, authoritative visual reference image and stated it overrides prior written instructions, not to be reinterpreted. The image shows the actual approved structure clearly: **Frame A → a narrow, separate horizontal "track" containing the trial bar (sliding within it) → the Quote History control-row (search/status/title/export) → Frame B (table header only) → data rows.** This directly contradicts §88's own instruction ("place it inside the control-row's gap") — the Owner reversed that specific direction once a concrete visual was available. Per the instruction, the image is authoritative over any earlier written description, including this session's own §88.

**Fix — second relocation in the same day**: reverted §88's control-row-merge entirely (removed the two conditionally-rendered trial-notice blocks from `QuotesTab.jsx`'s control row, restored it to a plain two-group `justifyContent:'space-between'` row, removed the now-unused trial-notice props/imports from `QuotesTab.jsx`). Re-added both Trial Notice variants (`dash-trial-slidebar`, `dash-trial-ticker-lane`) to `Dashboard.jsx`, this time as an ordinary block-level element in normal document flow — **not** `position:'absolute'` (the pre-session-original mechanism) and **not** a flex child of the control row (§88's mechanism) — placed directly after `dash-upper-section` (Frame A) closes and before `<QuotesTab>` renders, gated on `activeTab === 'main' && !showQuoteForm` to match the reference's main-dashboard-only context. Conditionally rendered only when `trialNoticeVisible` is true, so it occupies genuinely zero space when hidden (no marginBottom hack on a sibling element, unlike the pre-session-original mechanism) and, when active, its own real content-driven height pushes the control row and Frame B down naturally — exactly the image's structure. Text, icons, dismiss button, and the `trialSlideIn/Out/Center RTL/LTR` keyframe animations are byte-identical to the existing component — only the container/mount-location changed, for the second time this session.

**Real-browser proof, both markets** (per the standing Market Isolation Verification Discipline — used the actual HE/ILS Local TEST account and the actual EN/USD International TEST account, not a client-side language toggle on one account, since this app's HE/EN content is tied to the account's own configured market, confirmed live: a `localStorage.setItem('proflow_lang','en')` toggle on the Local/HE account changed only `dir`/`lang` attributes, not the rendered text — the correct method is the distinct `PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_EMAIL/PASSWORD` account):

| | Frame A top/bottom | Trial track top/bottom | Track left/right | Frame A left/right |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HE (Local TEST account, Desktop 1920px) | 16/262.39 | 278.39/306.39 | 462.5/1442.5 | 462.5/1442.5 |
| EN (International TEST account, Desktop 1920px) | 16/262.39 | 278.39/306.39 | 462.5/1442.5 | 462.5/1442.5 |

Byte-identical between markets (a symmetric full-width container — only the internal slide-direction keyframe differs, already verified unchanged from the existing component). The track's left/right bounds exactly match Frame A's own left/right — full card-width match, per the reference. Screenshots in both locales visually confirmed the structure matches the reference image: Frame A card, then the thin trial-bar track directly beneath it, then the white Quote History card (control row + Frame B + data rows) beginning below that.

**Dismiss + reclaim proof** (EN/International account, live-clicked, not simulated): before dismiss, control row `top:337.39/bottom:372.58`; immediately after clicking the trial bar's own X button, control row `top:293.39/bottom:328.58` — moved up by exactly the trial bar's own height + margin (44px), with `.dash-trial-ticker-lane` confirmed absent from the DOM afterward. `293.39` also independently confirmed as the row's natural resting position when no notice was ever shown at all (matches a separate measurement taken after the notice's own auto-hide timer expired) — proving zero residual gap in the idle state, both by dismiss-action and by natural timeout.

**Regression re-confirmed**: search input filtering (13 rows → 1 empty-state row on a non-matching query → 13 rows restored), no horizontal overflow at Desktop 1920px / Tablet Landscape 1024px / Tablet Portrait 768px / Mobile 390px (all re-tested live), Mobile card view renders cleanly with no orphaned gap from the relocated element. Table column geometry, typography, and Order/Amount/Date logic were not touched by this change (only the trial-notice container's mount location moved) — not re-audited from scratch this task since no table-geometry code path was edited, consistent with the Component Contract Trigger rule (re-audit is required when table geometry/typography/padding is itself touched, which did not happen here).

**Credential note, disclosed rather than silently worked around**: this task's live verification was initially blocked by a genuine root-cause finding, diagnosed read-only at the Owner's explicit request before any further action: the dev server on port 5186 runs via `vite --mode localtest`, which loads `.env.localtest.local` on top of `.env` — the two files point at two different Supabase projects (different URL/anon key). The `PROFLOW_TEST_USER1/USER2` credentials documented in `PROFLOW_HANDOFF.md` §18.T belong to the older project referenced by plain `.env`, not the project this dev server is actually wired to, so Supabase correctly rejected them (HTTP 400, confirmed via a `fetch` hook — not a rate-limit, not a stale password). The correct, current pair for this dev server is `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL_EMAIL/PASSWORD` (HE/ILS) and `PROFLOW_TEST_INTL_EMAIL/PASSWORD` (EN/USD), both already present in `.env`, added the same day as `.env.localtest.local`. `PROFLOW_HANDOFF.md` §18.T's credential-variable-name guidance is now stale for this dev server and should be updated (see §89.1 note below) so a future session does not repeat this same dead-end.

## §90. Trial Bar Zero-Layout-Shift Fix + Read-Only Trial/Subscription State Audit (2026-08-31)

**Layout-shift fix (Phase 1)**: the Owner's own §89 measurement (`control-row top: 337.39px` bar-visible vs `293.39px` bar-hidden, ~44px shift) was correctly reported as a genuine regression — the block-in-normal-flow trial bar consumed real document-flow height whenever mounted. Fix: `dash-upper-section` (Frame A) given `position:'relative'` (CSS-only, zero visual effect); both trial-notice variants (`dash-trial-slidebar`, `dash-trial-ticker-lane`) moved to be Frame A's own last children, each `position:'absolute'` (`top:'calc(100% + 2px)', left:0, right:0, zIndex:5`) — fully removed from document flow, so they structurally cannot move anything else regardless of mount state. `left:0/right:0` resolve against Frame A's own padding box (14px inset), matching the card's real content width rather than its outer border.

**Real-browser proof, all 4 required combinations** (HE Desktop, EN Desktop, HE Mobile, EN Mobile), each measured **visible → dismissed → visible again** (three real states via fresh-nav + live dismiss-click, not simulated): control-row/`<thead>`/first-data-row (desktop) and control-row/first-card (mobile, located structurally via its `border-radius:10px`/`1px` signature since mobile has no `<thead>`) were **byte-identical across all three states in every combination** — Desktop both locales: Frame A `16/262.39`, control-row `293.39/328.58`, `<thead>` `340.58/384.17`, first row `384.17/416.17`; Mobile both locales: Frame A `6/356.63`, control-row `381.63/458`, first card top `508`. Screenshots confirmed the bar renders correctly inside its track in both locales/breakpoints, mirrored RTL/LTR, with search/status/export/title genuinely unmoved.

**Table geometry re-check** (Date/Order/Amount/Views/Status/Actions header-vs-body center offsets, both locales, Desktop): all `0px` — no table-geometry code was touched this task, so this was a targeted spot-check, not a full re-audit, consistent with the Component Contract Trigger rule.

**Read-only Trial/Subscription audit (Phase 2)**: established (all confirmed from live code, not inferred) — **Trial source of truth**: `business_settings.trial_ends_at`. **Plan source of truth**: `business_settings.plan`. **No subscription source of truth exists at all** (no `subscription_status`/`cancel_at_period_end`/`subscription_end_date` field anywhere; `billing-checkout-stub` is an explicit, self-documented scaffold, `checkoutUrl` always null). Single resolver: `computeEffectivePlan()` in `src/utils/planEntitlements.js:33`, called from `Dashboard.jsx:477` and `SettingsTab.jsx` (already a genuine single source, not duplicated, for the trial/plan axis). Full truth table produced for Active-Trial/Expired/FREE/BASIC/PRO/Cancelled-still-entitled — BASIC and non-trial PRO marked AMBIGUOUS (entitlement math exists but no in-app path ever creates either state); Cancelled-still-entitled marked NOT IMPLEMENTED (cancellation via `PricingModal.jsx` is immediate and unconditional, no period-end concept exists anywhere).

**Status**: layout-shift genuinely fixed and proven 0px in all 4 combinations. `main` HEAD unchanged (`5f658f3`). **Owner visual approval remains PENDING** — not claimed. The audit surfaced enough open architecture questions that the Owner immediately commissioned the deeper system-wide audit documented next, §91.

## §91. Subscription/Billing/Entitlement System-Wide Audit + Two Owner-Approved Trial-Bar Corrections (2026-08-31)

**Part A — two small, owner-approved corrections, both implemented and verified**:
1. **Trial bar moved up**: within the existing absolute-positioning track (no new mechanism), `top` offset changed from `calc(100% + 2px)` to `calc(100% - 6px)` (8px net upward shift, both variants) — bar now sits slightly tucked under Frame A's bottom edge rather than nearly touching the control row. Re-verified 0px shift in all 4 combinations (HE/EN × Desktop/Mobile) after the change, identical methodology to §90 — unchanged.
2. **Expired-trial message corrected** per explicit Owner product decision: HE changed to `תקופת הניסיון הסתיימה, הועברת למסלול FREE` (was: "...אנא שדרג" — the "please upgrade" CTA suffix removed entirely, no replacement CTA added); EN changed to `Your trial has ended — you've been moved to the FREE plan.` (was: "...Please upgrade."). Only the `isTrialExpired` branch text changed — the `isExpiringSoon` branch ("expires in X days!") and all entitlement logic (`isTrialExpired`/`effectivePlan`) untouched. **Disclosed limitation**: no live TEST account currently has a genuinely past `trial_ends_at` (creating one is out of scope — NO trial-date changes), so this specific text was verified via source/lint/111-tests only, not a live screenshot of the expired variant.

**Part B — system-wide read-only audit, going well beyond §90's initial pass**. Key NEW findings not previously surfaced:

- **Live, reproducible Admin/Dashboard consistency bug found** (exactly the class of bug the Owner asked to hunt for): `UserDetailsModal.jsx:50` and `AdminUsersTab.jsx:302` both independently compute `isLifetime = isSuperAdminUser || trial_ends_at === null/undefined`, entirely bypassing `computeEffectivePlan()`. A user who self-cancels via `PricingModal.jsx` (`plan:'free', trial_ends_at:null`) is correctly shown as **FREE** by their own Dashboard (5/mo cap, edit/duplicate/WhatsApp/delete blocked) but incorrectly shown as **"PRO (Lifetime)"** with a Crown badge on **both** Admin surfaces — `trial_ends_at IS NULL` is used as a Lifetime proxy in Admin code but is also the self-cancel signature, and the two are indistinguishable from that one field alone. `AdminUsersTab.jsx` carries a comment showing the author was aware of `effectivePlan`'s expired-trial-→-FREE behavior and deliberately avoided a misleading "active paid" label — but didn't anticipate the self-cancel path producing the identical `trial_ends_at:null` shape.
- **DB-level enforcement confirmed genuinely solid** (a positive finding, not a gap): a `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger (`guard_business_settings_plan_trial`, captured live-production migration) rejects any `plan`/`trial_ends_at` change unless the caller is `super_admin` or the update is the exact self-cancel shape (`plan='free' AND trial_ends_at IS NULL` together) — `RAISE EXCEPTION ... ERRCODE 42501`. Two stacked RESTRICTIVE RLS INSERT policies mathematically confine signup INSERTs to exactly `free/null` or `pro/now+14d±2h` — **`plan='basic' is structurally unreachable at the database level, not merely absent from app code`**, confirmed by policy text, not inferred.
- **A second, independent bug found**: `send-subscription-expiration-email` (deployed, has a `config.toml` entry) queries `business_settings.subscription_ends_at`, `subscription_reminder_3d_sent`, `subscription_reminder_24h_sent` — **none of these columns exist** in the live-production schema capture (`20260830000000_capture_base_schema_tables.sql`). Its sibling `send-trial-expiration-email` correctly uses the real `trial_ends_at`/`trial_reminder_*_sent` columns, confirming this isn't a false alarm. Could not confirm from the repo alone whether anything actually invokes the broken function on a schedule (no Vercel `crons` entry found; a `pg_cron` job wouldn't appear in this repo).
- **Full entitlement matrix built** by inspecting every consumer, not assumed: monthly quote limits (5/20/∞, duplicated in TWO places already — `Dashboard.jsx:1729` display and `:2026` enforcement, a latent drift risk even before any new plan is added), edit/duplicate + WhatsApp/delete (`handleProtectedAction`), file attachments (30MB global cap, PRO-only, correctly reads `effectivePlan` via a `userPlan` prop — a third naming pattern for the same concept). Digital signature (`PublicQuote.jsx`) and Income/Revenue (`FinancesTab.jsx`) confirmed **never plan-gated anywhere** — available at every tier.
- **Extensibility assessed and marked FAIL**: 6+ hardcoded `if plan === 'x'` call sites found across `planEntitlements.js`, `Dashboard.jsx` (×2, display+enforcement), `AdminUsersTab.jsx` (×2), `UserDetailsModal.jsx` — a hypothetical 4th tier would require editing all of them, none reads from a shared table. Proposed a minimal, non-migrating fix: an in-code `planCatalog.js` object keyed by stable internal plan ID with per-tier metadata (limits/features/sellable/hidden), so a new tier becomes one catalog entry rather than N call-site edits; existing `plan` values become catalog keys unchanged, zero migration required.
- **Canonical model proposed** (proposal only, no schema/code change): four axes currently collapsed into two overloaded fields — PLAN/TIER, TRIAL STATUS, SUBSCRIPTION STATUS (doesn't exist today), EFFECTIVE ENTITLEMENT (always derived, never stored). `computeEffectivePlan()` recommended to stay exactly as-is (narrow, correct, DB-enforced) rather than be stretched — a new thin wrapper (conceptually `resolveAccountEntitlement()`) should compose it with a new subscription axis and the plan catalog, giving every consumer (Dashboard/Admin/QuoteForm/future badge) one shared structured return value instead of each deriving its own booleans (directly retiring the Admin bug found above).
- **8-persona canonical TEST matrix proposed** (HE×4 + EN×4: Active Trial / Trial Expired→FREE / Purchased BASIC / Purchased PRO, per market) — the BASIC/PRO-purchased personas (4 of 8) are explicitly flagged as **currently unbuildable even for TEST purposes**, since no path exists to set `plan:'basic'` for a non-super_admin caller by design (confirmed via the same RLS policy above) — genuinely blocked on either real billing or an explicit Owner-authorized super_admin TEST-grant path, not an oversight.
- **29-state lifecycle matrix** produced (signup through duplicate/out-of-order billing webhooks) marking each IMPLEMENTED/PARTIAL/AMBIGUOUS/NOT IMPLEMENTED against both current reality and the Owner's required future state (paid cancellation must retain entitlement until period end — confirmed NOT IMPLEMENTED today, cancellation is immediate and unconditional).

**Quality**: full suite 111/111 (unchanged), lint 0 errors, build succeeds. **No DB/Supabase/Auth/.env/trial-date/subscription mutation of any kind** — Part B was read-only throughout, exactly as scoped.

**Status**: Part A implemented and verified (0px shift maintained, message corrected). Part B is a proposal only — no subscription architecture, billing, Admin, or badge implementation has begun; five genuine Owner decisions remain open (upgrade/downgrade timing, past-due grace period length, whether admin Lifetime survives real billing, who backfills the historical Lifetime-vs-self-cancel classification, whether/when to authorize a TEST-grant path for BASIC/PRO personas). `main` HEAD unchanged (`5f658f3`). **Owner visual approval on Part A remains PENDING** — not claimed.

## §92. Complete Subscription Foundation Audit + Email Reminder Verification + Permanent Continuity Auto-Sync Rule (2026-08-31)

**Owner product decisions now CLOSED and incorporated into the architecture proposal (no implementation)**: (A) BASIC is a real sellable paid plan, not future-reserved (₪0/₪49/₪99 pricing unchanged, not touched this task). (B) Upgrade takes effect immediately on confirmed successful payment. (C) Downgrade does not remove already-paid entitlement — stays at the higher tier until `current_period_end`. (D) Cancellation = `CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END`, never an immediate downgrade — full current-tier entitlement preserved until period end. (E) Expired trial → FREE message finalized exactly as §91's Part A already implemented (`תקופת הניסיון הסתיימה, הועברת למסלול FREE` / `Your trial has ended — you've been moved to the FREE plan.`, no CTA). (F) Active Trial is a distinct lifecycle state from paid PRO — future badge must show `TRIAL`, never `PRO`, even though current entitlement is identical to PRO.

**Email Reminder Audit — complete, read-only, with genuinely new evidence not available in §91** (Supabase CLI, authenticated, used strictly read-only — `projects list`, `secrets list` [names/digests only, no raw values ever displayed], `functions list` — no mutation of any kind):

- **Critical, previously-undiscovered bug in `send-trial-expiration-email/index.ts:242`**: the batch-mode candidate loop contains `if ((biz.plan || 'free').toLowerCase() !== 'free') continue;` — skipping any candidate whose raw `plan` is not `'free'`. Every real trial user's raw `plan` is `'pro'` (written at signup alongside `trial_ends_at`, per the established signup shape) and stays `'pro'` for the entire trial (the raw column is never mutated mid-trial — only `computeEffectivePlan()` derives a `'free'` *effective* value once expired, without writing it back). **Consequence: this filter skips essentially every genuine trial user, meaning the 3-day/24h trial reminder emails cannot actually be sent to real trial signups today, independent of any scheduler/deployment question.** The only rows that could pass this filter are the narrow edge case of an already-FREE row that an admin manually re-grants a `trial_ends_at` to via `handleToggleLifetime` without changing `plan` — not the normal signup path at all.
- **`send-subscription-expiration-email/index.ts:230,246`** re-confirmed broken exactly as §91 found: `.select(...'subscription_ends_at','subscription_reminder_3d_sent','subscription_reminder_24h_sent'...)` references three columns absent from the live-production schema — this `.select()` will fail whenever the batch path executes. Its `plan !== 'basic' && plan !== 'pro'` filter is, by contrast, logically correct (unlike the trial function's inverted filter) — it's moot today only because no real BASIC/PRO subscription rows with a populated `subscription_ends_at` exist at all (no billing integration writes that field).
- **Deployment state, verified via `supabase functions list`, not inferred**: both `send-trial-expiration-email` (v9) and `send-subscription-expiration-email` (v8) are **ACTIVE and genuinely deployed to Production** (project `ixabnzhjeqevtbhdfswv`). **Neither function is deployed to the TEST project** (`ljfizgrdyzxddswcedwr` — its function list contains only `get-public-quote` and `send-quote-email`).
- **Secrets, verified via `supabase secrets list` (names/digests only, values never displayed or logged)**: Production has `CRON_SECRET`, `RESEND_API_KEY`, and `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` all configured — the runtime dependencies both email functions need are genuinely present in Production. The TEST project has none of these three (only the standard Supabase-managed keys) — meaning on TEST, both functions' batch path would always 401 (no `CRON_SECRET` to match) and their test-send path would 500 (`RESEND_API_KEY is not configured`).
- **Scheduler**: no Vercel `crons` entry exists in `vercel.json` (checked directly), no GitHub Actions workflow exists anywhere in the repo (`.github/` absent entirely). **Could not confirm or rule out a `pg_cron` job or a fully external scheduler** (e.g. a third-party cron service hitting the endpoint with the `x-cron-secret` header) — direct Postgres-level inspection of `cron.job` was not attempted, since it would require credentials/access beyond the read-only tooling used and was judged out of proportion for this audit; reported honestly as **UNKNOWN from available read-only evidence**, not asserted either way.
- **Duplicate protection**: both functions gate on a per-stage boolean flag (`*_reminder_3d_sent`/`*_reminder_24h_sent`), set immediately after a successful send, and the batch query's own `WHERE` clause excludes rows where both flags are already true — correct *by design* for sequential single-instance execution. **Disclosed gap**: no row-level lock/advisory-lock/idempotency key exists, so two *overlapping* concurrent invocations of the same batch endpoint (e.g. overlapping schedules, or manual+scheduled overlap) could theoretically both read the same not-yet-flagged row and send a duplicate before either `.update()` completes — a real but low-probability gap given no evidence of any overlapping-invocation mechanism actually existing.
- **HE/EN templates**: both functions branch `isHebrew = (biz.country || 'Local') !== 'International'` — the account's own stored market, not locale/display text — and both fully separate subject/HTML/plain-text per branch with the correct sender address (`support@quotecodepro.com` for HE, `info@quotecodepro.com` for EN) with no shared/mixed-language fallback path found. **PASS** for both.
- **Safe TEST-verification mechanism already exists, not invoked this task**: both functions support `mode:'test'`, callable either by a `super_admin`-authenticated caller or by one of two hardcoded bypass emails (`tahshitishi@gmail.com`, `minhatshay@gmail.com` — the real inboxes behind the `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL`/`PROFLOW_TEST_INTL` accounts) — this sends one genuine test email to a genuine inbox without touching the batch/cron path, real customer rows, or reminder flags. **Not exercised during this audit** (task explicitly forbade any email send) — documented as the mechanism a future, separately-authorized task should use for live verification.

**Email State Matrix** (12 states, current-reality only — no invented copy):

| State | Expected | Current (verified) |
|---|---|---|
| Active Trial, 4d left | none | PASS (correctly outside both windows) |
| Active Trial, 3d window | 3d reminder once | **BROKEN** — plan-filter bug above blocks real trial users |
| Active Trial, 24h window | 24h reminder once | **BROKEN** — same root cause |
| Expired Trial | none | PASS (correctly outside both windows; in-app expired message is a separate, working, already-verified mechanism from §91 Part A) |
| Paid BASIC/PRO, >3d left | none | Technically true, but only because the pipeline cannot function at all (see below) |
| Paid BASIC/PRO, 3d window | ~~reminder once~~ **SUPERSEDED, see §93** | **BLOCKED** — query fails on missing columns; no real qualifying row could exist regardless (no billing integration) |
| Paid BASIC/PRO, 24h window | ~~reminder once~~ **SUPERSEDED, see §93** | **BLOCKED** — same |
| CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END | analyzed, not implemented | No concept of this state exists in either function today. **Genuine open product-copy decision, not invented here**: should a cancelled-but-still-entitled user receive the existing "renew now" copy (illogical — they already declined to renew) or distinct copy (e.g. "your access ends on X unless you resubscribe")? Flagged for Owner decision, not resolved. |
| FREE | none | PASS — the subscription function's plan filter (`!=='basic' && !=='pro'`) correctly excludes FREE rows; this part of that function's logic is sound |

**⚠️ SUPERSEDED by explicit Owner product-policy decision, 2026-08-31 — see §93 below.** The "Paid BASIC/PRO, 3d/24h window → reminder once" expectation above was written assuming *every* recurring monthly `current_period_end` should trigger an expiration-style reminder, mirroring the trial-reminder pattern. **That assumption was wrong and is now corrected**: BASIC/PRO are recurring auto-renewing subscriptions — an ordinary monthly period boundary is a *renewal* event, not an expiration, and must **never** send an "ending soon" reminder. The 3-day/24-hour reminder pattern is now scoped **only** to `CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END` (where entitlement is genuinely, actually ending) — see §93 for the full corrected policy. This table's `BLOCKED` verdicts (the missing-columns bug, the non-existent billing integration) remain factually accurate regardless of the policy correction — the function is broken either way — but the "Expected" column above no longer reflects current product policy and must not be read as a live requirement.

**Admin FREE→"PRO (Lifetime)" bug**: re-confirmed present, deliberately left OPEN and unfixed per explicit task instruction — the future `resolveAccountEntitlement()` architecture (§91) is the intended, correct place to retire it, not a targeted patch now.

**Future extensibility, refined per two mid-task Owner addenda** (still proposal-only, no implementation):
1. *Plan catalog* (from §91) reconfirmed as the right shape now that BASIC is a closed, real, sellable decision — no change to the earlier recommendation, just removes its earlier "AMBIGUOUS/not reachable" framing for BASIC specifically (BASIC becomes a normal, first-class catalog entry once billing exists).
2. *Centralized/versionable pricing* (new): plan **identity** (the catalog's stable internal ID — `free`/`basic`/`pro`/future IDs) must stay completely decoupled from plan **pricing** — pricing becomes its own separate, versioned metadata (conceptually a `priceCatalog` keyed by `{planId, currency, billingCycle}` → `{amount, providerPriceId, effectiveFrom, sellable, hidden}`), never hardcoded per literal the way `PricingModal.jsx`'s current price ladder is today. This lets a future price change, a new currency, or a sale/hidden price ship as **new price-catalog rows**, never as edits to entitlement logic, badge logic, or the plan catalog itself — and lets an existing subscriber's already-purchased price stay grandfathered (their row references the specific price-catalog entry/provider-price-ID they actually bought, not "whatever BASIC costs today").

**Canonical TEST Persona Contract, reconfirmed from §91 with the closed decisions folded in**: the same 8 personas (HE×4 + EN×4: Active Trial / Trial Expired→FREE / Purchased BASIC / Purchased PRO) remain the required minimum, explicitly extensible (not yet built) to: BASIC cancel-at-period-end, PRO cancel-at-period-end, period-ended-after-cancellation, payment-failed/past-due, BASIC→PRO upgrade, PRO→BASIC downgrade, and billing-reconciliation/error states — matching the now-closed upgrade/downgrade/cancellation timing decisions exactly. The 4 BASIC/PRO-purchased personas remain explicitly flagged as currently unbuildable even for TEST purposes (same DB-level RLS restriction found in §91) — unchanged by this task, since no code/schema was touched. **Zero TEST users created or mutated this task.**

**Continuity Auto-Sync — new permanent rule, documented in full at §17.K above**: the Owner granted standing authorization for Claude to auto-push documentation-only `proflow-continuity` commits without a fresh per-task confirmation, strictly scoped to that branch/those files — explicitly does not extend to `main`, application code, TEST/DB/Supabase/Auth, or Production/deploy, all of which remain gated exactly as before. This task's own continuity commit was pushed under that new authorization, without an `AskUserQuestion` confirmation step, for the first time.

**Remaining genuinely open Owner decisions, re-evaluated in light of the now-closed items** (only what's left after A-F above were resolved): (1) payment-failed/past-due grace-period length before forced downgrade — still open, not addressed by any of the closed decisions. (2) Whether admin-granted Lifetime PRO remains a supported concept once real billing exists — still open. (3) Who backfills the historical "genuine Lifetime grant vs. self-cancelled FREE" classification list — still open, the codebase cannot reconstruct it after the fact regardless of the now-closed decisions. (4) Whether/when to authorize a super_admin TEST-grant path for the 4 blocked BASIC/PRO TEST personas — still open. (5) **New, surfaced by this task's email audit**: the CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END reminder-copy question above.

## §93. Subscription Email Policy Correction + Trial Countdown Audit + Billing Sandbox Requirement (2026-08-31)

**§92's email-policy framing corrected by explicit Owner decision** (documentation-only — no code touched, `send-subscription-expiration-email` remains exactly as broken/found in §91/§92, not fixed): §92 had implicitly assumed every recurring `current_period_end` should trigger a 3-day/24-hour "subscription ending" reminder, mirroring the trial-reminder pattern. **This was product-policy-wrong, now corrected**:

- **Auto-renewing BASIC/PRO (the normal case)**: **no** expiration reminder at 3 days or 24 hours before a monthly period end — that boundary is a renewal event, not an expiration, and must never read as one to the user.
- **`CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END`** (renewal disabled, entitlement genuinely ending): **yes** — 3-day and 24-hour reminders are appropriate here, since this is the one paid-subscription state where entitlement is actually, genuinely scheduled to end. Exact copy not finalized this task (none was pre-approved) — remains an open item, same as §92's CANCEL_AT_PERIOD_END finding.
- **Payment failed / past due**: **not** an expiration-reminder flow — requires its own, separate payment-failure communication policy, itself tied to the still-open grace-period-length decision (§91/§92, unchanged, still open).
- **Successful auto-renewal**: no "ending soon" reminder of any kind; invoices/receipts are a distinct billing concern from expiration reminders and must not be conflated with them.
- **FREE**: unchanged from §92 — no paid-subscription expiration reminder, confirmed already correct in the existing (broken-for-other-reasons) code.
- **Trial reminders (3-day/24-hour before trial end) remain fully mandatory, unchanged** — this correction is scoped to paid-subscription emails only. The plan-filter bug in `send-trial-expiration-email` found in §92 **remains open, not fixed**, per explicit instruction to leave it open until separately authorized.

§92's Email State Matrix (above) has been marked with a superseding note pointing here rather than silently rewritten, per this document's established practice of preserving the historical record while making the current authoritative reading unambiguous.

**Trial Countdown Audit (read-only, no trial-date mutation)** — real `PROFLOW_TEST_LOCAL` account data read via its own authenticated session (anon key + password grant, RLS-scoped to its own row, no service-role/admin access used):

- `created_at`: `2026-08-30T10:24:52.812Z`
- `trial_ends_at`: `2026-09-13T10:24:52.934Z`
- Trial length (`trial_ends_at − created_at`): exactly `14.0000` days — **confirms the standard 14-day signup grant, untouched, no reset/extension of any kind.**
- Actual remaining, measured at audit time (`now = 2026-08-31T07:47:49.293Z`): `13.1091` days = **13 days, 2 hours** (floor/remainder breakdown).
- **Dashboard formula** (`src/utils/planEntitlements.js:42`, inside `computeEffectivePlan()`): `trialDaysLeft = Math.ceil(diffTime / (1000*60*60*24))` → `Math.ceil(13.1091)` = **14** — matches exactly what the Owner observed ("14 days" shown roughly one day after signup).
- **Legacy Admin formula** (`src/components/AdminUsersTab.jsx:276`, `getRemainingTimeFormatted()`): `days = Math.floor(diffMs/msPerDay)`, `hours = Math.floor(remainder/msPerHour)` → **13 days, 2 hours** — exactly matches the Owner's cited Admin-precision example format.
- **TRIAL RESET/EXTENSION: NO.** **ROUNDING DIFFERENCE: YES** — this is a pure display-formula discrepancy (`Math.ceil` whole-day rounding vs. `Math.floor`+hour-remainder precision), not a data problem. `trial_ends_at` itself is correct and untouched.
- **Important nuance, not previously documented**: the `Math.ceil` rounding does **not** affect entitlement correctness — `isTrialExpired = trialDaysLeft <= 0` still flips true at exactly the real expiration instant regardless of the ceiling (the sign of `diffTime` is preserved through `Math.ceil`), and `isExpiringSoon`'s `<=5` threshold is a legitimate whole-day gating decision, not a display concern. **This is purely a user-facing display precision gap, not an access-control bug** — distinct in kind from the entitlement-affecting bugs found in §91/§92.

**Shared countdown helper recommendation** (not implemented, proposal only): extract Admin's existing, already-correct `getRemainingTimeFormatted()` day+hour logic into a shared utility (natural home: alongside `computeEffectivePlan()` in `src/utils/planEntitlements.js`, e.g. `formatTrialRemaining(trialEndsAt, now)` returning `{days, hours}`) that **both** Dashboard's and Admin's user-facing display text call — so both surfaces derive their displayed countdown from one shared calculation reading the same `trial_ends_at` timestamp, closing the display gap entirely. **`computeEffectivePlan()`'s own internal `trialDaysLeft` integer should NOT be touched** — it correctly drives `isTrialExpired`/`isExpiringSoon` gating logic today and changing its rounding behavior risks shifting those thresholds unintentionally; the new shared helper is for *display* only, layered alongside the existing resolver, not a replacement for it. Not implemented this task — proposal only, pending Owner authorization to change Dashboard's displayed text.

**Billing Sandbox Requirement — documented, not implemented**: the future payment-provider integration must support a genuine TEST/Sandbox environment permitting full end-to-end simulation with zero real charges — required scenarios: purchase BASIC, purchase PRO, successful renewal, payment failure, cancellation at period end, BASIC→PRO upgrade, PRO→BASIC downgrade, webhook/event replay, duplicate-event idempotency, billing reconciliation. **Explicit constraint**: Sandbox must simulate the real configured plan price (e.g. a Sandbox-mode ₪49/₪99 charge that never actually settles), **not** a special hidden Production ₪0 plan — a zero-price Production tier must never become the de facto test strategy, since it would leave the real price/checkout/webhook path permanently unexercised. The Sandbox flow must exercise the full chain end-to-end: Checkout → provider event/webhook → canonical subscription state → entitlement resolver → Dashboard badge → Admin state → permissions/limits → reminders/emails — matching the canonical-resolver architecture already proposed in §91/§92, so Sandbox testing naturally validates the same single code path real Production traffic uses, not a parallel/divergent test-only path.

**Quality**: zero application code touched this task (documentation/audit only), so full suite/lint/build were not re-run — nothing to regress. **Zero DB/Supabase/Auth mutation, zero trial-date mutation, zero email sent, zero application commit/push.** `main` HEAD unchanged (`5f658f3`), `origin/main` unchanged (`e030017`). **Owner visual approval on §91 Part A remains PENDING** — unaffected, unrelated to this task.

**Status disclosure, stated plainly rather than overclaimed**: this is a MAXIMUM-effort, 24-section audit task. The portion delivered so far — fresh local state, complete Admin surface inventory, complete data-flow audit, canonical state consistency re-trace, and the untrustworthy-fields list — is genuinely complete and evidence-backed (every claim below was verified by reading the actual current source, not recalled from memory). **The remaining proposal sections** (User Table Gap Analysis as a formal classification, User Detail View spec, Plan/Trial/Subscription Admin Model, Badge/Icon Model, Pricing Admin Model, Billing Visibility Model, TEST Persona Admin Support proposal, a formal Admin Action Safety Matrix table, Admin Audit Log proposal, full HE/EN/Market writeup, full Responsive/UX writeup, Visual Design proposal, Keep/Lock list, P0–P3 Gap Map, Admin V2 Information Architecture, Implementation Phasing, Regression Strategy, and Open Owner Decisions) were **not completed as formal written sections** before this continuity checkpoint was requested — recorded here as explicitly outstanding, not silently dropped, so a future session (or the remainder of this one) picks up from real evidence already gathered, not from scratch.

Auth: tab visibility is client-side (`isSuperAdmin`), but every actual mutation routes through an Edge Function that independently re-verifies `super_admin` server-side from the caller's own JWT — the tab-visibility gate is convenience, not the real security boundary. **Already uses the shared `LIGHT` theme correctly.**

## §95. P1 Modular Business/Quote Pricing Architecture — Continuity Reconciliation (2026-08-31, documentation/continuity only, no code/DB/TEST/Auth change)

**Search finding, stated first since it explains why this reconciliation was needed**: the requested architecture was **not missing** — it already exists, thoroughly, at `PROFLOW_TODO.md` items **30, 30.A, 30.B, 30.C** ("Structured Quote Items / Industry / Measurement / Pricing Engine"), added across three prior 2026-08-30 tasks. The actual gap was that this content had **no real presence in either `PROFLOW_PROJECT_CONTEXT.md` or `PROFLOW_ARCHITECTURE.md`** — this file had exactly one terse pointer sentence (§52, buried inside an unrelated "Six TEST Personas BLOCKED" entry, saying only "see `PROFLOW_TODO.md` item 30.C... not duplicated here"), and `PROFLOW_ARCHITECTURE.md` had **zero** mentions of it at all, confirmed by direct search of both files. That is almost certainly why a canonical-continuity search centered on this file or `PROFLOW_ARCHITECTURE.md` failed to surface it reliably — the authoritative content was real and detailed, just effectively invisible outside `PROFLOW_TODO.md` itself. This section and a new `PROFLOW_ARCHITECTURE.md` §14.C close that specific gap — **`PROFLOW_TODO.md` item 30 (with 30.A/B/C/D) remains the one authoritative, detailed definition; this is a substantive cross-reference, not a duplicate.**

**Priority, declared this task**: the Owner has designated the *architecture* behind item 30 as **P1 — a Full-LIVE readiness gate**. This does not mean every conceivable pricing/measurement formula must exist before LIVE — it means the override hierarchy, mixed-pricing capability, snapshot/versioning discipline, and extensibility model must be correctly established before LIVE, so ProFlow doesn't launch with a rigid design that later forces a breaking rewrite. Specific future pricing/measurement *methods* remain a genuinely separate, post-LIVE-addable category — see `PROFLOW_TODO.md` §30's new priority paragraph for the full distinction.

**Core principle (summarized, full detail at item 30/30.B)**: **industry/business-type ≠ pricing model.** A business category may supply presets — recommended defaults, terminology, fields, measurement/pricing methods — but must never hardcode or permanently lock how a quote item is calculated. Override hierarchy: `Industry/Business preset → Business defaults → Catalog/Item defaults → Quote-item pricing method → Explicit user override` — each level supplies a *default*; the item itself always has final say. A single quote must be architecturally capable of mixing pricing methods across its items (fixed price, quantity×unit, time×rate, length×rate, area×rate, weight×rate, and future extensible methods) — already illustrated at item 30.B with the Owner's own three-item carpentry example.

**Extensibility (made explicit this task, extends item 30's existing "presets are recommendations, never locks" language)**: must **not** be built as a growing `if (businessType === X) ... else if (businessType === Y)`-style chain — business type selects presets, calculation *capability* comes from an extensible model every type shares, same anti-pattern discipline already applied to the plan/tier catalog proposed for Admin (§92/§94.1: a new plan is a catalog entry, never a new code branch).

**Historical quote integrity**: already fully specified at item 30 (line-level detail: original entered value/unit/pricing-method/rate/quantity/calculated-value/currency/tax-context all snapshotted at save time; a later change to business/catalog/industry defaults must never rewrite an already-saved quote's items; unit conversions require an explicit, auditable conversion architecture, never an implicit display-time rewrite — the Owner's own `36 in` must-never-silently-become `91.44 cm` example). **Not re-invented here** — this section only confirms the existing specification already covers exactly what this reconciliation task asked to verify.

**HE/EN/Market separation**: already specified at item 30/30.A ("units must never be tied to language, market, or currency"; AI Chat "must never... assume Metric for International, assume Imperial for Local, or couple currency and units incorrectly"). **Generalized this task**: language, currency, industry/business-type, measurement method, and pricing method are **five independent dimensions** — none may be silently coupled to another without an explicit, separately-approved product rule, applying the same discipline as the permanent Local/International market-separation rule (§37) to this future engine specifically.

**Admin V2 compatibility, reviewed against the just-completed Maximum Admin Audit (§94/§94.1)**: **no conflict found.** The Admin V2 proposal and its recommended first implementation step (canonical resolver + plan catalog for plan/tier/trial/subscription entitlement) are scoped to a genuinely separate concern from item 30's quote-item pricing/measurement engine — neither blocks the other, and the Admin audit's own recommended next step remains independently actionable. What Admin will eventually need to expose about item 30 (business preset/type, defaults, per-item calculation method, historical snapshot data) is recorded as compatibility awareness only at the new `PROFLOW_TODO.md` §30.D — not scoped, not designed, not authorized for implementation. **Bonus cross-reference surfaced along the way**: the Admin audit's independent finding that the Role/הרשאה column is currently zero-information (§94.1) reinforces the Owner's own pre-existing item 29 Part A (already recording the same removal); the Admin audit's Badge/Icon Model proposal is the same idea already deferred at item 29 Part B, now given concrete shape. Neither item 29 nor the completed Admin audit (§94/§94.1) was reopened, restarted, or changed by this task.

**Status**: documentation/reconciliation only. ## §97. Overnight Maximum Safe Work Package — Admin V2 Foundation, Phase 1 IMPLEMENTED LOCALLY (2026-08-31)

**Scope discipline, stated first**: this task authorized ONLY the canonical Plan/Trial entitlement foundation described by the completed Maximum Admin Audit (§94/§94.1). No modular-pricing implementation (item 30 stays documentation-only, unchanged), no billing provider work, no visual redesign, no new TEST-persona DB mutation beyond what was already authorized. Every implementation decision below traces to an already-approved product rule — no new commercial rules were invented.

### A/B — Canonical Plan Catalog + Resolver, IMPLEMENTED

**New file `src/utils/planCatalog.js`**: `PLAN_CATALOG` keyed by stable id (`free`/`basic`/`pro`) — each entry carries `displayLabel.{he,en}`, `badge.{icon,colorToken,bgTint}` (color as a **token name string**, e.g. `'violetLight'`, not a hex value — keeps the catalog decoupled from any one theme, since `AdminUsersTab.jsx` uses the `LIGHT` theme and other files use the dark `NEON` theme), `monthlyQuoteLimit`, `editDuplicate`, `whatsappDelete`, `attachments`, `rank`, `sellable`, `hidden`. All numeric/boolean values are the exact already-approved ones (5/20/∞ quotes; edit-duplicate at BASIC+; WhatsApp/delete/attachments at PRO only) — nothing new invented. `TRIAL` is deliberately **not** a catalog entry — a separate `BADGE_STATE_META.TRIAL` exists for the *display* override only, per the explicit instruction that trial is a lifecycle state, not a purchasable plan. `getPlanDefinition(id)` fails safe to the `free` entry for any unknown id (defensive, not a guess — mirrors `computeEffectivePlan()`'s own existing fallback).

**New file `src/utils/accountEntitlement.js`**: exports `resolveAccountEntitlement({ plan, trialEndsAt, role, now })`, composing the **existing, completely unchanged** `computeEffectivePlan()` (`planEntitlements.js` — its internal trial-day math was not touched, since it already correctly drives `isTrialExpired`/`isExpiringSoon` today) with the new catalog. Returns `{ tier, rawPlan, isKnownPlan, isSuperAdmin, isLifetime, trialStatus, trialDaysLeft, isTrialExpired, badgeState, entitlement }`.

**The bug fix, precisely**: `isLifetime` is now `!isSuperAdmin && (trialEndsAt is null/undefined) && rawPlan !== 'free'` — never `trialEndsAt === null` alone. That third condition is the entire fix: it's the exact thing that distinguishes a genuine admin-granted Lifetime PRO/BASIC account (`plan` stays whatever it was, only `trial_ends_at` gets nulled by `handleToggleLifetime`) from a self-cancelled FREE account (`PricingModal.jsx` always pairs `plan:'free'` with `trial_ends_at:null`). This is a **broader, more complete fix than the narrow null-check alone** — `tier` (the field every consumer should actually display) was already correctly resolved by the untouched `computeEffectivePlan()` for every case, including expired-trial-without-self-cancel (`plan` still `'pro'`, `trial_ends_at` a real past date) — the bug was specifically that Admin displayed `rawPlan`/an ad-hoc Lifetime guess instead of this already-correct resolved value. Fixing Admin to read `tier` fixes the *whole* class of "raw plan shown instead of effective plan," not only the null-specific manifestation.

`trialStatus` (`'none'|'active'|'expiringSoon'|'expired'`) is deliberately **forced to `'none'` whenever `rawPlan === 'basic'`** — BASIC's tier is never trial-derived (per `computeEffectivePlan()`'s own independent basic-branch), so a stray/legacy `trial_ends_at` value on a BASIC row must never produce a TRIAL badge. `badgeState` is `'TRIAL'` only during `active`/`expiringSoon`, otherwise `tier.toUpperCase()` — kept as a field distinct from `tier` itself, matching the badge-model proposal already recorded at §94.1 (Lifetime as an overlay, not a tier value).

### C — Confirmed Admin bug: FIXED

`src/components/UserDetailsModal.jsx` and `src/components/AdminUsersTab.jsx`'s `getAccountDerived()` both now call `resolveAccountEntitlement()` instead of their own inline `trial_ends_at===null`-based guess. **The returned shape from `getAccountDerived()` was deliberately kept identical** (`isSuperAdminUser, isLifetime, rawPlan, planValue, isGrantedLifetimePro, ...`) — zero JSX changes were needed in either file; only the internal derivation was swapped. Diff: `AdminUsersTab.jsx` +22/-5 lines, `UserDetailsModal.jsx` +27/-8 lines — narrowly scoped, no unrelated lines touched (verified via `git diff --stat` before considering Phase 1 complete).

### D — One source of truth: ADVANCED, not fully unified this phase

Admin (`AdminUsersTab.jsx`, `UserDetailsModal.jsx`) now consumes the same resolver Dashboard's `computeEffectivePlan()` already fed into. `SettingsTab.jsx` was checked fresh this task and found **already correct** — it receives `effectivePlan`/`isTrialExpired`/`trialDaysLeft` as props from `Dashboard.jsx` (a prior, already-documented fix), never deriving its own plan logic; its one remaining minor inconsistency (`bizPlan !== 'free'` — raw plan, not resolved tier — gates the "Cancel Subscription" button's visibility) is a low-priority, purely cosmetic edge case (an expired-trial-not-yet-self-cancelled user might see a redundant Cancel button) explicitly **not fixed this task** — out of the named bug's scope, flagged for a future pass, not silently left undocumented. No broad UI redesign was performed anywhere, per instruction.

### E — Approved product rules: PRESERVED, all verified via the new test suite

FREE=5/mo, BASIC=20/mo+real-sellable, PRO=full entitlement, active-trial=full PRO-equivalent access, expired-trial-without-purchase=FREE, the exact HE/EN expired-trial message (unchanged, untouched by this task — lives in `Dashboard.jsx`, not in the files this task edited) — all confirmed intact by dedicated tests (below) and by the fact that `computeEffectivePlan()`'s own internals were never modified.

The catalog (`badge.icon`/`colorToken`/`bgTint` per plan) is built and ready to drive Dashboard/Admin/Settings/Billing badges consistently. **Deliberately not wired into `AdminUsersTab.jsx`'s existing icon ternary tonight** (`isGrantedLifetimePro ? <Crown/> : planValue==='pro' ? <Gem/> : ...`) — doing so would require a small icon-name-to-component lookup map and touches two render sites (desktop + mobile), which was judged as expanding tonight's diff beyond the narrowly-scoped bug-fix-plus-foundation this phase authorized, even though the visual output would be byte-identical. **Documented as the exact next micro-step**: replace the hardcoded ternary with `PLAN_CATALOG[tier].badge` lookups in both `AdminUsersTab.jsx` render sites — zero visual change, mechanical, low-risk, ready whenever authorized. The customer Dashboard has **no plan/tier badge at all today** (confirmed in §94.1) — that remains a genuinely new UI addition for a future phase, not a wiring task.

This is accurate for **today's schema reality** (confirmed via `planEntitlements.js`'s own documented audit: the only way a non-free plan gets a null `trial_ends_at` today is the admin's own "Toggle Lifetime" action — there is no purchase flow at all). **However**, once real billing exists, an ordinary non-trial paid subscriber (BASIC or PRO, purchased directly or converted post-trial) will *also* have `trial_ends_at:null` — and this same rule would then mislabel a normal healthy paying customer as "Lifetime." This is a genuine, not-yet-relevant-today, future schema dependency: Lifetime needs its own explicit field (e.g. an `admin_override` boolean, already anticipated in the 4-axis subscription model at §92) once the `subscriptionStatus` axis is built. Not a defect to fix tonight — a forward pointer for that future phase.

### P0 Email Bug #1 — `send-trial-expiration-email`: FIXED

**Root cause, confirmed by fresh audit**: batch mode's plan filter (`if ((biz.plan||'free').toLowerCase() !== 'free') continue;`) required `plan==='free'` to consider a candidate — but per `planEntitlements.js`'s own documented model, an active-trial account **always** has `plan==='pro'` with a real, non-null `trial_ends_at`. The filter excluded its entire real target audience, unconditionally, on every run — a complete no-op for its intended purpose. Fixed to require `plan==='pro'` instead. The eligibility decision (email/role/plan/window/idempotency) was extracted into a new, dependency-free `eligibility.ts` (`resolveTrialReminderStage()`), consumed by both the Deno runtime (`index.ts`) and a new Vitest suite (`eligibility.test.js`, 14 cases — 3-day window, 24-hour window, outside-window exclusion, expired exclusion, genuine-FREE exclusion (the exact bug regression), active-trial inclusion (the exact fix regression), BASIC exclusion, Lifetime exclusion, super_admin exclusion, missing-email exclusion, both idempotency directions, stage-progression, timezone-safety). Duplicate-send protection (`trial_reminder_3d_sent`/`24h_sent`, set only after a successful send) and HE/EN sender/template selection (`country`-based, matching the established convention) were both confirmed already correct — untouched.

### P0 Email Bug #2 — `send-subscription-expiration-email`: SAFELY DISABLED (fail-safe), per the Owner's explicit product rule

**Root cause, confirmed against the live-captured schema** (`supabase/migrations/20260830000000_capture_base_schema_tables.sql`, itself captured from Production introspection): `business_settings` has no `subscription_ends_at`, `subscription_reminder_3d_sent`, or `subscription_reminder_24h_sent` columns — confirmed via full-table grep, no writer anywhere in the repo populates them. Every batch invocation would throw at the `.select(...)` step and 400. **No schema migration authorized tonight; no columns invented.** Per the Owner's explicit rule (healthy auto-renewing subscriptions must never get a routine "ending soon" email — that only makes sense for a genuine known-end lifecycle state like an approved cancel-at-period-end, which the schema doesn't yet support), batch mode now short-circuits to an explicit, tested safe response (`buildSubscriptionEmailSafeResponse()` in a new dependency-free `safeResponse.ts`, tested by `safeResponse.test.js`, 3 cases) — no query against the missing columns, no send attempt, no throw. A Hebrew "iron rule" comment at the call site documents the exact schema dependency for the future phase: a real `subscriptionStatus`/`subscription_period_end` axis (§92's 4-axis model), written only when a subscription genuinely enters a non-renewing state — never on ordinary monthly renewal.

No Production invocation, no real-customer email, no scheduler/cron invocation attempted against either function tonight. Both fixes were verified entirely via Vitest (deterministic, no network) plus static code/schema audit — no live Resend call was made.

## §101. Dashboard-Centered Remaining Work Bundle — Semantic Reconstruction + Commit + TEST QA (2026-08-31)

**Owner-authorized, no push.** Completed the semantic reconstruction of `Dashboard.jsx` and its 7 dependent files, made an evidence-based grouping decision, created one atomic commit, verified intermediate-head safety, ran representative TEST Browser QA with real interaction (not just rendering checks), and performed a read-only pre-push production-dependency audit.

### Semantic reconstruction — why one atomic commit, not several

Read `Dashboard.jsx`'s full 814-line diff line-by-line (not by grep/guess) and traced every prop into its actual consumer. Confirmed by direct evidence, not inference: `Dashboard.jsx` computes `effectivePlan`/`isTrialExpired`/`trialDaysLeft` once (via the already-committed `computeEffectivePlan()`) and passes them as `QuoteForm`'s `userPlan` prop and `SettingsTab`'s `effectivePlan` prop; it holds `warranty`/`defaultWarranty` state and passes `warranty`/`setWarranty` to `QuoteForm` and `defaultWarranty`/`setDefaultWarranty` to `SettingsTab`, snapshotting `warranty` into every quote INSERT/UPDATE payload that `PublicQuote.jsx`/`PublicQuoteEn.jsx` later render; it passes `editingQuoteNumber` to `QuoteForm`. Each of these is a genuine runtime dependency confirmed by reading the actual wiring (not assumed) — committing any one file without its counterpart(s) would leave a real gap (e.g. `SettingsTab.jsx`'s Warranty textarea would call an `undefined` `setDefaultWarranty` and crash on input). A further, independent finding: `useSignaturePad.js`'s new default-off `isActive` gate is consumed identically by `PublicQuote.jsx` and `PublicQuoteEn.jsx` (confirmed HE/EN parity by direct diff comparison) — but the version of those two files *already on `origin/main`* (via `ffc741d`) calls the hook without ever activating it, so `useSignaturePad.js` cannot be committed without its two consumers either. Given every thread genuinely required cross-file coordination for runtime correctness at intermediate commits — not merely for tidiness — all 8 files (`Dashboard.jsx`, `QuoteForm.jsx`, `QuotesTab.jsx`, `QuotesTab.test.jsx`, `SettingsTab.jsx`, `PublicQuote.jsx`, `PublicQuoteEn.jsx`, `useSignaturePad.js`) were committed together as one atomic unit: **`fde680b`**.

**New findings from the reconstruction, not previously documented by name**: "Item 27" (Attn/recipient fallback to the client's own name when the explicit Attn field is left blank/whitespace-only) — self-contained within `handleSaveQuote`, already TEST-verified per pre-existing TEST quotes found in the account named for exactly this scenario ("Item27 Explicit/WS/Empty"). A small, unlabeled `storage_path: filePath` addition to the file-upload insert — `filePath` is an existing local variable, a safe completeness addition, not a separate thread. The comment describing Trial Notice being "passed as props to QuotesTab" describes a now-superseded intermediate design (the "Slider Location Correction" task) that a later task ("Trial Bar Zero-Layout-Shift Fix") reverted back into `Dashboard.jsx`'s own render — confirmed by grep: `QuotesTab.jsx` has zero trial-notice references today.

### Intermediate-head safety

Verified immediately before AND after the commit: full suite 162/162 PASS, lint 0 errors, build PASS (working directory). A dedicated clean committed-tree proof (`git archive HEAD` = `fde680b`, isolated, no working-tree access) additionally confirmed: build PASS, 141/141 tests PASS — and confirms `entry-server.jsx` is not required by the committed tree either.

EN signature-pad parity confirmed via direct source comparison (identical wiring to the HE file, both already read in full during the semantic reconstruction) rather than a second live draw-test, given the mechanism is already proven and file-identical.

### Pre-push dependency audit (read-only, no push performed)

18 commits total ahead of `origin/main`. Per-dependency assessment:

- **attn_name/attn_role**: code referencing these columns is **already on `origin/main`** via the pre-existing `ffc741d` — not new exposure. `fde680b`'s Item 27 addition builds on the same already-referenced fields, adding no new column reference. **GIT-SAFE ≠ PRODUCTION-SAFE, concretely**: the full 18-commit chain is GIT-SAFE (builds and tests cleanly, confirmed twice via isolated clean-tree proofs). It is **not** uniformly PRODUCTION-SAFE to push as one unit — Warranty specifically requires its migration applied to Production first, or must be held back / feature-gated separately from the rest of the chain.

## §102. Production Pre-Push Preflight — Read-Only Schema + Runtime Reconciliation (2026-08-31)

**Strictly read-only.** Target verified: Production is `ixabnzhjeqevtbhdfswv` (name `quotecode`, created 2026-07-27, `linked: true`), distinct from `ljfizgrdyzxddswcedwr` (`quotecode-test`, created 2026-08-27, `linked: false`) — confirmed via `supabase projects list` before any query. Every query that followed was scoped with `--linked` (Production only) or `--project-ref ixabnzhjeqevtbhdfswv` explicitly. Method: a `supabase db dump --schema public` (schema-only, confirmed zero `INSERT`/`COPY` rows — no data, no PII) plus targeted `information_schema`/aggregate `SELECT` queries via `supabase db query --linked`. No INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE/UPSERT/DDL/RPC/Edge-Function-invocation was executed at any point.

### Warranty — MISSING, confirmed via two independent methods

`business_settings.default_warranty`: **MISSING**. `quotes.warranty`: **MISSING**. Cross-verified via both the schema dump and a direct `information_schema.columns` query (which also returned `business_settings.default_terms` correctly, confirming the query itself works). **`6430cf5` STATUS: REQUIRED** — not applied, not superseded, not partially present.

**Severity correction, sharper than previously stated**: `fde680b`'s `Dashboard.jsx` includes `warranty: warranty` unconditionally in every quote INSERT/UPDATE payload and `default_warranty: defaultWarranty` unconditionally in every Business Settings save payload — **neither has the fail-safe retry pattern already used for Attn** (see below). If pushed today, this would not merely leave Warranty non-functional — it would make **every quote save and every Business Settings save fail** against Production's live schema (a PostgREST "column does not exist" error on each attempt), a P0-severity regression to core functionality, not a narrow feature gap.

### Attn — MISSING, but already compatible by design

`quotes.attn_name`: **MISSING**. `quotes.attn_role`: **MISSING**. However, `origin/main`'s already-live `Dashboard.jsx` (from `ffc741d`) has a **deliberate fail-safe retry**: `handleSaveQuote` first attempts the insert/update including `attn_name`/`attn_role`, and — only if the returned error message specifically mentions `attn_name` or `attn_role` (exact string match, not swallowing unrelated errors) — retries the identical operation without those two fields. **ORIGIN/MAIN COMPATIBILITY: PASS.** This was a deliberate, already-documented design decision (item 18's own in-code comment), not an oversight — Production has been running compatibly with the attn-less schema since `ffc741d` was pushed. `fde680b`'s new Item 27 (Attn fallback-to-client-name) builds on the same already-protected code path — no new incompatibility introduced.

### Quote Numbering — A90 mechanism, confirmed unchanged from the 2026-08-28 audit

`quotes.quote_number`: `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT nextval('quotes_quote_number_seq')` — a single **global** Postgres sequence (not per-business, not any custom trigger/function/RPC/Edge-Function/application-side allocator). Confirmed via both the schema dump and a direct `information_schema` query. No trigger touches `quote_number` itself (the two existing `quotes` triggers — `guard_quote_immutability_update`/`_delete_trigger` — guard content immutability post-approval, confirmed unrelated by name and by their already-documented function bodies). No `allocate_quote_number` function or `business_quote_sequences` table exists in Production. **WHO CURRENTLY GENERATES A90: Postgres's own native column-default sequence mechanism — nothing in this repository's application code, nothing custom.** This is unchanged from the original 2026-08-28 finding, re-confirmed live 3 days later — not stale.

**Uniqueness/collision check (sanitized aggregate only, no row content)**: `total_quotes=23`, `min_qn=11`, `max_qn=89`, `distinct_businesses=7`, `distinct_quote_numbers=23` — **zero duplicate `quote_number` values exist today**, and all 23 rows share one continuous global range across 7 different businesses (confirms "globally unique, not business-scoped" exactly as previously documented). **Collision risk for Item 17's per-business unique index: LOW** — global uniqueness today trivially implies per-business uniqueness, matching that migration's own safety claim, now independently re-verified with live numbers. (Minor, immaterial note: the original audit's "A90" observation vs. today's confirmed max of 89 — a one-off discrepancy not worth chasing; does not change the finding.)

| `..._add_quote_number_sequence.sql` (table+RPC) | `quotes` table exists | Yes | **READY** — additive, no conflict |

### Email / scheduler Production preflight — a long-standing "unknown" now resolved

**A real, already-configured, already-`origin/main`-committed Vercel Cron exists**: `vercel.json`'s `crons` entry (`/api/cron`, daily `0 8 * * *` UTC) → `api/cron.js` → `triggerExpirationReminders()`, which calls `supabase.functions.invoke('send-trial-expiration-email', { body: { mode: 'batch' } })` **and** `send-subscription-expiration-email` the same way, both guarded by `CRON_SECRET`. Confirmed via `git log origin/main -- api/cron.js` and a diff against local HEAD (zero difference — this file is untouched by every task in this whole engagement, already live). **TRIAL EMAIL LIVE INVOCATION: YES. SUBSCRIPTION EMAIL LIVE INVOCATION: YES.** Separately confirmed Production has no `pg_cron` extension installed (`SELECT extname FROM pg_extension WHERE extname LIKE '%cron%'` returned zero rows) — the invocation path is Vercel-native, not DB-native. **This corrects several prior sessions' documented "no scheduler could be confirmed or ruled out" — it should have read this one file.**

**Practical consequence, not yet acted on**: this cron has been invoking the *currently-deployed* (unfixed) Edge Functions daily since it was set up — meaning the original trial-email plan-filter bug has likely been silently sending zero reminders every day, and the subscription-email missing-column bug has likely been throwing (caught, non-fatal to the cron) every day. `99c4ba3`'s fixes are git-committed but **Edge Function commits do not auto-deploy** (confirmed established pattern) — so this cron's live behavior is unaffected by anything in this local commit chain until someone *separately* runs `supabase functions deploy`. That is a distinct, future authorization point the Owner should be aware of: the moment the trial-email fix is deployed, the very next 08:00 UTC run will attempt real customer email sends to genuinely-eligible active-trial accounts — not hypothetical.

`PROFLOW_ARCHITECTURE.md`'s existing, Owner-verified statement (git push to `main` → automatic Vercel Production frontend deployment, confirmed directly in the Vercel dashboard) remains accurate and was not contradicted by anything in this investigation. **DB migrations and Edge Function deployments remain on their own separate, manual paths** (`supabase db push`, `supabase functions deploy`) — a push does not touch either.

## §103. Warranty Production Release Preflight + Vercel Canonical Redirect Verification (2026-08-31)

**Strictly read-only.** No migration executed, no push, no deploy, no Production mutation, no email sent.

### Warranty migration (`6430cf5`) — full source review

`supabase/migrations/20260830000004_add_warranty_fields.sql`, read in full: exactly two `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS ... text` statements (`business_settings.default_warranty`, `quotes.warranty`, both nullable, no default, no constraints, no index, no new trigger), two column-scoped `GRANT INSERT/UPDATE (default_warranty) ON business_settings TO authenticated` (matching the table's own already-established column-level least-privilege convention — `quotes` needs no new grant since it already has a table-wide grant), and two pure-metadata `COMMENT ON COLUMN` statements. A commented-out (non-executing) rollback block is included for reference only.

**MIGRATION MINIMALITY: MINIMAL.** No unrelated statements found — every line traces directly to the two required columns.

**Read-only Production compatibility checks performed**: `information_schema.columns` confirms zero existing column anywhere matching `%warranty%` (no naming collision); `pg_views` confirms zero views depend on `business_settings` or `quotes` (no dependent-view risk); `business_settings` currently holds 12 rows, `quotes` 23 (both sanitized counts, no PII) — trivially small, and irrelevant to risk regardless, since `ADD COLUMN ... text` with no default is a Postgres 11+ metadata-only operation (no table rewrite, no full-table lock) on any table size. Immutability is inherited automatically: the existing `guard_quote_immutability()` trigger already compares whole rows (`NEW` vs `OLD` via a `public.quotes`-typed variable), so the new `warranty` column is covered with zero trigger changes — the same pattern already proven for every other content column added since that trigger was written.

**Existing data rewrite: NO.** **Idempotent: YES** (`IF NOT EXISTS` on both `ADD COLUMN`; re-granting an already-granted privilege is a no-op in Postgres). **Lock/runtime risk: LOW.** **Failure risk: near-zero** — the only plausible failure modes (insufficient privilege, missing `authenticated` role) don't apply to a Supabase-run migration.

### Failure/rollback analysis

| Scenario | Production effect | App remains functional? | Recovery |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. Migration fails before any statement applies | No change at all | Yes, unaffected | Re-run; nothing to undo |
| B. One statement applies, a later one fails | At most: columns added, grants not yet applied (or vice versa) — Postgres DDL in one `db push` batch runs as a transaction, so a mid-batch failure normally rolls back everything | Yes — old code doesn't reference these columns yet | Re-run the migration; `IF NOT EXISTS` makes retry safe |
| C. Migration succeeds, application push delayed | Columns exist, unused by any live code (old frontend doesn't reference them) | Yes, fully | None needed — this is actually the safest intermediate state |
| D. Migration succeeds, later application push fails | Same as C | Yes, fully | Retry the push; no DB state to unwind |
| E. Application push succeeds, Warranty UI has a runtime defect | Only the Warranty feature itself is affected (isolated code path); every other already-verified feature (entitlement, signature pad, quote-number sort, Attn) is independent and unaffected | Yes, for everything except Warranty specifically | Forward-fix the defect in a follow-up commit; no rollback of schema needed, since the columns being present and merely unused-by-a-buggy-UI is harmless |

No destructive rollback SQL is needed for any realistic scenario — every path is forward-safe. The migration's own commented-out `DROP COLUMN` rollback exists only for a hypothetical full-reversal decision, not because failure is expected to require it.

### Recommended order of operations

The Owner's proposed 8-step model is **confirmed as the recommended order**, with one clarification: step 4 ("read-only smoke of existing Production app BEFORE code push") should happen both immediately after migration (to confirm the app is unaffected by the new-but-unused columns) and is inherently safe since old code never references them. No safer alternative ordering was found — schema-then-code is the only order that avoids any intermediate broken state (code-then-schema would briefly make old code try to reference not-yet-existing columns, which the Attn precedent shows is handled by explicit fail-safe retries, but Warranty's code has no such retry, so schema-first is not merely preferred here, it is required).

### Production smoke-test checklist (prepared, not executed)

**Pre-push, after migration**: landing page loads; login works; existing Dashboard loads; existing Quote History loads — all read-only, no mutation unless separately authorized.
**Post-push**: Dashboard loads; Settings loads; quote creation/save using a TEST/authorized account only; default Warranty save; new quote Warranty snapshot; historical quote's warranty remains unchanged after a default-value edit; HE Public Quote Warranty section renders; EN Public Quote Warranty section renders; Trial/Expired/BASIC/PRO plan behavior spot-checked; signature pad tap-to-activate/draw/clear/deactivate; Admin table renders correctly; one representative mobile + one desktop check per market.

### Vercel canonical redirect — CONFIRMED GAP, root path only

**HTTP-level (curl, read-only)**: `quotecode.vercel.app/` → **200 OK**, real cached app content (`Content-Length: 3615`, `X-Vercel-Cache: HIT`, `Set-Cookie: proflow_geo_country=IL`) — **not a redirect**, confirmed not a stale-cache artifact (identical result with a cache-busting query string). `quotecode.vercel.app/dashboard`, `/dashboard?lang=he`, `/en`, `/he` → all correctly **308 Permanent Redirect** to the matching `www.quotecodepro.com` path, with query string preserved.

**Browser-level (browser-harness, read-only, no login)**: navigating to `https://quotecode.vercel.app/` leaves the browser on `quotecode.vercel.app` (title renders the real Hebrew landing page) — confirms the HTTP finding is genuinely user-visible, not a curl-only artifact. Navigating to `https://quotecode.vercel.app/en` correctly ends on `https://www.quotecodepro.com/en`.

**Exact root cause found**: `middleware.ts` (project root) has `export const config = { matcher: ['/'] }` — a root-only Vercel Edge Middleware that sets a geolocation cookie (`proflow_geo_country`, matching exactly what the curl response showed) and always calls `next()`, by its own explicit in-file design ("לעולם אינו מבצע redirect" — never performs a redirect). This middleware is **not host-aware** — it runs identically on every host including `quotecode.vercel.app`. Vercel's Edge Middleware executes independently of `vercel.json`'s `redirects` array for the exact root path, so the host-conditional redirect rule (`"source": "/:path*"`, `has: [{type:"host", value:"quotecode.vercel.app"}]`) never gets the chance to apply to `/` specifically — every other path (no root-scoped middleware intercepting it) reaches the redirect rule normally and works correctly. Confirmed this `vercel.json`/`middleware.ts` pair is unmodified and already on `origin/main` (`git diff origin/main HEAD` — zero difference) — a pre-existing, already-live gap, untouched by and unrelated to this session's 18-commit chain.

**REDIRECT IMPLEMENTATION**: `vercel.json`'s `redirects[0]` (host-conditional, `permanent: true`) — correct in isolation; the gap is the interaction with `middleware.ts`'s root-only, non-host-aware matcher, not a defect in the redirect rule itself.

**CANONICAL DOMAIN PRE-LIVE GATE: RED** — the "no independent app session on `vercel.app`" criterion is directly violated for the literal root path. Not GREEN.

### Release interaction check — confirmed independent

`6430cf5` touches exactly two files (`supabase/migrations/20260830000004_add_warranty_fields.sql`, `supabase/functions/get-public-quote/index.ts`) — zero overlap with Item 17's migrations (different tables/columns entirely), the Attn fallback code, Admin V2 Phase 1, the Trial/Plan resolver, the email Edge Functions, or `vercel.json`/`middleware.ts`. **Confirmed independent, not assumed.**

### Final decision package

**WARRANTY MIGRATION READY FOR OWNER AUTHORIZATION: YES.** Minimal, additive, idempotent, no data rewrite, low lock risk, zero dependency conflicts, fully forward-safe on every failure path. If authorized, the exact next action would be applying this one migration file to Production via `supabase db push` (a distinct DB-mutation authorization, not covered by this read-only task) — followed by the already-documented smoke checklist before any application push.

**VERCEL CANONICAL REDIRECT: RED.** A real, confirmed, already-live gap exists (root path only) — independent of the Warranty decision and not blocking it, but should be fixed in its own small, separate, low-risk commit (adding an explicit `/` entry to `vercel.json`'s redirect `source`, or making `middleware.ts`'s matcher host-aware) before this repo's canonical-domain migration is considered fully complete.
